- **Evidenza:** column.service.ts:58-65 `prisma.$transaction(items.map((item) => prisma.kanbanColumn.update({ where: { id: item.id }, data: { position: item.position } })))` — no `boardId` in the where clause and no ownership pre-check. routes/kanban.ts:428-434 only does `assertBoardAccess(id, request.user.id, 'WRITE')` on the URL board, and reorderColumnsSchema (routes/kanban.ts:37-39) validates the ids as plain strings.
- **Impatto utente:** Any authenticated user has WRITE on at least one board (their own). Given a board shared with them READ-only, they can read its column IDs from GET /boards/:id, then POST those IDs to `PUT /boards/<their own board>/columns/reorder` and scramble the victim board's column order — a READ→WRITE escalation across board boundaries.
- **Fix:** Scope the writes to the authorised board: replace the per-id updates with `prisma.kanbanColumn.updateMany({ where: { id: item.id, boardId }, data: { position: item.position } })`, so ids from another board match nothing. (If finding #2 is fixed by adding `position` to updateColumn, delete this endpoint instead — updateColumn already goes through getColumnWithAccess.)
- **Correzione del verificatore:** Confirmed as a cross-board write with only READ on the target, and the `updateMany({ where: { id, boardId } })` fix is right. Scope it honestly though: the writable surface is a single Int (`position`) on columns whose UUIDs the attacker already had to obtain from a board shared with them, and the endpoint currently has no frontend caller at all (finding #2), so the practical damage is a scrambled column order the victim can see and (once #2 is fixed) undo. Permission hole, but not an escalation that reads or destroys anything.

#### `medium` · bug · S — getBoard writes on every read and silently undoes the user's 'remove completed' on a column
`backend/src/services/kanban/board.service.ts:147` · anche: `frontend/src/features/kanban/components/KanbanColumn.tsx`, `backend/src/services/kanban/card.service.ts`

- **Problema:** A GET of a board re-flags the last column as isCompleted whenever no column carries the flag. There is no way to express 'this board has no completion column' — the state is restored on the next load, and the restored flag re-arms the 7-day auto-archive.
- **Evidenza:** board.service.ts:146-162: on every getBoard, `if (columns.length > 0 && !columns.some(c => c.isCompleted))` → `prisma.kanbanColumn.update({ ..., data: { isCompleted: true } })`. The UI explicitly offers the inverse action: KanbanColumn.tsx:203-205 renders `kanban.column.removeCompleted` → KanbanBoardPage.tsx:209-211 → updateColumn with `isCompleted: false`, which column.service.ts:39 does persist. board.service.ts:144 then runs archiveCompletedCards on that same completed column (card.service.ts:374-394, ARCHIVE_AFTER_DAYS = 7). The route only requires READ (routes/kanban.ts:143-147), and syncPull fetches EVERY board through this endpoint on each cycle (syncService.ts:341-344).
- **Impatto utente:** A user turns off 'completed' on their Done column (e.g. to stop auto-archiving). The very next board load turns it back on with no notification, and a week later the cards in that column start disappearing into the archive again. Separately, a read-only collaborator's page load mutates the owner's board, and every sync cycle issues a findMany + potential update per board on a read path.
- **Fix:** Move the defaulting to where the state is created rather than where it is read: createBoard and createBoardFromTaskList already set isCompleted on the Done column (board.service.ts:130, 326). Delete the block at board.service.ts:146-162 (keep a one-off backfill migration if legacy boards need it), so an explicitly unflagged board stays unflagged.
- **Correzione del verificatore:** Correct, and the revert is faster than 'the very next board load': useKanbanMutations.ts:53-60 invalidates the board query immediately after the toggle, so the refetch that repaints the menu already carries isCompleted back to true — the user sees the switch flip itself back within the same interaction. Also note the re-flag targets the last column by position, which need not be the one the user just unflagged.

#### `low` · bug · S — createBoardFromTaskList's local splitText writes card descriptions the API's own Zod cap rejects
`backend/src/services/kanban/board.service.ts:308` · anche: `frontend/src/features/kanban/kanbanService.ts`, `backend/src/routes/kanban.ts`, `frontend/src/features/kanban/components/CardDetailModal.tsx`

- **Problema:** The task-list conversion has its own splitText that puts the FULL, unclamped item text into the card description, while TaskItem.text has no length limit and the card description cap is 5000. It also truncates titles at 100 chars while the frontend mirror truncates at 500.
- **Evidenza:** board.service.ts:308-313 `const splitText = (text) => { if (text.length > 100) return { title: text.substring(0,100)+'...', description: text }; ... }` — `description: text` with no slice. backend/src/routes/tasklists.ts:17 `text: z.string().min(1)` — no max. routes/kanban.ts:47 `description: z.string().max(5000).nullable().optional()`. The frontend mirror is explicitly documented as matching this function (kanbanService.ts:37-39) but clamps: `description: carriesMore ? text.slice(0, CARD_DESCRIPTION_MAX) : undefined` (kanbanService.ts:53) with CARD_TITLE_MAX = 500.
- **Impatto utente:** Converting a task list that contains a long item produces cards the API can no longer accept edits for. When the user later edits that card's description, CardDetailModal.tsx:124-129 sends the whole string (kanbanService.updateCard at :348-370 does not clamp, unlike createCard at :297), the server 400s, and syncPush marks the item permanently 'failed' — the edit is lost behind a sync-error banner that only a full retype under 5000 chars clears. Conversions also produce inconsistent titles: 100 chars from a task list vs 500 from a note.
- **Fix:** Delete the local splitText and apply the same caps the API enforces: `title = text.slice(0, 500)`, `description = text.slice(0, 5000)`. One clamp at board.service.ts:308 covers both card loops (:345 and :363).
- **Correzione del verificatore:** The inconsistency is real but the failure path is narrow. It needs a task item over 5000 characters, AND the user must actually change that card's description afterwards — CardDetailModal.tsx:127-129 only sends `description` when it differs from the stored value, and the textarea already carries maxLength={5000} (CardDetailModal.tsx:305), so the user can only shorten it (which usually lands under the cap anyway). The 100-vs-500 title difference loses no data — splitText keeps the full text in the description — it is a cosmetic inconsistency between the two conversion paths. Fix is still the right one-liner.

#### `low` · ux · S — updateBoard never broadcasts, so a board rename is invisible to everyone else on the board
`backend/src/services/kanban/board.service.ts:256` · anche: `backend/src/services/kanbanSSE.ts`, `backend/src/services/kanban/linking.service.ts`, `frontend/src/features/kanban/hooks/useKanbanRealtime.ts`

- **Problema:** Every other kanban mutation broadcasts an SSE event; the board title/description update is the only one that doesn't. The event type it needs already exists and is already handled client-side.
- **Evidenza:** board.service.ts:252-269 — updateBoard returns the Prisma result with no `broadcast()` call, and column.service.ts imports broadcast while board.service.ts does not (no import at board.service.ts:1-5). `{ type: 'board:updated'; boardId: string }` is declared at kanbanSSE.ts:41 and emitted from linking.service.ts:213, 235, 453, 475 (note/task-list link and unlink) — never from a title change. The client already reacts: useKanbanRealtime.ts:62 invalidates the board query for any non-presence event.
- **Impatto utente:** Two people are on the same shared board. One renames it; the other keeps seeing the old title indefinitely — until an unrelated card or column event happens to fire an invalidation, or they navigate away and back. The board chat and cards update live, so the stale header reads as a bug.
- **Fix:** One line at the end of updateBoard: `broadcast(boardId, { type: 'board:updated', boardId });` plus the import from '../kanbanSSE'. No client change needed.
- **Correzione del verificatore:** The missing broadcast is real and the one-line fix is right, but 'indefinitely' is wrong. queryClient defaults are staleTime 5min with react-query v5's refetchOnWindowFocus/onReconnect left on (frontend/src/lib/queryClient.ts:3-10), so the other viewer picks the new title up on the next tab refocus, reconnect, or any other kanban SSE event. The stale header only persists while they sit continuously in the tab.

#### `low` · bug · S — deleteBoard leaves cover and avatar files on disk forever — no unlink, no reaper
`backend/src/services/kanban/board.service.ts:272` · anche: `backend/src/routes/kanban.ts`, `backend/src/scripts/pruneAttachments.ts`

- **Problema:** deleteBoard is a bare Prisma delete. The DB rows cascade, but the uploaded cover (≤5MB) and avatar (≤2MB) files are never unlinked, and no maintenance script cleans them.
- **Evidenza:** board.service.ts:271-273 `export async function deleteBoard(boardId: string) { return prisma.kanbanBoard.delete({ where: { id: boardId } }); }` — coverImage/avatarUrl are never read. The upload routes are careful about this everywhere else: routes/kanban.ts:197-207 and :222-238 both `fs.unlinkSync` the previous file. backend/src/scripts/pruneAttachments.ts:16-21 iterates `prisma.attachment.findMany` only — it has no notion of uploads/kanban or uploads/kanban/avatars (grep for 'kanban' in backend/src/scripts returns nothing). MAX_COVER_SIZE = 5MB, MAX_AVATAR_SIZE = 2MB (routes/kanban.ts:79-80).
- **Impatto utente:** Every deleted board permanently leaks up to 7MB in uploads/kanban. On a single-server IIS deployment with no reaper, this grows without bound and is invisible until the disk fills; the ZIP backup (npm run backup) carries the dead files too.
- **Fix:** Read the two paths before deleting and unlink them, reusing the exact idiom already in the upload routes: select `{ coverImage, avatarUrl }`, `fs.unlinkSync(path.join(UPLOADS_DIR, url.replace(/^\/uploads\//, '')))` inside an existsSync guard, then delete the row.
- **Correzione del verificatore:** Confirmed as a leak with the right fix, but the impact is overstated: it is bounded by (boards deleted) × ≤7MB, and only boards that actually had a cover or avatar uploaded leak anything. 'Grows without bound until the disk fills' is a scenario, not a normal-use outcome — treat it as housekeeping debt (it does bloat `npm run backup`), not an operational risk.

#### `low` · bug · S — listBoards cardCount includes archived cards, disagreeing with the board view and with Dexie's own counter
`backend/src/services/kanban/board.service.ts:83` · anche: `frontend/src/features/kanban/components/BoardCard.tsx`, `frontend/src/features/sync/syncService.ts`

- **Problema:** The board list sums `col._count.cards` with no archivedAt filter, while the board detail, the frontend's local counter, and the shared-board sync path all count only live cards. One number, three sources of truth.
- **Evidenza:** board.service.ts:83 `cardCount: b.columns.reduce((sum, col) => sum + col._count.cards, 0)` and the identical line at :101 for shared boards — the `columns.select._count.cards` at :24-26 / :55-57 has no where clause. board.service.ts:170-171 excludes archived cards from the detail payload, and :190-195 reports archivedCardsCount as a separate field. frontend kanbanService.ts:332 and :406 increment/decrement the Dexie cardCount on create/delete only (archiving never decrements), and syncService.ts:437 recomputes it for shared boards from the non-archived cards arrays. BoardCard.tsx:162 renders `t('kanban.stats.cards', { count: board.cardCount })`.
- **Impatto utente:** A board card in the grid claims '32 cards'; opening it shows 12, because 20 were auto-archived after 7 days in the Done column. The same board can even show two different totals depending on whether the tile was rendered from the API list or from Dexie (owned vs shared boards take different code paths).
- **Fix:** Add the same filter the detail query uses: `_count: { select: { cards: { where: { archivedAt: null } } } }` at board.service.ts:25 and :56. While there, `shareCount` can come from `shares.length` — the `_count.shares` at :23/:54 duplicates a relation that is already fully selected.

#### `low` · race · M — createColumn's read-then-write max+1 races into duplicate positions with no DB constraint to catch it
`backend/src/services/kanban/column.service.ts:8` · anche: `backend/prisma/schema.prisma`, `backend/src/services/kanban/board.service.ts`, `frontend/src/features/kanban/components/KanbanColumn.tsx`

- **Problema:** Position is computed with an aggregate read followed by a separate create, outside any transaction, and KanbanColumn has no unique constraint on (boardId, position).
- **Evidenza:** column.service.ts:8-16 `const maxPos = await prisma.kanbanColumn.aggregate({ where: { boardId }, _max: { position: true } }); const position = (maxPos._max.position ?? -1) + 1; await prisma.kanbanColumn.create({ data: { ..., position } })`. schema.prisma:485-494 declares only `@@index([boardId])` — no `@@unique([boardId, position])`. Ordering everywhere assumes position is total: board.service.ts:168 `orderBy: { position: 'asc' }`, KanbanColumn.tsx:71 sorts by position alone with no tiebreaker.
- **Impatto utente:** Two collaborators (or two tabs, or two offline clients replaying their queues) add a column at the same time and both land on the same position. From then on the two clients render the columns in different orders, and because column reorder never persists (finding #2) the user cannot drag their way out of it.
- **Fix:** Make the DB the source of truth: add `@@unique([boardId, position])` in a migration and compute the position inside a transaction (or retry once on P2002). Cheaper stopgap that removes the visible symptom: make the ordering total by adding `{ id: 'asc' }` as a secondary orderBy in board.service.ts:168 and in the frontend sort.
- **Correzione del verificatore:** Right about the race and the missing constraint, wrong about one citation and understating the everyday cause. KanbanColumn.tsx:71 sorts CARDS, not columns; the position-only column sort with no tiebreaker is useBoardDnD.ts:303-306 (rendered via KanbanBoardPage.tsx:173-178). More to the point, duplicate positions do not need concurrency at all: the frontend computes its own position from Dexie (kanbanService.ts:183-189, maxPos+1) while the server recomputes independently (column.service.ts:8-12) and createColumnSchema (routes/kanban.ts:31-34) accepts only id+title — so an offline-created column already diverges from the server's value until the next pull. The cheap stopgap (secondary orderBy) addresses the visible symptom; the unique constraint would need a data backfill first, since existing boards may already hold duplicates.

### dnd-selection

#### `high` · bug · S — DndContext has no onDragCancel — Escape/tab-switch/resize freezes the board and leaves a phantom cross-column move
`frontend/src/features/kanban/KanbanBoardPage.tsx:901` · anche: `frontend/src/features/kanban/hooks/useBoardDnD.ts`

- **Problema:** The DndContext wires onDragStart/onDragOver/onDragEnd but not onDragCancel (KanbanBoardPage.tsx:901-906). activeCardId/activeColumnId are only cleared inside handleDragEnd (useBoardDnD.ts:188-189), and dnd-kit calls onDragEnd OR onDragCancel, never both. Any cancelled drag leaves both ids set forever.
- **Evidenza:** KanbanBoardPage.tsx:901-906 lists exactly `sensors, collisionDetection, onDragStart, onDragOver, onDragEnd` — no onDragCancel. node_modules/@dnd-kit/core/dist/core.cjs.development.js:3159 `const eventName = type === Action.DragEnd ? 'onDragEnd' : 'onDragCancel';` (mutually exclusive). AbstractPointerSensor binds handleCancel to Escape (line 1600-1604), `pointercancel` (1446), window `resize` (1449) and `visibilitychange` (1451). useBoardDnD.ts:50-54 gates the whole server->local resync on `!activeCardId && !activeColumnId`, and setActiveCardId(null) appears only at line 188 inside handleDragEnd.
- **Impatto utente:** User starts dragging a card from To Do to Done, drags over Done (handleDragOver:148-180 already moved it in localColumns), then presses Escape, alt-tabs, or the browser fires visibilitychange/resize. The card stays rendered in Done, no move request is ever sent, and localColumns is frozen: teammates' realtime changes, new cards and column renames stop appearing for the rest of the session (until the user completes another full drag). On reload the card is back in To Do — the move the user believes they made is silently gone.
- **Fix:** Add `handleDragCancel` in useBoardDnD (setActiveCardId(null); setActiveColumnId(null); setLocalColumns(board?.columns ?? [])) and pass it as `onDragCancel` on the DndContext. ~5 lines, no new state.
- **Correzione del verificatore:** Mechanism and freeze are exactly as described, but this is not data loss: nothing was ever written, the server state stays correct, and the next completed drag clears activeCardId and unfreezes the board. What the user gets is a lying, frozen board for the rest of the session (or until the next full drag) — a high-severity UX/correctness bug, not a critical one.

#### `high` · bug · M — Backend same-column reorder writes duplicate positions when a card is dragged DOWN
`backend/src/services/kanban/card.service.ts:202` · anche: `backend/src/services/kanban/__tests__/card.service.test.ts`, `frontend/src/features/kanban/hooks/useBoardDnD.ts`

- **Problema:** moveCard shifts `position >= newPosition` up by 1, sets the card to newPosition, then decrements everything `> card.position` (the ORIGINAL position). For a downward move inside the same column the second shift over-corrects the cards between old and new, producing two cards with the same position and a hole. Board reads use `orderBy: { position: 'asc' }` with no tie-breaker, so the resulting order is whatever Postgres returns.
- **Evidenza:** card.service.ts:204-231 (three updateMany steps). Simulated the exact algorithm on [A0,B1,C2,D3]: `move A -> 2` yields `B@0 A@2 C@2 D@3` (expected B0 C1 A2 D3); `move B -> 3` yields `A@0 C@1 B@3 D@3` (expected A0 C1 D2 B3). Upward moves are correct. board.service.ts:172 `orderBy: { position: 'asc' }` — no secondary sort. Every existing moveCard test (card.service.test.ts:449,481,526,556,582) passes position 0 and a different column, so the same-column path is completely untested.
- **Impatto utente:** User drags a card down two slots inside a column. The optimistic UI shows the new order; after the refetch the card can snap back above the card it was dropped under, and the column's positions are now corrupt (two cards share an index), so subsequent drags in that column also land unpredictably and every collaborator sees a different order.
- **Fix:** Split the same-column branch: if newPosition > card.position, `updateMany({ position: { gt: card.position, lte: newPosition } }, decrement 1)`; else `updateMany({ position: { gte: newPosition, lt: card.position } }, increment 1)`; then set the card to newPosition. Drop the generic 'shift >= newPosition' step for that branch. Add the two failing cases above as tests.

#### `high` · bug · S — Menu/bulk moves send `position: 999` as an insert index — batch order is reversed and later drops land in the wrong slot
`frontend/src/features/kanban/hooks/useBoardDnD.ts:295` · anche: `frontend/src/features/kanban/KanbanBoardPage.tsx`, `backend/src/services/kanban/card.service.ts`

- **Problema:** handleMoveCardToColumn optimistically appends the card at `col.cards.length` but persists `position: 999` (same literal in handleBulkMove, KanbanBoardPage.tsx:363). The backend treats position as an absolute index and shifts `>= 999` up, so each successive 999 pushes the previous one to 1000, 1001… The stored order is the reverse of what the UI showed, and the column now contains an outlier index that breaks the frontend's 0..n assumption.
- **Evidenza:** useBoardDnD.ts:285-296 (`newPosition = col.cards.length` optimistically, `position: 999` sent). KanbanBoardPage.tsx:360-365 same. card.service.ts:204-213 increments `position >= newPosition`. Simulation of three menu moves into [A0,B1]: result `A@0 B@1 Z@999 Y@1000 X@1001` while the UI appended X,Y,Z. Follow-up simulation on [A0,B1,C@999]: dragging A to the bottom (frontend sends index 2, useBoardDnD.ts:244) yields `B@0 A@2 C@999` → order B,A,C, not B,C,A.
- **Impatto utente:** Marquee-select 5 cards, bulk-move them to Done: the UI shows them appended in order, the refetch shows them reversed. After any menu/bulk move into a column, dragging a card to the very bottom of that column drops it second-to-last instead — repeatably, with no error.
- **Fix:** Send the real append index instead of 999: `position: targetColumn.cards.length` in handleMoveCardToColumn and in handleBulkMove (increment per card in the loop). Alternatively clamp server-side in moveCard: `newPosition = Math.min(newPosition, await tx.kanbanCard.count({ where: { columnId: toColumnId } }))` — one line that fixes every caller.

#### `high` · race · M — isMoveInFlight is released on the local Dexie write, not on the server push — every drop can snap back
`frontend/src/features/kanban/hooks/useBoardDnD.ts:47` · anche: `frontend/src/features/kanban/hooks/useKanbanMutations.ts`, `frontend/src/features/sync/syncService.ts`, `frontend/src/features/kanban/hooks/useKanbanBoard.ts`

- **Problema:** The guard that protects the optimistic localColumns is a boolean cleared in moveCard's onSettled. The mutationFn only writes Dexie + enqueues, so onSettled fires within milliseconds while the actual PUT is still queued; onSuccess simultaneously invalidates the board query, whose queryFn is a plain server GET. The refetch therefore returns pre-move data and the effect at :50-54 overwrites localColumns with it. Being a boolean rather than a counter, it is also cleared by the first of N concurrent moves.
- **Evidenza:** useBoardDnD.ts:47,51-54 (`!isMoveInFlight` gate), :207-211/:242-246/:259-263 (`onSettled: () => setIsMoveInFlight(false)`). useKanbanMutations.ts:106-113: mutationFn = kanbanService.moveCard (Dexie only, kanbanService.ts:372-385), onSuccess = `flushSync(); invalidateBoard();` where flushSync is fire-and-forget. useKanbanBoard.ts:10-11 queryFn calls getBoard → kanbanService.ts:129-132 `api.get('/kanban/boards/:id')`, server-only. syncService.ts:550-557: if a sync is already running, syncPush returns immediately and is re-scheduled with `setTimeout(..., 1000)` (:816-818), so the push can be a full second behind the refetch.
- **Impatto utente:** Drop a card in another column: it lands, then visibly jumps back to its old column for a few hundred ms to over a second, then jumps forward again when the SSE card:moved event triggers a second refetch. With a bulk move of N cards the first onSettled unblocks the effect while N-1 moves are still pending, so the whole batch flashes back to its original columns.
- **Fix:** Make moveCard's onSuccess `async () => { await syncPush(); invalidateBoard(); }` (react-query awaits onSuccess before onSettled, so the guard stays up until the server has the move and the refetch sees post-move data), and turn isMoveInFlight into a pending counter (`setPending(n => n+1/-1)`, gate on `pending === 0`).

#### `medium` · architecture · M — Bulk move fires two move requests per card (sync queue + direct silent PUT), defeating the silent/grouped-notification design
`frontend/src/features/kanban/KanbanBoardPage.tsx:357` · anche: `frontend/src/features/kanban/hooks/useKanbanMutations.ts`, `frontend/src/features/sync/syncService.ts`, `frontend/src/features/kanban/kanbanService.ts`

- **Problema:** handleBulkMove first calls dnd.handleMoveCardToColumn for every card — which runs mutations.moveCard, i.e. a Dexie write plus a syncQueue UPDATE that syncPush later replays against the NON-silent move endpoint — and then issues a second, explicit `?silent=true` PUT for the same card. Every bulk-moved card is moved twice on the server.
- **Evidenza:** KanbanBoardPage.tsx:357-359 loops handleMoveCardToColumn; :360-365 loops `api.put('/kanban/cards/{id}/move?silent=true')`. useBoardDnD.ts:293-297 → useKanbanMutations.ts:106-113 → kanbanService.ts:372-385 enqueues `{type:'UPDATE', entity:'KANBAN_CARD', data:{columnId, position}}`; syncService.ts:688-695 routes any KANBAN_CARD UPDATE carrying columnId to `PUT /kanban/cards/{id}/move` with no silent flag; card.service.ts:272-311 sends a per-card push/email notification when `!skipNotification` and the column changed.
- **Impatto utente:** A 30-card bulk move issues 60 move requests (v1.10.3 already hit a rate-limit spiral at this endpoint), re-shuffles positions twice, and — whenever the queued non-silent PUT wins the race — sends individual 'X moved card Y' push/email notifications to every board member in addition to the grouped bulk-move-notify the code was written to send instead.
- **Fix:** Drop the manual `api.put(...silent=true)` loop; move the silent flag into the move path itself: pass `silent` through kanbanService.moveCard into the queued payload and have syncService append `?silent=true` when present. Keep the single grouped bulk-move-notify call.
- **Correzione del verificatore:** The duplicate request and the double position shuffle are real, but the notification-spam impact is overstated. card.service.ts:241 gates the whole notify/activity/auto-assign block on `actorId && card.columnId !== toColumnId`; by the time the queued non-silent PUT lands the card is already in the target column, so that branch is skipped. And the silent PUT is issued first in practice — it runs in the same synchronous tick as handleBulkMove, while the queued one only goes out after the Dexie write resolves and onSuccess calls the fire-and-forget flushSync (useKanbanMutations.ts:16-18). Duplicate push/email therefore requires network reordering, not the normal case. What remains is 2N requests instead of N and a redundant second position write — structural waste, not a notification storm.

#### `medium` · ux · S — Bulk move and bulk archive swallow every error — partial failures are invisible and leave a lying UI
`frontend/src/features/kanban/KanbanBoardPage.tsx:364` · anche: `frontend/src/features/kanban/components/BulkArchiveDialog.tsx`, `frontend/src/lib/api.ts`

- **Problema:** handleBulkMove ends every request with `.catch(() => {})` and clears the selection unconditionally. BulkArchiveDialog's handlePreview/handleArchive use try/finally with no catch. There is no global axios error toast — the response interceptor only handles 401 — so a 403/429/500/offline failure produces literally no feedback.
- **Evidenza:** KanbanBoardPage.tsx:364 `}).catch(() => {});`, :368 same for bulk-move-notify, :370 `marquee.clearSelection()` runs regardless. BulkArchiveDialog.tsx:207-218 and 220-233: `try { await api.post(...) } finally { setIsArchiving(false) }` — no catch, and `onPreview(new Set())`/`onArchived()`/`handleClose()` sit inside the try so a rejection leaves the amber preview highlight on the cards forever. api.ts:28-68 rejects everything that is not a 401 without notifying the user. bulk-archive is owner-only (kanban.ts:592-595) so a WRITE collaborator gets a silent 403.
- **Impatto utente:** A shared-board collaborator bulk-moves 10 cards; 3 are rejected (403/429). The cards stay visually in the target column, the selection clears, nothing is reported — the user only discovers it on reload. Same for bulk archive: clicking Archive appears to do nothing, cards keep pulsing amber, dialog stays open.
- **Fix:** Replace the empty catches with `Promise.allSettled` over the move calls and `toast.error(t('common.genericError'))` when any settle rejected (and only clearSelection/refetch after). Wrap the two BulkArchiveDialog calls in try/catch with the same toast. ~10 lines, keys already exist.
- **Correzione del verificatore:** Two parts of the evidence do not hold. (1) The BulkArchiveDialog line numbers are wrong — the file is 164 lines; handlePreview is at :50-61 and handleArchive at :63-76, not :207-233. (2) The '403 to a WRITE collaborator' example is refuted twice over: bulk archive is owner-gated in the UI (KanbanBoardPage.tsx:926 and :1140-1141 render the trigger and the dialog only when isOwner), and marquee selection — the only entry point to bulk move — is gated on `enabled: !readOnly` (KanbanBoardPage.tsx:134-137), so a READ collaborator can never reach it and a WRITE one is authorized. Also, bulk-move failures are NOT fully silent: the parallel syncQueue move (finding #4) survives, retries with backoff, and is marked failed and surfaced by SyncStatusIndicator (syncService.ts:795-812). The genuine silent-failure surface is therefore bulk ARCHIVE (owner, 500/offline/network: no toast, dialog stuck open, amber ring stuck) plus the cosmetic loss of the grouped notification.

#### `medium` · bug · S — Marquee selection gets stuck when the mouse button is released outside the browser window
`frontend/src/features/kanban/hooks/useMarqueeSelection.ts:127`

- **Problema:** The drag lifecycle is mousedown on the container + mousemove/mouseup on document, with no pointer capture, no `pointercancel`/`blur` handler and no `e.buttons` check. A mouseup delivered outside the page (browser chrome, another window, off-screen) never reaches the document listener, so isDrawingRef stays true and the rectangle keeps following the cursor with no button held.
- **Evidenza:** useMarqueeSelection.ts:174-182 registers only mousedown/mousemove/mouseup; :128 `if (!isDrawingRef.current) return;` is the only guard in handleMouseMove; isDrawingRef is cleared exclusively in handleMouseUp (:153) and clearSelection (:97). :116 preventDefault on mousedown also disables the browser's native selection fallback.
- **Impatto utente:** User drags a marquee upward past the top of the viewport (a natural gesture when selecting the top cards of a column) and releases over the tab bar. Coming back into the page, the blue rectangle keeps rubber-banding and cards keep selecting/deselecting on plain mouse movement until they click again.
- **Fix:** One line at the top of handleMouseMove: `if (e.buttons === 0) { handleMouseUp(e); return; }` (hoist handleMouseUp above it). Optionally also `window.addEventListener('blur', clearSelection)`.

#### `medium` · mobile · S — Mobile column swipe has no axis lock and no touchcancel — vertical scrolling drags the carousel sideways
`frontend/src/features/kanban/hooks/useBoardMobileSwipe.ts:51` · anche: `frontend/src/features/kanban/KanbanBoardPage.tsx`

- **Problema:** handleTouchStart records only clientX and handleTouchMove translates the carousel by dx on every move, ignoring dy. The handlers sit on the carousel wrapper while each column is `overflow-y-auto` inside it, so every vertical scroll gesture also bubbles up and slides the board horizontally; if the accumulated |dx| exceeds 50 at touchend the board even switches column. There is no onTouchCancel, so an interrupted gesture leaves swipeOffset frozen mid-slide.
- **Evidenza:** useBoardMobileSwipe.ts:45-49 (`touchStartRef.current = e.touches[0].clientX` only), :51-61 (offset from dx with no dy comparison), :63-76 (commit on |dx|>50). KanbanBoardPage.tsx:852-856 binds the three handlers on the `flex-1 overflow-hidden` wrapper; :868 each column pane is `overflow-y-auto`, so touch events from the scrolling list bubble to it. No `onTouchCancel` anywhere in either file.
- **Impatto utente:** On a phone, scrolling down a long column makes the whole board wobble horizontally, and a slightly diagonal flick jumps to the next column mid-scroll, losing the user's place. An interrupted touch (incoming call, system gesture) leaves the board visibly offset until the next touch.
- **Fix:** Store startY in handleTouchStart; on the first move set a ref `axis = Math.abs(dy) > Math.abs(dx) ? 'y' : 'x'` and return early for every subsequent move when axis === 'y'. Add `onTouchCancel={swipe.handleTouchEnd}` on the wrapper.
- **Correzione del verificatore:** The wobble and the frozen-offset-after-touchcancel are exactly right. 'A slightly diagonal flick jumps to the next column' is the overstated part: the commit threshold is |dx| > 50px, so a near-vertical scroll produces visible sideways wobble but does not switch columns — that needs a genuinely diagonal gesture. Severity stays medium on the wobble plus the missing touchcancel.

#### `medium` · ux · S — Any active filter silently turns the whole board read-only (no drag, no add card, no column menu) with zero explanation
`frontend/src/features/kanban/KanbanBoardPage.tsx:920` · anche: `frontend/src/features/kanban/components/KanbanColumn.tsx`, `frontend/src/features/kanban/components/KanbanCard.tsx`, `frontend/src/features/kanban/components/KanbanFilterBar.tsx`

- **Problema:** `readOnly={readOnly || filtersActive}` is passed to every KanbanColumn (desktop :920, mobile :878). filtersActive removes the card drag handles, the column drag handle, the Add card section, the column menu and the per-card 'move to column' menu — but nothing tells the user why, and the right-click context menu (gated on the share-derived readOnly only, :307) still offers Move/Delete, so the affordances contradict each other.
- **Evidenza:** KanbanBoardPage.tsx:878 and :920 `readOnly={readOnly || filtersActive}`; :121 `const filtersActive = isFiltersActive(filters)`. KanbanColumn.tsx:64 `useSortable({ id, disabled: readOnly })`, :134 handle hidden when readOnly, :182 menu hidden, :283 add-card hidden. KanbanCard.tsx:54 and :151 same. KanbanBoardPage.tsx:306-312 handleCardContextMenu checks only the share-derived `readOnly`. No key resembling 'filters disable editing' exists under `kanban.filters` in en.json:971-990.
- **Impatto utente:** User types a word in the card search box to find a card, then tries to drag it to Done — the grip is simply gone and nothing is draggable. It reads as a broken board, not as a deliberate restriction; the only way out is discovering they must clear the filter first.
- **Fix:** Show the reason where the affordance disappeared: render a one-line hint in KanbanFilterBar when filtersActive (`kanban.filters.dragDisabled` in en.json + it.json), e.g. 'Clear filters to reorder cards'. Two keys and one conditional span.

#### `medium` · a11y · S — Card drag handle is a 4px unlabeled bar; column handle has no accessible name either
`frontend/src/features/kanban/components/KanbanCard.tsx:152` · anche: `frontend/src/features/kanban/components/KanbanColumn.tsx`

- **Problema:** The only way to drag a card is a `w-1` (4px) vertical div. dnd-kit's spread attributes give it role="button" and tabIndex=0 but it contains no text and has no aria-label, so screen readers announce an unnamed button; the column handle is an icon-only button with the same problem. 4px is far below any pointer-target guideline and the whole card body is not a drag activator.
- **Evidenza:** KanbanCard.tsx:152-157: `<div data-dnd-handle {...attributes} {...listeners} className="flex-shrink-0 self-stretch w-1 ..." />` — w-1 = 0.25rem = 4px, no children, no aria-label. KanbanColumn.tsx:135-141: `<button ... {...attributes} {...listeners}><GripVertical size={16} /></button>` — icon only, no aria-label, no type. useBoardDnD.ts:116 does register a KeyboardSensor, so these handles are the keyboard entry point too.
- **Impatto utente:** Mouse users repeatedly miss the 4px strip and click the card open instead of dragging it — the primary interaction of a kanban board is a pixel-hunt. Keyboard/screen-reader users tab onto two unnamed 'button's with no clue that they reorder cards or columns.
- **Fix:** Widen the hit area without changing the visual: keep the 4px bar but add `px-1 -mx-1` (or wrap it in a 12px padded span carrying the listeners), and add `aria-label={t('kanban.card.dragHandle')}` / `aria-label={t('kanban.column.dragHandle')}` plus `type="button"` on the column handle. Two new keys in en.json and it.json.

#### `medium` · a11y · S — Context menu submenus (Move to / Assign to / Priority) are hover-only divs — unreachable by keyboard
`frontend/src/features/kanban/components/CardContextMenu.tsx:237`

- **Problema:** The three submenu triggers are plain `<div>` elements whose only handlers are onMouseEnter/onMouseLeave. They have no tabIndex, no role, no onClick and no onFocus, so there is no way to open Move to, Assign to or Priority without a mouse — while the four sibling actions below them are real buttons and are reachable.
- **Evidenza:** CardContextMenu.tsx:237-247 (Move to), 250-260 (Assign to), 263-273 (Priority): each is `<div className={itemClass} onMouseEnter={() => handleSubmenuEnter(key)} onMouseLeave={handleSubmenuLeave}>`. itemClass (:219) only adds cursor-pointer. By contrast :276 (Due date), :284 (Link note), :298 (Duplicate), :312 (Delete) are `<button>`. The menu already handles Escape (:77-85), so keyboard interaction is otherwise expected.
- **Impatto utente:** A keyboard-only user opens the card context menu (Shift+F10 / menu key) and can reach Due date, Link note, Duplicate and Delete but can never open the three most useful submenus — moving, assigning and prioritising a card are mouse-exclusive.
- **Fix:** Change the three divs to `<button type="button" aria-haspopup="true" aria-expanded={activeSubmenu === key}>` and add `onClick={() => handleSubmenuEnter(key)}` / `onFocus={() => handleSubmenuEnter(key)}` next to the existing mouse handlers. No layout change (itemClass already renders full-width).
- **Correzione del verificatore:** Accurate as a code claim; one detail in the impact is shakier. The card itself is not focusable (KanbanCard.tsx:132-138 has onContextMenu but no tabIndex), so Shift+F10 works only while focus sits on the dnd-kit drag handle inside the card, and the opened portal menu gets no focus management — a keyboard user has to tab to the end of the document to reach the four buttons. So the asymmetry is real (4 actions reachable, 3 submenus not), but keyboard access to this menu is fragile overall, not otherwise clean.

#### `low` · ux · S — Context menu submenu clamps to a hardcoded 300px height and can render off the bottom of the screen
`frontend/src/features/kanban/components/CardContextMenu.tsx:191`

- **Problema:** computeSubmenuPosition clamps the top with `Math.min(submenuY, window.innerHeight - 300)` — a constant guess — while the submenu itself is allowed to grow to `max-h-[60vh]`. On any viewport the assign/move submenu can be taller than 300px, so its bottom lands below the fold with no way to scroll to it (the internal overflow container's own bottom edge is off-screen).
- **Evidenza:** CardContextMenu.tsx:183-193 `const clampedY = Math.max(8, Math.min(submenuY, window.innerHeight - 300));` and :226 `submenuContainerClass = '... overflow-y-auto max-h-[60vh]'`. Board members are owner + every ACCEPTED share (:128-153), so a board shared with 12 people renders 14 rows of ~40px = 560px against a 300px allowance.
- **Impatto utente:** On a board shared with a dozen people, right-click a card near the middle of the screen and open Assign to: the last members are rendered below the viewport and cannot be reached, so those people cannot be assigned from the context menu.
- **Fix:** Replace the constant with the real budget: keep clampedY as is but pass `maxHeight: window.innerHeight - clampedY - 8` in the submenu's inline style (and drop max-h-[60vh]). One line.

#### `low` · i18n · S — Bulk move header has no plural form — reads "1 cards selected"
`frontend/src/locales/en.json:870` · anche: `frontend/src/locales/it.json`, `frontend/src/features/kanban/components/BulkMoveMenu.tsx`

- **Problema:** `kanban.card.bulkMove.title` is a single string interpolating {{count}} with no `_one`/`_other` variants, but a marquee selection of exactly one card is common and the menu is rendered for any size > 0.
- **Evidenza:** en.json:870 `"title": "{{count}} cards selected"`; it.json:1125 `"title": "{{count}} card selezionate"`. BulkMoveMenu.tsx:97 and :104 call `t('kanban.card.bulkMove.title', { count: selectedCount })`. KanbanBoardPage.tsx:1011 renders the menu whenever `selectedCardIds.size > 0`.
- **Impatto utente:** Marquee over a single card and the dialog title reads '1 cards selected' / '1 card selezionate'.
- **Fix:** Add `title_one`/`title_other` in both locale files (i18next picks them automatically from the count option): en 'card selected'/'cards selected', it 'card selezionata'/'card selezionate'.

### frontend-data-layer

#### `critical` · dataloss · S — Offline/queued board creation silently loses every card: queued card CREATEs keep dead local column IDs
`frontend/src/features/sync/syncService.ts:651` · anche: `frontend/src/features/kanban/kanbanService.ts`, `frontend/src/components/editor/TransformToKanbanModal.tsx`

- **Problema:** `createBoard` invents local column UUIDs and queues them as `_localColumnIds`; after the board CREATE is pushed, syncPush rewrites the column IDs in Dexie (kanbanColumns + kanbanCards.columnId) but NOT the sync-queue items that already reference the old local column ID. Those queued card CREATEs then POST to a column that never existed on the server, get a 404, and are deleted from the queue with only a console.warn.
- **Evidenza:** kanbanService.ts:104-117 queues the board with `_localColumnIds: columns.map(c => c.id)`; kanbanService.ts:335-342 queues each card with `data: { id, columnId, title, description }` where columnId is the LOCAL uuid. syncService.ts:651-668 reconciles inside `db.transaction('rw', db.kanbanColumns, db.kanbanCards, …)` — db.syncQueue is not in the transaction and no queue item is rewritten. syncService.ts:686-687: `const columnId = item.data?.columnId; await api.post(`/kanban/columns/${columnId}/cards`, …)`. Backend: routes/kanban.ts:447 `getColumnWithAccess(id,…)` → kanbanPermissions.ts:36 `throw new NotFoundError` → utils/errors.ts:9 status 404. syncService.ts:780-784: `if (status === 404 || 410) { console.warn(…); await db.syncQueue.delete(item.id); }`. The Dexie row keeps syncStatus 'created', so syncPull's dirty-filter never deletes it and never re-pushes it.
- **Impatto utente:** User is offline (or the board POST hits one 429/500 and enters backoff), converts a note's list into a NEW kanban board via TransformToKanbanModal (createBoard.mutateAsync at line 134, then handleCreateCards → createCard.mutateAsync at line 119 using the Dexie column id). On reconnect the board appears on the server with three empty columns and every card is gone — no error, no toast, no failed-sync badge; the cards survive only as invisible Dexie rows. The user gets a green 'kanbanSuccess' toast beforehand.
- **Fix:** Add `db.syncQueue` to the reconciliation transaction (syncService.ts:651) and, for each localId→serverId pair, rewrite pending queue items that point at the dead column: `const stale = await db.syncQueue.filter(i => i.entity === 'KANBAN_CARD' && (i.data as any)?.columnId === localId).toArray(); for (const s of stale) await db.syncQueue.update(s.id!, { data: { ...s.data, columnId: serverId } });`. Covers both card CREATE and the move-shaped UPDATE at line 690. Add one vitest for it — kanbanService.test.ts currently tests only splitTextForCard/createCard caps/deleteCard, not this path.
- **Correzione del verificatore:** Two corrections. (1) The ONLINE happy path is already protected and the finding overstates its reach: TransformToKanbanModal reads columns from the Dexie live query (lines 44-64), and the reconciliation swaps the column ids in Dexie, so by the time the user clicks a column the live query normally already returns the SERVER id. The loss fires when the board CREATE is still pending — offline, in backoff after a 429/5xx, or a fast click before the POST returns. (2) The proposed fix is incomplete: syncPush snapshots the whole queue into memory at syncService.ts:549-550 (`allQueue = await db.syncQueue.orderBy('createdAt').toArray()`) BEFORE the loop, so rewriting db.syncQueue rows during reconciliation does not fix items already in the current pass — exactly the pass where board CREATE and card CREATE are processed back to back after reconnect. The fix must also patch the in-memory `queue` entries (or re-read `item.data` from Dexie immediately before the POST).

#### `high` · offline · M — Any transient board GET failure ejects the user from the board; offline board view is impossible despite full Dexie mirror
`frontend/src/features/kanban/hooks/useKanbanBoard.ts:73` · anche: `frontend/src/features/kanban/KanbanBoardPage.tsx`

- **Problema:** The board detail view is server-only (`getBoard` REST) with `retry: false`, and the page redirects to /kanban on ANY `isError` — network offline, 429, 500, a failed background refetch — even when react-query still holds valid cached data. Kanban mirrors every column and card into Dexie, but none of it is used to render the board.
- **Evidenza:** useKanbanBoard.ts:8-11 `queryFn: getBoard(boardId)` (pure REST), line 73 `retry: false, // Don't retry 404s (deleted boards)`. KanbanBoardPage.tsx:61-69: `const { data: board, isLoading, isError } = useKanbanBoard(boardId); useEffect(() => { if (!isLoading && (isError || !board)) navigate('/kanban', { replace: true }); }, …)` — no status check. The board list (KanbanPage.tsx:25 → useKanbanBoards.ts:6, a Dexie useLiveQuery) works offline, so boards are listed but none can be opened. In react-query v5 a failed refetch sets status 'error' while keeping `data`, so a blip mid-session also fires the redirect.
- **Impatto utente:** Offline (the core PWA promise): user sees their boards, taps one, and is bounced straight back to the list with no message — every time. Online: a single 429 (the feature already had a rate-limit spiral) or a 5s network drop during a background refetch throws the user out of the board mid-drag, losing open modals, filters and scroll position.
- **Fix:** Redirect only on a real 404: in the page, `const status = (error as AxiosError)?.response?.status; if (!isLoading && (status === 404 || (!board && !isError))) navigate(...)`. Keep rendering `board` when cached data exists; for the cold-offline case, build the board object from Dexie (kanbanColumns + kanbanCards by boardId, both already populated) as the queryFn fallback when the request fails.

#### `medium` · race · S — Card drag reverts visually: board query is invalidated before the queued write reaches the server
`frontend/src/features/kanban/hooks/useKanbanMutations.ts:16` · anche: `frontend/src/features/kanban/hooks/useBoardDnD.ts`

- **Problema:** Every offline-first mutation does `flushSync(); invalidateBoard();` — `flushSync` is fire-and-forget (`syncPush().catch(() => {})`, return value discarded), so the GET /kanban/boards/:id refetch races the POST/PUT it just queued. The mutation itself only writes Dexie, so it settles in milliseconds and releases the DnD guard long before the server has applied anything.
- **Evidenza:** useKanbanMutations.ts:17-19 `function flushSync(): void { syncPush().catch(() => {}); }` and lines 106-113 `moveCard … onSuccess: () => { flushSync(); invalidateBoard(); }`. useBoardDnD.ts:242-246 `setIsMoveInFlight(true); mutations.moveCard.mutate({…}, { onSettled: () => setIsMoveInFlight(false) })` — onSettled fires right after the Dexie write. useBoardDnD.ts:50-54 `if (board?.columns && !activeCardId && !activeColumnId && !isMoveInFlight) setLocalColumns(board.columns)`. KanbanBoardPage.tsx:173-178 renders `dnd.sortedColumns`. The server move (card.service.ts:202-232, three updateMany + activity log + notifications) is slower than the plain board GET, so the refetch commonly returns pre-move state.
- **Impatto utente:** User drags a card to another column; ~300ms later it jumps back to the original column, then jumps forward again when the user's own SSE `card:moved` echo arrives (kanbanSSE.ts:102 broadcasts to every connection including the actor). If SSE is disconnected (reconnect window is 5s, useKanbanRealtime.ts:118), the card stays visually reverted until a manual reload — the user re-drags it and creates a second move.
- **Fix:** Make the flush awaited and invalidate after it: `const flushSync = () => syncPush();` then `onSuccess: async () => { await flushSync(); invalidateBoard(); }` (react-query awaits an async onSuccess before onSettled, which also holds `isMoveInFlight` for the real duration). Make `syncPush` return the in-flight promise instead of `return`ing early at syncService.ts:551-555 so the await is meaningful.
- **Correzione del verificatore:** Severity is one notch too high: the move itself is never lost — it is durably queued in Dexie, and the actor receives their own `card:moved` SSE echo (kanbanSSE.ts:102-112 writes to every connection on the board), which invalidates the board query again and restores the correct order within one round trip. The observable symptom is a jump-back/jump-forward flicker on drop, degrading to a lasting visual revert only while SSE is disconnected (5s reconnect, useKanbanRealtime.ts:118). Degraded UX, not a data bug.

#### `medium` · bug · S — Kanban mutations swallow every error: no onError anywhere, and duplicateCard still hard-throws when the card is absent from Dexie
`frontend/src/features/kanban/kanbanService.ts:424` · anche: `frontend/src/features/kanban/hooks/useKanbanMutations.ts`, `frontend/src/features/kanban/KanbanBoardPage.tsx`, `frontend/src/lib/queryClient.ts`

- **Problema:** `duplicateCard` and `createCard` throw when the row/column is missing from the local Dexie cache — the exact failure mode that was fixed for `deleteCard` in v1.10.2 but left in its sibling callers. No mutation in useKanbanMutations declares `onError`, the call sites mostly pass no options, and queryClient has no MutationCache error handler, so the throw is swallowed by react-query and the user sees nothing at all.
- **Evidenza:** kanbanService.ts:423-424 `const original = await db.kanbanCards.get(cardId); if (!original) throw new Error('Card not found');` and :300-301 `const column = await db.kanbanColumns.get(columnId); if (!column) throw new Error('Column not found');` — versus the deliberate fix at :390-400 ('ALWAYS enqueue the DELETE; do local cleanup best-effort'). KanbanBoardPage.tsx:332 `mutations.duplicateCard.mutate(cardId)` and :214 `mutations.createCard.mutate({ columnId, title })` pass no onError (only deleteColumn:204, uploadCover:239 and uploadAvatar:298 do). useKanbanMutations.ts:21-190: zero onError. lib/queryClient.ts:3-10 defines only `defaultOptions.queries` — no MutationCache.
- **Impatto utente:** The board view renders from the server query while these mutations read Dexie, and Dexie hydration is explicitly best-effort (useKanbanBoard.ts:61-67 catches and logs). When they diverge — hydration failed, board opened in another tab, card arrived via a code path that didn't write Dexie — right-click → Duplicate does nothing at all: no card, no toast, no console message the user can see. Same for adding a card to a column missing locally.
- **Fix:** One global handler removes the class for every mutation in the app: in lib/queryClient.ts pass `mutationCache: new MutationCache({ onError: () => toast.error(i18n.t('common.somethingWentWrong')) })`. Then mirror the deleteCard fix in duplicateCard: when the row isn't in Dexie, fall back to the card in the board query cache instead of throwing.
- **Correzione del verificatore:** The 'no feedback on any mutation failure' half is fully proven and is the part worth fixing (a MutationCache onError in queryClient.ts removes it app-wide, not just for kanban). The specific trigger — card/column missing from Dexie — is rarer than stated: useKanbanBoard.ts:15-59 re-hydrates every column and card on each board fetch, and useKanbanRealtime.ts:138-210 writes SSE-created rows too, so divergence needs a hydration failure or a cross-tab/stale-view case. The in-repo [BACKUP] comment at kanbanService.ts:390-397 confirms the divergence was observed in production, so it is not speculative.

#### `medium` · perf · S — Board chat polls every 3 seconds forever, even with the sidebar closed and SSE already pushing messages
`frontend/src/features/kanban/hooks/useKanbanChat.ts:12` · anche: `frontend/src/features/kanban/hooks/useKanbanRealtime.ts`, `frontend/src/features/kanban/components/BoardChatSidebar.tsx`, `frontend/src/features/kanban/KanbanBoardPage.tsx`

- **Problema:** `refetchInterval: 3000` duplicates a channel that already exists: the SSE handler invalidates the exact same query key on `chat:message`. The sidebar is mounted unconditionally for any owned or shared board, so the poll runs whether or not the chat is open.
- **Evidenza:** useKanbanChat.ts:8-13 `queryKey: queryKeys.kanban.boardChat(boardId!), refetchInterval: 3000`. useKanbanRealtime.ts:33-34 `else if (event.type === 'chat:message') queryClient.invalidateQueries({ queryKey: queryKeys.kanban.boardChat(boardId!) })`, and the backend broadcasts it at services/kanban/comments-chat.service.ts:179-183. BoardChatSidebar.tsx:34-35 'Always fetch chat — sound/badge must work even when sidebar is closed'; KanbanBoardPage.tsx:1072-1086 renders it for `isOwner || isShared` with no isOpen gate.
- **Impatto utente:** 20 GET /kanban/boards/:id/chat per minute per open board tab, multiplied by every collaborator with the board open, for data SSE already delivers. On a board with 5 members that is 100 req/min of pure noise against a feature whose per-route rate limits have already had to be raised once (v1.10.3); on mobile it is a constant battery/data drain.
- **Fix:** Drop `refetchInterval` and rely on the SSE invalidation, or keep it as a slow safety net only (`refetchInterval: 60000`) since the SSE path is already wired.

#### `low` · bug · S — Phantom query key: `queryKeys.kanban.boards` is invalidated by two call sites and read by none
`frontend/src/features/kanban/hooks/useKanbanComments.ts:18` · anche: `frontend/src/features/kanban/components/ArchivedCardsModal.tsx`, `frontend/src/features/kanban/hooks/useKanbanBoards.ts`, `frontend/src/lib/queryKeys.ts`

- **Problema:** After adding a comment the hook invalidates `queryKeys.kanban.boards` (['kanban-boards']), but no `useQuery` in the codebase uses that key — the board list comes from a Dexie live query, and `kanbanService.listBoards()` is dead code. The data that actually went stale (`commentCount` on the card inside the ['kanban-board', id] query) is never invalidated; deleting a comment invalidates nothing board-related at all.
- **Evidenza:** useKanbanComments.ts:17-19 invalidates comments(cardId) + `queryKeys.kanban.boards`; line 24 (removeComment) invalidates only comments(cardId). queryKeys.ts:18 defines `boards: ['kanban-boards']`; a repo-wide grep for `queryKeys.kanban.boards` returns exactly two hits, both `invalidateQueries` (here and ArchivedCardsModal.tsx:32) — zero readers. useKanbanBoards.ts:6-8 is `useLiveQuery(() => db.kanbanBoards.orderBy('updatedAt')…)`. kanbanService.ts:59-62 `listBoards()` has no callers (syncService.ts:297 calls the endpoint directly). types.ts:71 `commentCount` is served by the board query.
- **Impatto utente:** The comment-count badge on a card is only corrected because the author receives their own SSE `comment:added` echo (kanbanSSE.ts:102 broadcasts to all connections, including the actor). Whenever SSE is down — the 5s reconnect window, a proxy that buffers the stream — adding or deleting a comment leaves a permanently wrong count on the card until a full reload. The dead key also misleads the next person adding an invalidation.
- **Fix:** Pass boardId into `useKanbanComments` and invalidate `queryKeys.kanban.board(boardId)` in both mutations; delete `queryKeys.kanban.boards` (queryKeys.ts:18), its ArchivedCardsModal.tsx:32 use, and the unused `listBoards` export.
- **Correzione del verificatore:** Two factual slips. (1) There are THREE invalidation call sites, not two: useKanbanComments.ts:18, ArchivedCardsModal.tsx:32, and features/tasks/ConvertTaskListToKanbanModal.tsx:150 — the last one lives outside the kanban feature dir and must be removed too if the key is deleted. (2) The user-visible impact is smaller than claimed: comments are only ever added from CardDetailModal on the board page, where the SSE stream is connected and the actor receives their own comment:added/comment:deleted echo (comments-chat.service.ts:58 → kanbanSSE.ts:102 broadcasts to all connections), which invalidates the board query via useKanbanRealtime.ts:62. The stale badge is confined to the SSE-down window. This is dead-code/foot-gun cleanup, not a bug users routinely hit.

#### `low` · architecture · S — useKanbanBoard's Dexie hydration re-copies syncPull's logic minus its pending-delete guard, resurrecting deleted cards
`frontend/src/features/kanban/hooks/useKanbanBoard.ts:47` · anche: `frontend/src/features/sync/syncService.ts`, `frontend/src/features/kanban/hooks/useKanbanRealtime.ts`

- **Problema:** The same server→Dexie mapping exists in three hand-written copies (board query hydration, SSE handler, syncPull), and they don't agree. The hydration copy guards only against dirty rows (`!local || local.syncStatus === 'synced'`); it lacks syncPull's zombie guard for entities with a DELETE still sitting in the sync queue, so a locally deleted card is re-inserted into Dexie by the very refetch the delete triggered.
- **Evidenza:** useKanbanBoard.ts:47-59 `const local = await db.kanbanCards.get(card.id); if (!local || local.syncStatus === 'synced') await db.kanbanCards.put(card);` — `!local` is exactly the state deleteCard leaves behind (kanbanService.ts:399-418 deletes the row and enqueues the DELETE). syncService.ts:352-365 builds `pendingCardDeleteIds` / `pendingColDeleteIds` from the queue and filters them out precisely to prevent this; useKanbanBoard has no such filter. Third copy of the mapping: useKanbanRealtime.ts:138-158 and 166-184. The mappers also disagree on shape — syncService.ts:391-399 spreads the raw server card (`...card`) into Dexie while the other two enumerate fields.
- **Impatto utente:** Delete a card while the push is pending (offline, or backoff after a 5xx): deleteCard removes it from Dexie and `invalidateBoard()` immediately refetches, and the refetch writes the card straight back into Dexie with syncStatus 'synced'. Dexie's cardCount was already decremented, so counts drift, and the zombie is what TransformToKanbanModal reads for its duplicate detection (it queries db.kanbanCards, lines 53-58) — the user is told a card already exists on a board where they deleted it.
- **Fix:** Extract the pending-delete lookup used by syncPull into one exported helper and call it in the hydration block before `put` (three added lines), or simplest: skip the hydration writes entirely for ids returned by `db.syncQueue.where('entity').equals('KANBAN_CARD').and(i => i.type === 'DELETE')`.
- **Correzione del verificatore:** The user impact is materially weaker than written. The resurrection is transient: while the DELETE is pending the card genuinely still exists server-side, and once it lands the actor's own `card:deleted` SSE echo (kanbanSSE.ts:102) removes the Dexie row, with syncPull's own cleanup (syncService.ts:401-408) as backstop on the 30s tick (useSync.ts:55). Fully offline the scenario cannot even occur — the board GET fails, so nothing is written back. The cardCount drift and the 'told a card already exists where you deleted it' claim in TransformToKanbanModal are consequently short-lived and, during the window, not actually wrong. What stands is the structural half: a triplicated mapper where one copy silently omits a guard the other deliberately added.

#### `low` · bug · S — Menu-driven card move sends the sentinel position 999, which the backend stores verbatim
`frontend/src/features/kanban/hooks/useBoardDnD.ts:295` · anche: `backend/src/services/kanban/card.service.ts`, `frontend/src/features/kanban/kanbanService.ts`

- **Problema:** `handleMoveCardToColumn` hardcodes `position: 999` as 'append to end', but the backend does no clamping — it shifts `position >= 999` (nothing) and writes 999 straight into the row. Two cards appended this way both get 999.
- **Evidenza:** useBoardDnD.ts:293-297 `mutations.moveCard.mutate({ cardId, toColumnId: targetColumnId, position: 999 }, …)` — note the same handler already computed the correct index at line 285 (`const newPosition = col.cards.length`) for the optimistic state and then discards it. backend/src/services/kanban/card.service.ts:204-213: `updateMany({ where: { columnId: toColumnId, position: { gte: newPosition } }, data: { position: { increment: 1 } } })` then `update({ data: { columnId: toColumnId, position: newPosition } })` — no clamp against the column size. kanbanService.ts:376-384 stores the same 999 in Dexie.
- **Impatto utente:** Move two cards into the same column from the mobile/context menu: both end up at position 999, so their relative order is whatever Postgres returns for the tie and can flip between refetches. Dragging a third card into that column computes positions 0..n while the two sentinels sit at 999, so drop targets near the bottom of the column land in a different slot than the drop indicator showed.
- **Fix:** Pass the real index that is already in hand: `const target = localColumns.find(c => c.id === targetColumnId); … position: target ? target.cards.length : 0`.
- **Correzione del verificatore:** The mechanism is misdiagnosed. Two cards moved this way do NOT both end up at 999 and there is no tie: the `updateMany({ position: { gte: 999 }, increment: 1 })` at card.service.ts:204-208 bumps the first card to 1000 before the second is written at 999. The actual symptoms are (a) the second menu-moved card lands ABOVE the first, inverting the order the optimistic UI just showed, and (b) the sentinels sit at 999/1000 while drag-drops append at index n, so a card dropped at the visual bottom of the column is stored at position n and renders above the sentinel cards — the drop lands in a different slot than the indicator showed. Order is deterministic, not Postgres-dependent.

#### `low` · bug · S — KanbanSSEEvent union omits two events the backend actually broadcasts
`frontend/src/features/kanban/types.ts:170` · anche: `backend/src/services/kanbanSSE.ts`, `frontend/src/features/kanban/hooks/useKanbanRealtime.ts`

- **Problema:** The frontend's discriminated union is missing `board:updated` and `card:unarchived`, both of which the backend emits. `updateDexieFromSSE` switches on that union, so those events fall through every case and write nothing to Dexie — the type reads as exhaustive but isn't.
- **Evidenza:** types.ts:170-183 lists 13 variants, none named `board:updated` or `card:unarchived`. backend/src/services/kanbanSSE.ts:33 `| { type: 'card:unarchived'; boardId: string; cardId: string }` and :41 `| { type: 'board:updated'; boardId: string }`; emitted at services/kanban/linking.service.ts:213, 235, 453, 475 (board:updated) and card.service.ts:441. Frontend useKanbanRealtime.ts:37-67 handles them only by falling into the generic else branch, and updateDexieFromSSE (lines 136-238) has no matching case.
- **Impatto utente:** Linking or unlinking a note/task list to a board, and unarchiving a card, refresh the server query but leave Dexie untouched, so an offline read of that board is stale. More practically, the next developer adding an SSE consumer trusts the union and writes an exhaustive switch that silently drops real events.
- **Fix:** Add the two variants to types.ts:170-183 (they are copy-paste from kanbanSSE.ts:28-42) and give `card:unarchived` a case in updateDexieFromSSE, or drop the two mappers in favour of the single shared one from the previous finding.
- **Correzione del verificatore:** The UI is not affected: handleEvent's else branch (useKanbanRealtime.ts:37-67) fires for any unrecognised type and still invalidates `queryKeys.kanban.board(boardId)`, so linking/unlinking and unarchiving do refresh the visible board. Only the offline Dexie mirror goes stale, and only for `card:unarchived` — `board:updated` carries no payload and needs no Dexie write at all, so the real fix is one union variant plus one case, not two. Correctly rated low; the main value is removing the false exhaustiveness for the next SSE consumer.

### gap:account-switch-dexie-kanban-leak

#### `high` · permission · S — Kanban board list reads Dexie with zero user scope — the previous account's boards render for the next user
`frontend/src/features/kanban/hooks/useKanbanBoards.ts:7` · anche: `frontend/src/features/sync/syncService.ts`, `frontend/src/store/authStore.ts`, `frontend/src/lib/db.ts`

- **Problema:** useKanbanBoards is an unfiltered full-table scan of db.kanbanBoards. Nothing in the app scopes the offline kanban mirror to a user: the table has no viewer column, logout never clears it, and this hook is the single source for every board list in the app. After A logs out and B logs in on the same browser, B's kanban page paints A's boards.
- **Evidenza:** useKanbanBoards.ts:5-13 is the whole hook: `const boards = useLiveQuery(async () => { return db.kanbanBoards.orderBy('updatedAt').reverse().toArray(); });` — no authStore import, no filter, no deps array. db.ts:253 declares `kanbanBoards: 'id, ownerId, updatedAt, syncStatus, ownership'` and the LocalKanbanBoard interface (db.ts:103-120) has no viewer/userId field. authStore.ts:74-78 `logout()` only does `set({user:null,token:null})`, `resetVault()`, `queryClient.clear()` — queryClient.clear() wipes the react-query cache, which is exactly why this Dexie-backed list survives. Contrast useNotes.ts:24 which explicitly does `if (note.userId !== user.id) return false;` — kanban is the outlier. Three consumers all feed off this one hook: KanbanPage.tsx:25, TransformToKanbanModal.tsx:37, ConvertTaskListToKanbanModal.tsx:45.
- **Impatto utente:** Shared browser (family tablet, office kiosk, demo laptop). A logs out, B logs in, opens /kanban. useLiveQuery resolves from IndexedDB in a few ms, long before syncPull reaches its kanban section — syncPull (syncService.ts:9-12) starts with `await api.get('/notebooks')` and the kanban pull is at line 297, six sequential HTTP round-trips later. In that window B sees A's board cards fully rendered by BoardCard.tsx: cover image (BoardCard.tsx:64-71 — /uploads/kanban/:filename is explicitly unauthenticated, app.ts:271-278, so the image really loads), avatar, title (BoardCard.tsx:124), description (BoardCard.tsx:130-132), 'shared by <owner name or email>' (BoardCard.tsx:145-147), column/card counts, share count and last-modified time. B also gets the owner context menu on them (isOwned check, BoardCard.tsx:25/74) with Share and Delete.
- **Fix:** Scope the read at the one choke point. In useKanbanBoards.ts: `const userId = useAuthStore(s => s.user?.id);` then `useLiveQuery(async () => { if (!userId) return []; return db.kanbanBoards.orderBy('updatedAt').reverse().filter(b => b.ownerId === userId || (b.ownership === 'shared' && b.viewerId === userId)).toArray(); }, [userId])`. Owned rows need no new data — ownerId is already correct on every existing row (server sets it, kanbanService.ts:74 sets it locally). For shared rows add a plain `viewerId: <current user id>` property where the two pulls write them (syncService.ts:313-316 and 431-439); it is a non-indexed property, so Dexie stores it with NO schema change and db.ts (TIER 1) is not touched. It fails closed: pre-upgrade shared rows lack viewerId and stay hidden until the first successful pull rewrites them. Prefer this over a wipe in logout() — the wipe does not help when B's session simply never prunes (offline), and it destroys A's un-pushed offline work on an ordinary logout. Prefer it over a v16 userId column too: you cannot backfill the viewer for existing shared rows, and a TIER 1 migration is a lot of risk for a list of a few dozen rows that an in-memory filter already handles.
- **Correzione del verificatore:** The claim is accurate as written; only the severity label needs calibration. In the ordinary online case the leak is time-boxed to the first syncPull's kanban section (syncService.ts:294-339 prunes A's synced owned boards, and 435-448 prunes A's stale shared boards), i.e. seconds, not forever. It becomes permanent only via the companion findings — offline/failed pull (finding 3) and dirty rows (finding 4). Exposure during the window is real and includes collaborator emails (finding 2), so high rather than critical. The proposed fix is sound and is the right choke point: the hook is the single reader for all three consumers.

#### `high` · permission · S — Stale board rows disclose collaborators' email addresses through SharedUsersModal with no server round-trip
`frontend/src/features/kanban/KanbanPage.tsx:46` · anche: `frontend/src/features/kanban/components/BoardCard.tsx`, `frontend/src/components/sharing/SharedUsersModal.tsx`

- **Problema:** The 'view shares' modal is populated entirely from the local Dexie board row's shares[] array — no API call, no permission check. Combined with the unscoped list, user B can read the full name/email/avatar of everyone user A shared a board with, straight out of IndexedDB, offline.
- **Evidenza:** KanbanPage.tsx:46-54 builds `viewSharesUsers` from `viewSharesBoard?.shares?.map(s => ({ id: s.user.id, name: s.user.name, email: s.user.email, ... }))` where viewSharesBoard comes from `boards?.find(...)` (line 44) — i.e. the unfiltered Dexie array. KanbanPage.tsx:146-152 renders `<SharedUsersModal users={viewSharesUsers} owner={viewSharesOwner} />`. The data is genuinely there: backend listBoards selects `shares: { user: { select: { id, name, email, avatarUrl } } }` (board.service.ts:27-34, 58-65) and syncService.ts:311-316 spreads `...b` verbatim into Dexie. db.ts:114 types it: `shares?: { userId, permission, user: { id, name, email, avatarUrl } }[]`. The entry point is a visible button — BoardCard.tsx:163-172 renders the share-count chip whenever `board.shareCount > 0`.
- **Impatto utente:** B, on the shared browser, clicks the little person-count chip on one of A's leftover board cards and gets a modal listing the email addresses of A's colleagues. Works with the network cable pulled — it is pure IndexedDB. No 403 is possible because no request is made.
- **Fix:** Fixed for free by scoping useKanbanBoards (finding 1) — viewSharesBoard is a `.find()` over that same array, so a board that is not yours is no longer findable. No change needed here once the hook filters.

#### `high` · dataloss · S — Boards with unsynced local changes are never pruned, and their queue items can never push after an account switch — permanent ghosts
`frontend/src/features/sync/syncService.ts:319`

- **Problema:** The prune only considers boards whose syncStatus === 'synced'. A board that user A created or edited offline stays 'created'/'updated' forever in user B's session, because the queue entries that would sync it are correctly filtered out by userId in syncPush and therefore never resolve. The result is a board of A's that no successful pull will ever delete.
- **Evidenza:** syncService.ts:318-324: `const allLocalSyncedBoards = await db.kanbanBoards.where('syncStatus').equals('synced').filter(b => b.ownership !== 'shared').toArray();` — deletion candidates are synced rows only; dirty rows are also excluded from boardsToPut at 311-312. Meanwhile syncPush.ts (syncService.ts:561-563) does `const allQueue = await db.syncQueue.orderBy('createdAt').toArray(); const queue = allQueue.filter(item => item.userId === currentUserId);` and every kanban enqueue does set userId (kanbanService.ts:108, 146, 169, 208, 228, 244, 275, 339, 366, 381, 414, 476 — verified all twelve). So A's kanban queue items are correctly NOT pushed under B's JWT (good), but they are also never cleared, so the entity stays dirty, so the prune skips it, forever.
- **Impatto utente:** A creates a board on the train (offline), never gets back online, logs out. B logs in, goes online, syncPull succeeds — and A's board is still sitting at the top of B's board list, permanently, with its title and description. The one path that would clean it up (B deleting it) produces the failure in the next finding.
- **Fix:** Same single fix as finding 1: the read filter makes dirty foreign rows unreachable regardless of prune behaviour. If you also want the storage reclaimed, add to the prune at 322-324 a second candidate set: dirty boards whose ownerId is not the current user (`b.ownerId !== currentUserId`) — the current user can never sync those anyway.

#### `medium` · offline · M — syncPull's kanban prune never runs if any earlier pull throws — offline the leak is permanent
`frontend/src/features/sync/syncService.ts:490`

- **Problema:** The entire syncPull body sits inside one try whose catch only console.errors. The kanban reconciliation is the last section (line 295+), reached only after five sequential un-guarded awaits. If the first request fails — offline, or one 500 on /notebooks — the kanban prune is skipped entirely and no stale board is ever removed.
- **Evidenza:** syncService.ts:9-12: `export const syncPull = async () => { try { const notebooksRes = await api.get<Notebook[]>('/notebooks');` — the very first statement, not in its own try. The kanban prune is at 294-339, after notebooks/tags/notes/shared-notes/tasklists/shared-tasklists. The outer catch is syncService.ts:490-492: `} catch (error) { console.error('Sync Pull Failed:', error); }`. So on any earlier throw, control jumps past line 294. Only the kanban blocks themselves have inner try/catch (296/419-421, 424/486-488) — the earlier sections do not.
- **Impatto utente:** B logs in on the shared browser while offline, or the backend hiccups on /notebooks. A's boards are displayed and stay displayed for the whole session: nothing ever deletes them, and the periodic pull (useSync.ts:37, every 30s) fails at the same first line each time. Same effect for the ordinary offline user: an offline session never reconciles kanban at all, even for boards deleted on another device.
- **Fix:** Wrap each pull section in its own try/catch the way the kanban ones already are, or at minimum move the kanban blocks ahead of nothing and give notebooks/tags/notes the same inner try. Cheapest: change the six leading `await api.get(...)` sections to the same `try { ... } catch (e) { console.error(...) }` shape used at 296 and 424, so one failing endpoint stops that entity only. Note this is defence-in-depth — the read filter in finding 1 is what actually removes the leak, since it does not depend on any network call succeeding.
- **Correzione del verificatore:** Two evidence details are wrong. (a) There are three unguarded leading awaits, not five: shared notes (:133-186), tasklists (:191-250) and shared tasklists (:255-291) each already have their own try/catch, so the sentence 'the earlier sections do not' is false for half of them. (b) The blast radius is correspondingly narrower — only a notebooks/tags/notes failure aborts the kanban prune. Severity medium, not high: the finding itself concedes it is defence-in-depth behind the finding-1 read filter, and the fix it proposes (give the three leading sections the same inner try/catch shape already used at :133/:191/:255) is a mechanical three-block change, not the M it is scored at.

#### `medium` · bug · S — Deleting a leftover board queues a DELETE that 403s permanently, leaving an unclearable 'sync failed' banner
`frontend/src/features/kanban/kanbanService.ts:152` · anche: `frontend/src/features/kanban/components/BoardCard.tsx`, `frontend/src/features/sync/syncService.ts`

- **Problema:** deleteBoard is unconditionally local-first: it removes the rows and queues a DELETE with the current user's id, without checking the board is actually the current user's. On a board belonging to another account the server answers 403, which syncService treats as terminal — the item is marked 'failed' and surfaced in the sync indicator with a retry that can never succeed, for an entity the user can no longer see.
- **Evidenza:** kanbanService.ts:152-173: no ownership check; deletes Dexie rows then `db.syncQueue.add({ type:'DELETE', entity:'KANBAN_BOARD', entityId: boardId, userId, ... })`. BoardCard.tsx:25 `const isOwned = board.ownership === 'owned';` and :74 `{isOwned && (` — a foreign board pulled as 'owned' shows the delete item (BoardCard.tsx:100-106). Backend: kanban.ts:156-160 `const { isOwner } = await assertBoardAccess(id, request.user.id, 'WRITE')`, and kanbanPermissions.ts:16-20 throws ForbiddenError when there is no accepted share → 403. syncService.ts:797-806 turns 403 into `status:'failed', lastError:'forbidden'` immediately. retryFailedSyncItems (syncService.ts:826-838) re-enables it and it 403s again.
- **Impatto utente:** B tries to tidy up by deleting the strange board. The card vanishes, and a permanent red sync-failure indicator appears; pressing Retry re-fails every time. B has no way to identify or clear the offending item because the entity is already gone from Dexie.
- **Fix:** Guard the local-first delete: in kanbanService.deleteBoard, read the row first and bail (or fall back to a server-only delete) when `board.ownerId !== userId`. One `const board = await db.kanbanBoards.get(boardId); if (board && board.ownerId !== userId) throw new Error('errors.common.accessDenied');` before the transaction. With finding 1 fixed the case is unreachable through the UI, but the guard is the cheap belt for any other caller.

#### `medium` · permission · S — Transform/Convert modals read columns and cards straight from Dexie by boardId, letting one account target another's leftover board
`frontend/src/components/editor/TransformToKanbanModal.tsx:47` · anche: `frontend/src/features/tasks/ConvertTaskListToKanbanModal.tsx`, `frontend/src/features/kanban/kanbanService.ts`

- **Problema:** The note→kanban and tasklist→kanban wizards list boards from the same unscoped hook and then read the chosen board's columns and cards directly from Dexie by boardId, with no ownership check and no server involvement. Selecting a foreign board shows its column titles and card counts, uses its card titles as a duplicate oracle, and queues card CREATEs that the backend will refuse.
- **Evidenza:** TransformToKanbanModal.tsx:37 `useKanbanBoards()` (unfiltered), rendered as a picker at :231-246. On selection, :47-61 read `db.kanbanColumns.where('boardId').equals(selectedBoardId)`, `db.kanbanCards.where('boardId').equals(selectedBoardId)` and `db.kanbanBoards.get(selectedBoardId)`; :63 `const columns = (liveColumns?.length ? liveColumns : boardDetail?.columns) ?? []` explicitly prefers Dexie over the server query. :282-295 renders each column title with `cardCount(col.id)`, and :66-72 `cardTitlesInColumn` feeds the duplicate check at :88-97 from those local cards. ConvertTaskListToKanbanModal.tsx:45 uses the same hook for its picker (:239-250). Committing then goes through createCard (kanbanService.ts:458-479) → POST /kanban/columns/:id/cards → assertColumnAccess → 403 → syncService.ts:797-806 marks the item permanently failed.
- **Impatto utente:** B transforms a note checklist into cards, and the board picker offers A's boards. Picking one shows A's column names and how many cards each holds; the duplicate check quietly reveals which of B's item texts already exist as cards on A's board. The cards are written into B's Dexie under A's boardId and the push dies with a permanent failed-sync item.
