- **Fix:** Covered by finding 1: once useKanbanBoards filters, selectedBoardId can only ever be a board the user may see, so the by-boardId Dexie reads below it become safe. No separate change needed — which is the argument for putting the fix in the hook rather than in each page.
- **Correzione del verificatore:** One citation is wrong: createCard is kanbanService.ts:284-347, not :458-479 (that range is the tail of duplicateCard, :421-486). The substance is unaffected — the CREATE enqueue is at :335-341 and carries `{ id, columnId, title, description }`. Worth adding for whoever fixes this: even after finding 1's filter, a READ-only shared board is still offered by both pickers and will still 403 on card creation, so the picker arguably wants `ownership === 'owned' || permission === 'WRITE'`, not just a viewer scope.

#### `low` · dataloss · M — Columns and cards of boards outside the current user's server list are never pruned — orphan rows accumulate with their content
`frontend/src/features/sync/syncService.ts:405` · anche: `frontend/src/features/kanban/kanbanService.ts`

- **Problema:** Card and column reconciliation runs inside the per-board loop over the server's board list, so it can only ever clean boards the current user already has. Rows keyed to any other boardId — another account's, or a board dropped while dirty — are never visited by any prune and stay in IndexedDB indefinitely with full card titles, descriptions, due dates and assignees.
- **Evidenza:** syncService.ts:342 `for (const board of serverBoards) {` wraps the reconciliation; the card prune at 405-411 is `db.kanbanCards.where('boardId').equals(fullBoard.id)` — scoped to that board. The only other cascade is syncService.ts:326-335, which runs only for `toDeleteIds`, itself limited to synced non-shared boards (line 319-324). kanbanService.ts:159-162 makes it worse locally: the card cascade is inside `if (columnIds.length > 0)`, so deleting a board whose columns were never hydrated leaves its cards behind. Nothing anywhere calls .clear() on these tables — `grep '\.clear()' frontend/src` returns only useKanbanRealtime.ts:25 (a timer map) and authStore.ts:77 (react-query).
- **Impatto utente:** On a browser used by several accounts over time, IndexedDB accumulates every card any of them ever opened, unbounded, and those card bodies remain readable through the Dexie-backed pickers (next finding). It also means 'log out' is not a meaningful privacy boundary for card content.
- **Fix:** Move the card cascade in kanbanService.deleteBoard out of the `columnIds.length > 0` guard (one line), and in the kanban pull add a sweep after line 338 for rows whose boardId is not in the local board table: `const liveIds = new Set((await db.kanbanBoards.toCollection().primaryKeys()) as string[]); await db.kanbanCards.filter(c => !liveIds.has(c.boardId)).delete();` plus the same for columns. Cheap, runs once per pull, and needs no schema change.
- **Correzione del verificatore:** Two overstatements. (a) 'The only other cascade is syncService.ts:326-335' is incomplete — the shared-kanban pull at syncService.ts:440-448 deletes columns and cards for every stale shared boardId, so revoked shares do get cleaned. (b) The userImpact is wrong where it says the orphan card bodies 'remain readable through the Dexie-backed pickers': those pickers key off a selected boardId that comes from the board list, so once the board row is pruned its orphan cards are unreachable from any UI — they are dead bytes in IndexedDB, visible only via devtools. That leaves storage growth and data-at-rest hygiene, not a UI-reachable disclosure; low rather than medium. The dangling-cards half of the kanbanService fix is also near-theoretical, since columns and cards are written in the same pull transaction (:373-407, :456-476) — a board with cards but no local columns is hard to produce. The one-line guard removal is still free and worth taking.

### gap:board-image-upload-and-serving

#### `medium` · permission · S — Kanban cover/avatar upload: stored file extension comes from the client filename, no allowlist — .svg served as an active document from an unauthenticated same-origin URL
`backend/src/routes/kanban.ts:210` · anche: `backend/src/routes/kanban.ts:291`, `backend/src/app.ts:137`, `backend/src/app.ts:146`, `backend/src/app.ts:263`, `backend/src/app.ts:272`, `backend/src/services/attachment.service.ts:25`, `backend/src/services/chat-file.service.ts:15`

- **Problema:** The only type gate is `ALLOWED_IMAGE_TYPES.has(data.mimetype)` (kanban.ts:176 cover, :257 avatar), and `mimetype` is the client-supplied multipart Content-Type header — never the bytes. The stored name is then `randomUUID() + path.extname(data.filename).toLowerCase()` (kanban.ts:210/291) with no extension allowlist, written verbatim (kanban.ts:213/294). app.ts:146-148 maps that extension back to a MIME through IMAGE_MIME_MAP, which includes `svg: 'image/svg+xml'`, and app.ts:263-278 serves it with no auth ('no auth required'). So any logged-in user stores arbitrary bytes under an arbitrary extension at a stable, unauthenticated URL on the app origin.
- **Evidenza:** kanban.ts:176 `if (!ALLOWED_IMAGE_TYPES.has(data.mimetype))` — header only, never the buffer; kanban.ts:210 `const ext = path.extname(data.filename || '.jpg').toLowerCase();` then :211 `${randomUUID()}${ext}` and :213 `fs.writeFileSync(filepath, buffer)`. app.ts:137-140 `IMAGE_MIME_MAP = { ... svg: 'image/svg+xml' }`; app.ts:272-278 `server.get('/uploads/kanban/:filename', ...) => serveFile(filepath, safeImageType(safeName), ...)` with no `fastify.authenticate` (that hook only exists inside the kanban plugin, kanban.ts:86). Verified mitigations, so the honest scoring: app.ts:60-70 sets a global CSP with `script-src 'self' 'wasm-unsafe-eval'` and NO 'unsafe-inline', which does block `<script>` and `onload=` inside a directly-navigated SVG document, and app.ts:55 `nosniff` makes `.html`/`.htm` (which fall through to `application/octet-stream` at app.ts:148) download instead of render. frontend/public/web.config only rewrites `/uploads/(.*)` to the node backend and adds no headers, so the Fastify CSP is what ships. This is therefore NOT live stored XSS today — it is one CSP relaxation (or one proxy that drops the header) away from being exactly that, and note attachments (attachment.service.ts:25 BLOCKED_EXTENSIONS incl. .html/.php/.js) and chat files (chat-file.service.ts:15) both validate the extension. Kanban is the only upload path in the repo with no extension check at all.
- **Impatto utente:** Any authenticated user POSTs `file=<svg xmlns="..."><foreignObject>...` with `filename=x.svg` and `Content-Type: image/png` to their own board's cover, then shares `https://notiq.epartner.it/uploads/kanban/<uuid>.svg`: attacker-controlled content renders in-browser on the product's own domain, unauthenticated and permanently (phishing surface, arbitrary-file hosting under the company domain, reputation/abuse-report exposure). Any weakening of the CSP turns the same stored file into script execution against a page whose JWT sits in localStorage `auth-storage`.
- **Fix:** Stop trusting the client filename: derive the extension from the mimetype that was already validated two lines above. `const EXT_BY_MIME: Record<string,string> = { 'image/jpeg':'.jpg','image/png':'.png','image/gif':'.gif','image/webp':'.webp' }; const ext = EXT_BY_MIME[data.mimetype];` at kanban.ts:210 and :291. Three lines, kills the whole class (arbitrary extension, svg, .aspx/.php on a Windows host, and the empty-extension bug below), and needs no new dependency. Re-encoding through sharp (the chat path's trick) is the bigger fix, not the smaller one: it costs CPU per upload, silently flattens animated GIFs unless you pass `{animated:true}`, and still stores the client's extension unless you change this line anyway. Add one route test in backend/src/routes/__tests__/kanban.route.test.ts (which today has zero coverage of /cover and /avatar) asserting that filename `x.svg` + mimetype `image/png` is stored as `.png`.
- **Correzione del verificatore:** Mechanism is exactly as described, but the realistic impact today is unauthenticated arbitrary-content hosting / phishing on the app origin, not code execution — the finding itself concedes this, so 'high' overstates it. Correcting to medium.

#### `medium` · dataloss · S — Deleting a kanban board leaves its cover and avatar files on disk, still publicly readable, with no cleanup job that knows about them
`backend/src/services/kanban/board.service.ts:271` · anche: `backend/src/routes/kanban.ts:156`, `backend/src/scripts/pruneAttachments.ts:5`, `backend/src/app.ts:263`, `backend/src/app.ts:272`

- **Problema:** `deleteBoard` is `prisma.kanbanBoard.delete({ where: { id: boardId } })` and nothing else. The cover and avatar files written at kanban.ts:213/294 are never unlinked (the DELETE /cover and /avatar routes unlink, but board deletion does not), and `npm run prune` only walks the `Attachment` table — it has no notion of `uploads/kanban/`. Grep for 'kanban' across backend/src/scripts returns nothing.
- **Evidenza:** backend/src/services/kanban/board.service.ts:271-273 `export async function deleteBoard(boardId: string) { return prisma.kanbanBoard.delete({ where: { id: boardId } }); }` — no fs call. backend/src/scripts/pruneAttachments.ts:5 `const UPLOAD_DIR = path.join(__dirname, '../../uploads')` then iterates `prisma.attachment.findMany(...)` only. The orphaned files remain served unauthenticated by app.ts:263-278 because those handlers only stat the filesystem, they never consult the DB.
- **Impatto utente:** A user deletes a board to get rid of a cover image containing sensitive content (a whiteboard photo, a customer screenshot). The board is gone from the UI, but anyone who ever saw the `/uploads/kanban/<uuid>.png` URL — including a collaborator whose share was revoked — keeps full access to that image forever, and the bytes are never reclaimed. At 5MB per cover plus 2MB per avatar with board creation allowed at 10/min, disk grows monotonically with no operator tooling to reclaim it.
- **Fix:** Unlink in the same place the DB row dies. In the DELETE /boards/:id route (kanban.ts:156-164), before `kanbanService.deleteBoard(id)`, select `{ coverImage, avatarUrl }` and reuse the exact unlink snippet already at kanban.ts:203-206. Ten lines, one place, and it covers every deletion path since that route is the only caller.
- **Correzione del verificatore:** Two nits that do not change the verdict: category 'dataloss' is backwards (this is data *retention*, not loss), and the orphan URLs are randomUUID-based, so post-deletion exposure requires someone to already hold the link — the monotonic disk growth with zero operator tooling is the solid half.

#### `medium` · mobile · S — Cover and avatar controls are hover-only: on touch devices an existing cover can never be changed or removed
`frontend/src/features/kanban/KanbanBoardPage.tsx:412` · anche: `frontend/src/features/kanban/KanbanBoardPage.tsx:472`, `frontend/src/features/kanban/KanbanBoardPage.tsx:575`, `frontend/src/features/kanban/KanbanBoardPage.tsx:649`

- **Problema:** The change/remove cover buttons live in `opacity-0 group-hover/cover:opacity-100` and the avatar remove badge in `opacity-0 group-hover/avatar:opacity-100`. Touch devices have no hover, and the two alternative entry points (toolbar button at :575, board menu item at :649) are both gated on `!board.coverImage` — they only offer 'add cover', never change or remove. So once a cover exists there is no reachable control on mobile at all. Keyboard users hit the same wall: the buttons are focusable but stay at opacity 0, so focus is invisible.
- **Evidenza:** KanbanBoardPage.tsx:412 `<div className="absolute top-2 right-2 flex items-center gap-1 opacity-0 group-hover/cover:opacity-100 transition-opacity">` wrapping both the change (:415) and remove (:421) buttons; :472 `className="absolute -top-1 -right-1 ... opacity-0 group-hover/avatar:opacity-100 transition-opacity"` for avatar remove; :575 `{!readOnly && !board.coverImage && (` toolbar add-cover; :649 the identical `!board.coverImage` guard on the mobile board-menu entry. The page already knows it is on a phone — `isMobile` is used at :440 — but never uses it here.
- **Impatto utente:** A user sets a board cover from the desktop app, then opens the board on their phone (this is a PWA — mobile is a first-class target per frontend/CLAUDE.md). The cover is wrong or outdated and there is no button anywhere to change or remove it: the overlay never appears on tap, the toolbar icon is hidden because a cover exists, and the board menu offers nothing. Same for removing a board avatar.
- **Fix:** Add `focus-within:opacity-100` to both overlay wrappers (fixes keyboard in one token) and drop the `!board.coverImage` guard on the board-menu entry at :649 so the menu always offers change/remove — the menu is the mobile-reachable surface that already exists. No new component.
- **Correzione del verificatore:** One overstatement: the board avatar CAN be changed on touch — KanbanBoardPage.tsx:459-465 puts `onClick={() => !readOnly && avatarInputRef.current?.click()}` directly on the avatar `<img>`; only avatar *removal* is hover-gated. The cover claim is stronger than written: the toolbar add-cover button at :575 sits inside `{!isMobile && (` at :551, so it is desktop-only regardless of the `!board.coverImage` guard.

#### `low` · bug · S — `path.extname('.jpg')` returns '' — the upload fallback is dead code and extensionless files render as broken images forever
`backend/src/routes/kanban.ts:210` · anche: `backend/src/routes/kanban.ts:291`, `backend/src/app.ts:148`

- **Problema:** `path.extname(data.filename || '.jpg')` intends '.jpg' as a fallback, but `path.extname('.jpg')` is `''` (a leading dot is a hidden file, not an extension), and `path.extname('photo')` is `''` too. So any upload whose multipart filename is empty or has no extension is stored with NO extension. app.ts:148 then returns `application/octet-stream` for it, and the browser refuses to paint it in an `<img>`.
- **Evidenza:** Verified by running node: `[path.extname('.jpg'), path.extname('photo')]` → `["", ""]`. kanban.ts:210/291 `const ext = path.extname(data.filename || '.jpg').toLowerCase();` → filename becomes a bare UUID; app.ts:146-148 `IMAGE_MIME_MAP[ext] || 'application/octet-stream'` with `ext === ''` → octet-stream; frontend renders it via `<img src={board.coverImage}>` at KanbanBoardPage.tsx:407 and BoardCard.tsx:67, neither of which has an onError fallback.
- **Impatto utente:** A user picks a camera capture, a pasted clipboard blob, or any file whose name has no extension (common on Android share sheets and drag-and-drop of a canvas blob). The upload returns 200, the DB records a coverImage URL, and the board header plus the board-list card show a permanently broken image with no error message and no way to tell why. Removing and re-uploading the same file reproduces it every time.
- **Fix:** Same one-line change as the finding above: derive `ext` from the validated mimetype instead of the filename. That makes the fallback unnecessary and the stored extension always correct.
- **Correzione del verificatore:** The defect is real, but the described triggers are speculative: both inputs are plain `<input type="file" accept="image/jpeg,image/png,image/gif,image/webp">` (KanbanBoardPage.tsx:1054-1068) — there is no paste/clipboard-blob or canvas-blob path in this feature, so the filename always comes from an OS picker where extensionless names are an edge case, not 'common'.

#### `low` · dataloss · S — Removing a cover or avatar irreversibly deletes the file from disk with no confirmation, from an X button 4px from the change button
`frontend/src/features/kanban/KanbanBoardPage.tsx:246` · anche: `frontend/src/features/kanban/KanbanBoardPage.tsx:421`, `frontend/src/features/kanban/KanbanBoardPage.tsx:471`, `backend/src/routes/kanban.ts:233`, `backend/src/routes/kanban.ts:314`

- **Problema:** `handleRemoveCover` fires `mutations.deleteCover.mutate(boardId)` immediately on click, and the avatar X calls `mutations.deleteAvatar.mutate(boardId)` inline — both server-side handlers `fs.unlinkSync` the file (kanban.ts:233-236, 314-317). There is no ConfirmDialog, no undo, and no way to recover the image. The page imports ConfirmDialog and uses it exactly once (line 1129, board delete), so the destructive-action convention exists and these two paths skip it.
- **Evidenza:** KanbanBoardPage.tsx:246-248 `function handleRemoveCover(): void { mutations.deleteCover.mutate(boardId); }`; :471 `onClick={() => mutations.deleteAvatar.mutate(boardId)}`. The remove button sits inside the same 2-button hover overlay as 'change cover': :415 `onClick={() => coverInputRef.current?.click()}` and :421 `onClick={handleRemoveCover}`, separated only by `gap-1` (:412). Backend: kanban.ts:232-236 `if (board?.coverImage) { ... fs.unlinkSync(oldFile); }`. Only one ConfirmDialog exists in the whole 1100-line page (grep: line 1129).
- **Impatto utente:** A user hovering the cover to swap the image mis-clicks the adjacent X (they are 4px apart at 14px icon size) and the cover is gone from disk instantly — no prompt, no undo, no toast, and the original file only exists on their machine if they still have it. On a shared board any WRITE collaborator can do the same to the owner's cover.
- **Fix:** Route both removals through the ConfirmDialog already imported at line 24 and already wired for board delete at 1129 — reuse the existing modal state pattern in useBoardModals rather than adding a new component. Two handlers change from `mutate(...)` to `setShowRemoveCoverConfirm(true)`.
- **Correzione del verificatore:** Accurate on every mechanical point, but the lost asset is one decorative image the user just picked from their own filesystem, not user-authored content — 'medium/dataloss' is heavier than the actual consequence.

#### `low` · race · S — Cover/avatar changes fire no SSE event and never touch the Dexie board row — collaborators and the board list keep showing a stale or 404 image
`frontend/src/features/kanban/hooks/useKanbanMutations.ts:131` · anche: `frontend/src/features/kanban/hooks/useKanbanBoards.ts:6`, `frontend/src/features/kanban/hooks/useKanbanBoard.ts:14`, `frontend/src/features/kanban/components/BoardCard.tsx:64`, `backend/src/routes/kanban.ts:166`, `backend/src/services/kanban/linking.service.ts:213`

- **Problema:** Three separate layers miss the update. (a) The four routes at kanban.ts:166-333 never call `broadcast()` — every other mutating kanban path does (linking.service.ts:213 emits `board:updated`), so an SSE-connected collaborator is never told. (b) The mutations only run `invalidateBoard()`, which invalidates `queryKeys.kanban.board(id)` — the detail query. (c) The board LIST renders from Dexie (`useKanbanBoards` → `db.kanbanBoards`), and `useKanbanBoard`'s hydration writes columns and cards to Dexie but deliberately never writes the board row, so `coverImage`/`avatarUrl` in Dexie only heal on the next full `syncPull`. Note the permission asymmetry that makes this matter: uploading and deleting a cover/avatar require only WRITE (kanban.ts:170, 226, 251, 307) while deleting the board requires `isOwner` (kanban.ts:156-164), so a collaborator can replace or wipe the owner's images and the owner gets no SSE event and no notification.
- **Evidenza:** useKanbanMutations.ts:131-142 `const uploadCover = useMutation({ ... onSuccess: invalidateBoard })` and `deleteCover` likewise; :165-174 same for avatar; :10-14 `invalidateBoard` only invalidates `queryKeys.kanban.board(boardId)`. useKanbanBoards.ts:6-8 `useLiveQuery(async () => db.kanbanBoards.orderBy('updatedAt').reverse().toArray())`. useKanbanBoard.ts:14-19 hydrates only `db.kanbanColumns` and `db.kanbanCards` ('Write columns + cards to Dexie'). BoardCard.tsx:64-70 renders `board.coverImage` from that Dexie row with no onError. grep for `broadcast` in backend/src/routes/kanban.ts returns zero hits.
- **Impatto utente:** User removes a board cover, navigates back to /kanban, and the board card still shows the deleted cover — now a broken image, because the file was unlinked server-side and the URL 404s. It stays broken until the next syncPull or a reload. On a shared board, a collaborator who swaps the cover leaves every other member looking at the old image (or a 404) for the whole SSE session, with no indication anything changed.
- **Fix:** Two one-liners. Frontend: in the four onSuccess handlers, also write the returned value into Dexie — `db.kanbanBoards.update(bid, { coverImage: res.coverImage })` (safe: syncPush for KANBAN_BOARD only ships the queued `data` payload, which kanbanService.ts:134-150 fills with title/description only, so this field is never pushed back). Backend: add `broadcast(id, { type: 'board:updated', boardId: id })` before the return in each of the four routes — the event type already exists (kanbanSSE.ts:41) and the client already handles it via the generic branch in useKanbanRealtime.ts:59-61.
- **Correzione del verificatore:** All three mechanisms verified, but the board-list window is bounded, not open-ended: useSync.ts:50-55 runs syncPull on mount and every 30s, and syncPull (syncService.ts:296-338) bulkPuts full server board rows including coverImage/avatarUrl into Dexie, so the broken card on /kanban self-heals within ~30s. The durable half is the missing SSE broadcast for collaborators (staleTime 5min in lib/queryClient.ts; board:updated is the only thing that would invalidate an idle board).

#### `low` · ux · S — Upload errors show a generic toast although the backend returns precise translated keys, and both delete mutations have no error handler at all
`frontend/src/features/kanban/KanbanBoardPage.tsx:241` · anche: `frontend/src/features/kanban/KanbanBoardPage.tsx:300`, `frontend/src/features/kanban/hooks/useKanbanMutations.ts:139`, `frontend/src/features/kanban/hooks/useKanbanMutations.ts:171`, `frontend/src/utils/errorUtils.ts:8`, `backend/src/routes/kanban.ts:186`

- **Problema:** The backend already replies with specific i18n keys — `errors.kanban.coverTooLarge`, `errors.kanban.avatarTooLarge`, `errors.common.onlyImageFormatsAllowed`, `errors.attachments.noFileUploaded` — and all of them exist and are translated in both locales. The client discards the response and toasts a fixed string ('Failed to upload cover image' / 'An error occurred'). Worse, `deleteCover` and `deleteAvatar` have no onError at all, so a failed removal (403, offline — these are server-only mutations with no offline queue) is completely silent while the UI is invalidated as if it worked.
- **Evidenza:** KanbanBoardPage.tsx:241 `{ onError: () => toast.error(t('kanban.cover.uploadError')) }` and :300 `{ onError: () => toast.error(t('common.genericError')) }` — neither inspects the error. Backend keys: kanban.ts:186 `reply.status(400).send({ message: 'errors.kanban.coverTooLarge' })`, :177 `'errors.common.onlyImageFormatsAllowed'`. Locale files already carry them: en.json:1558 'File too large (max 5MB)', it.json:1558 'File troppo grande (max 5MB)', en.json:1506 / it.json:1506 for the format message. useKanbanMutations.ts:138-142 and :171-174 define deleteCover/deleteAvatar with only `onSuccess`. The helper for exactly this already exists: frontend/src/utils/errorUtils.ts:8 `getApiErrorMessage(error, fallbackKey)` which runs the server key through i18n.
- **Impatto utente:** A user picks a 7MB phone photo for a cover. The server explains 'File too large (max 5MB)' in their language; they see 'Failed to upload cover image' and retry the same file, or conclude the feature is broken. A READ-only collaborator (or anyone offline) taps remove-cover: nothing happens, no error, and the image reappears on the next refetch.
- **Fix:** Use the existing helper: `onError: (e) => toast.error(getApiErrorMessage(e, 'kanban.cover.uploadError'))` on the two upload handlers, and add the same onError to the two delete mutations in useKanbanMutations.ts. Four lines, no new keys needed (though `kanban.avatar.uploadError` would be worth adding to en.json:950 / it.json alongside `add`/`change`/`remove`, since avatar currently borrows `common.genericError`).
- **Correzione del verificatore:** Everything verified; 'medium' is generous for a wrong-toast-text papercut plus a silent no-op on a button already hidden from READ-only users (`!readOnly` gates it at KanbanBoardPage.tsx:411 and :470), which leaves offline as the realistic silent-failure case.

#### `low` · perf · S — The two 5MB upload endpoints have no per-route rate limit and do their buffering plus writes synchronously on the event loop
`backend/src/routes/kanban.ts:213` · anche: `backend/src/routes/kanban.ts:183`, `backend/src/routes/kanban.ts:294`, `backend/src/routes/kanban.ts:124`, `backend/src/app.ts:130`

- **Problema:** POST /boards/:id/cover and /boards/:id/avatar buffer the whole file in memory (`chunks.push(chunk)` then `Buffer.concat`) and then call `fs.writeFileSync`, plus `existsSync`/`mkdirSync`/`unlinkSync` — every one of them blocking the single Node event loop for the duration of a 5MB write. Unlike every other write-heavy kanban route they carry no `config.rateLimit`, so they inherit only the global 600/min that was raised in v1.10.3 for read traffic.
- **Evidenza:** kanban.ts:183-191 accumulates into `chunks`, :192 `Buffer.concat(chunks)`, :195-197 `fs.existsSync`/`fs.mkdirSync`, :203-206 `fs.existsSync`/`fs.unlinkSync`, :213 `fs.writeFileSync(filepath, buffer)` (identical block at :274-294 for avatar). Compare the routes that do declare limits: kanban.ts:124 `{ config: { rateLimit: { max: 10, timeWindow: '1 minute' } } }` for board create, :400 30/min for chat, :445 50/min for cards. Global limit is app.ts:130 `max: 600`.
- **Impatto utente:** One client looping cover uploads on a single board can occupy the event loop with hundreds of synchronous multi-MB writes per minute, stalling every other request on the process — SSE heartbeats, the collab websocket upgrade, sync pushes — for everyone on notiq.epartner.it, without ever tripping a limit.
- **Fix:** Copy the rate-limit config from kanban.ts:124 onto both POST routes (`max: 10, timeWindow: '1 minute'` is plenty for a cover image), and change the two `fs.writeFileSync` calls to `await fs.promises.writeFile`. Two lines each, no new middleware.
- **Correzione del verificatore:** 'without ever tripping a limit' is imprecise — the global limiter does apply at 600/min/IP (app.ts:127-131). The point survives: 600 synchronous multi-MB writes per minute per IP is orders of magnitude past what a cover-image endpoint should permit.

### gap:note-autoshare-privilege-escalation

#### `high` · permission · S — shareWithUserIds is an unchecked grant list: link-note force-shares your note with any user IDs
`backend/src/services/kanban/linking.service.ts:208` · anche: `backend/src/routes/kanban.ts`, `backend/src/services/sharing.service.ts`, `backend/src/services/kanban/linking.service.ts`

- **Problema:** The client-supplied `shareWithUserIds` array is passed straight from the request body into `autoShareNoteForBoard` and used as a grant list. Nothing — not the route, not the service, not the sharing service — ever checks that those IDs belong to the board. The only authorization performed is that the caller owns the note.
- **Evidenza:** routes/kanban.ts:330 `shareWithUserIds: z.array(z.string().uuid()).optional()` (no bound, no membership constraint) → kanban.ts:345-346 `linkNoteToBoard(id, noteId, request.user.id, shareWithUserIds)` → linking.service.ts:208-211 `if (shareWithUserIds && shareWithUserIds.length > 0) { ... await autoShareNoteForBoard(actorId, noteId, shareWithUserIds, 'READ', board.title); }`. Identical path for cards at kanban.ts:541-542 → linking.service.ts:99-108. In sharing.service.ts:150-152 the ONLY checks are `if (!note) throw NotFoundError` and `if (note.userId !== ownerId) throw ForbiddenError` — i.e. it validates the SOURCE, never the TARGETS. Then sharing.service.ts:161-165 upserts `status: 'ACCEPTED'` per target. `grep -rn "shareWithUserIds"` over backend/src returns only these five sites plus the schema — there is no membership intersection anywhere. Target UUIDs are freely harvestable: routes/friendships.ts:82-96 `GET /api/friendships/search?q=` returns `{ id, name, email }` for up to 20 arbitrary users per call on a substring match of name OR email.
- **Impatto utente:** Any authenticated user creates a board they own (so `assertBoardAccess(id, me, 'WRITE')` at kanban.ts:344 passes trivially), harvests 400 user UUIDs/min from /api/friendships/search, then POSTs `/api/kanban/boards/<my-board>/link-note` with `{noteId: <my note>, shareWithUserIds: [<400 strangers>]}`. Every one of those strangers instantly gets the attacker's note in their 'Shared with me' list and synced into their notes (getAcceptedSharedNotes feeds syncPull), plus an in-app notification and an email — with no invitation, no consent, and no relationship to the attacker. Unsolicited-content / harassment vector at platform scale.
- **Fix:** Make the client list a filter, not a grant: re-derive the allowed set server-side in linking.service.ts and intersect. In both `linkNoteToBoard` (before line 208) and `linkNoteToCard` (before line 99), load the board's participants and filter: `const board2 = await prisma.kanbanBoard.findUnique({ where: { id: boardId }, select: { ownerId: true, shares: { where: { status: 'ACCEPTED' }, select: { userId: true } } } }); const allowed = new Set([board2.ownerId, ...board2.shares.map(s => s.userId)]); const targets = shareWithUserIds.filter(id => allowed.has(id));` then pass `targets`. This is a pure server-side re-derivation with ZERO frontend change: the UI only ever offers the server's own list — checkNoteSharingForBoard (linking.service.ts:40-55) computes `usersWithoutAccess` from exactly `[board.owner, ...board.shares(ACCEPTED)]`, and SharingGapModal.tsx:24/37 only ever posts back a subset of that list. Extract the 4 lines into one `boardParticipantIds(boardId)` helper used by both link functions so the two call sites cannot drift.
- **Correzione del verificatore:** Same defect, but the ceiling is unsolicited-content push at READ on the attacker's own note (plus SMTP amplification), not exposure or takeover of other users' data. High, not critical.

#### `high` · permission · M — Auto-shared notes are written ACCEPTED and the recipient has no way to decline or remove them
`backend/src/services/sharing.service.ts:164` · anche: `backend/src/services/sharing.service.ts`, `frontend/src/features/sharing/SharedWithMePage.tsx`

- **Problema:** `autoShareNoteForBoard` bypasses the PENDING/consent flow every other note-share path uses, and the resulting ACCEPTED share is a one-way door: decline silently no-ops, revoke is owner-only, and the recipient UI offers no removal affordance at all.
- **Evidenza:** sharing.service.ts:161-165 creates `status: 'ACCEPTED'`, while the consented path `shareNote` creates `status: 'PENDING'` (sharing.service.ts:47-56) and requires the sharer to know the target's EMAIL, not a UUID. Recipient escape hatches, all verified closed: (a) `respondToShareById` sharing.service.ts:410 — `if (existing.status !== 'PENDING') return { success: true, status: existing.status };` so a decline on an ACCEPTED share returns success and changes nothing; (b) `revokeNoteShare` sharing.service.ts:114-122 throws `NotFoundError('errors.notes.notFoundOrDenied')` unless `note.userId === ownerId`, so only the sharer can undo it; (c) the recipient's ACCEPTED section in SharedWithMePage.tsx:518-546 renders each item as a bare `<Link>` card with no action buttons — the revoke button at :836-843 lives in the SENT (owner) list and calls the owner-only endpoint. `grep -rn "unshare|leaveShare|removeSelfFrom"` over routes/sharing.ts and sharing.service.ts returns nothing.
- **Impatto utente:** A user who receives an auto-share — whether from finding #1's abuse or from a legitimate board member linking a note they don't care about — is stuck with that note permanently in their Shared-with-me list and synced into their local Dexie notes. Clicking anything they can find does nothing; the only person who can remove it is the sender.
- **Fix:** One-line backend change that fixes the whole class for notes, notebooks and kanban at once — at sharing.service.ts:410 (and the twin guards at :427 and :444) replace `if (existing.status !== 'PENDING')` with `if (existing.status === status)`. That keeps the guard's actual purpose (idempotent double-click, no duplicate notifications) while allowing ACCEPTED → DECLINED. Then surface it: in SharedWithMePage.tsx:518-546 add the decline action already wired for PENDING items to the accepted card, with a new i18n key in BOTH en.json and it.json (`sharing.removeAccess`) and `dark:` variants matching the existing red button at :839.

#### `high` · perf · S — Unbounded share array + awaited SMTP per target inside the request; link-note has no rate limit
`backend/src/routes/kanban.ts:332` · anche: `backend/src/services/sharing.service.ts`, `backend/src/routes/sharing.ts`

- **Problema:** `shareWithUserIds` has no max length, and `autoShareNoteForBoard` loops over it doing an awaited DB upsert + user lookup + notification + blocking SMTP send per element, all inside the HTTP request. The two link-note routes are also the only share-creating endpoints in the codebase with no per-route rate limit.
- **Evidenza:** routes/kanban.ts:332 and :526 both declare `z.array(z.string().uuid()).optional()` — no `.max()`. sharing.service.ts:157-199 is a sequential `for` loop with four awaits per iteration, including `await emailService.sendNotificationEmail(...)` at :187, which awaits `transporter.sendMail(...)` (email.service.ts:47). The whole call is awaited by linkNoteToBoard at linking.service.ts:210 before the response is produced. Contrast with every sibling share endpoint, which is throttled: routes/sharing.ts:70 `POST /notes/:id` `{ rateLimit: { max: 10, timeWindow: '1 minute' } }`, :183 group-share `max: 5`, :41 resend `max: 3 / 5 minutes`. routes/kanban.ts:342-347 and :537-543 carry no `config` at all, leaving only the 600/min global limit from app.ts:121-131.
- **Impatto utente:** One POST with 500 IDs holds a Fastify worker for 500 sequential SMTP round-trips — request timeout for the caller, board link possibly applied with the response lost, and 500 emails sent from the app's SMTP identity in a single request (repeatable ~600×/min against the global limiter). Even in the legitimate case, linking a note on a 30-person board makes the user stare at a spinner for 30 blocking mail sends.
- **Fix:** Two small edits. (1) Bound the input: `z.array(z.string().uuid()).max(50).optional()` at kanban.ts:332 and :526 — matching the maxLength/Zod-cap discipline already applied to kanban inputs in v1.10.4. (2) Stop blocking the request on mail: at sharing.service.ts:187 drop the `await` and keep the existing `.catch(...)` (it is already fire-and-forget-safe — the handler at :198 only logs), so the loop no longer serializes on SMTP. Optionally add `{ config: { rateLimit: { max: 10, timeWindow: '1 minute' } } }` to the two link-note routes so they match routes/sharing.ts:70.

#### `medium` · permission · S — check-note-sharing returns the title and owner of ANY note id, with no note-access check
`backend/src/services/kanban/linking.service.ts:19` · anche: `backend/src/routes/kanban.ts`

- **Problema:** `checkNoteSharingForBoard` fetches the note by id and returns its title and owner id without ever checking that the requesting user owns or can read that note. The routes only assert access to the BOARD, not to the note.
- **Evidenza:** linking.service.ts:19-23 `const note = await prisma.note.findUnique({ where: { id: noteId }, select: { id: true, title: true, userId: true } }); if (!note) throw new NotFoundError(...)` — no `userId` filter, no ownership assertion (contrast with linkNoteToBoard:195 and linkNoteToCard:89, which both enforce `if (note.userId !== actorId) throw ForbiddenError('errors.kanban.onlyOwnerCanLink')`). The values are returned to the caller at :58-59 `noteTitle: note.title, noteOwnerId: note.userId`. Route guards: kanban.ts:336-339 does `assertBoardAccess(id, request.user.id, 'READ')` then passes the query `noteId` straight through; kanban.ts:531-534 does `getCardWithAccess(id, request.user.id, 'READ')` and does the same. Neither touches the note.
- **Impatto utente:** Any user with READ on any board can probe `GET /api/kanban/boards/<own board>/check-note-sharing?noteId=<uuid>` and learn the title and owner of a note they have no access to — including a note whose access was revoked, or one whose id leaked via a chat message, an old share, or a stale Dexie record. It also confirms note existence, distinguishing valid ids from invalid ones (404).
- **Fix:** One line, mirroring the check the link operation already enforces two functions below. After linking.service.ts:23 add `if (note.userId !== requestingUserId) throw new ForbiddenError('errors.kanban.onlyOwnerCanLink');` — the pre-flight check should require exactly what the action it precedes requires, and the picker only ever offers notes the user owns (searchUserNotes filters `userId` at :249), so no legitimate UI flow can hit it. `ForbiddenError` and that i18n key are already imported/used in this file.

#### `low` · ux · S — Note-link sharing check failures are swallowed: picker closes, nothing links, no error shown
`frontend/src/features/kanban/components/CardDetailModal.tsx:175` · anche: `frontend/src/features/kanban/KanbanBoardPage.tsx`

- **Problema:** Both note-link entry points wrap the `checkNoteSharing` call in an empty `catch` that only resets local state. Any failure — 429 from the global limiter, offline, the note deleted server-side, the ForbiddenError proposed in finding #4 — closes the picker and produces no link, no toast, no explanation.
- **Evidenza:** CardDetailModal.tsx:161-177: `setIsNotePickerOpen(false); setPendingNote(note); try { const check = await kanbanService.checkNoteSharing(...) ... } catch { setPendingNote(null); }` — the picker was already closed at :161 before the request, so on failure the user is returned to a modal with no linked note and nothing said. Identical shape at KanbanBoardPage.tsx:251-265: `modals.setIsNoteLinkPickerOpen(false); ... catch { setPendingBoardNote(null); }`. Note linking is server-only (kanbanService.ts:563-572 posts directly, no Dexie/syncQueue fallback), so offline this path fails 100% of the time, silently.
- **Impatto utente:** A user picks a note to link while the connection is flaky or offline. The picker closes as if it worked, the note never appears on the card or board, and no message explains why. They retry, get the same silent nothing, and conclude the feature is broken.
- **Fix:** Add a toast in both catch blocks: `catch { setPendingNote(null); toast.error(t('kanban.noteLink.linkError')); }` with the new key added to BOTH frontend/src/locales/en.json and it.json under `kanban.noteLink` (note: that block currently has 14 keys and does not include `sharingGapDescription` either, used at SharingGapModal.tsx:49). `toast` and `t` are already in scope in KanbanBoardPage.tsx (used at :240 for the cover-upload error).

### gap:share-lifecycle-pending-accept-revoke

#### `high` · permission · S — Re-sharing a kanban board demotes an already-ACCEPTED collaborator back to PENDING (instant access loss)
`backend/src/services/sharing.service.ts:576`

- **Problema:** shareKanbanBoard's upsert unconditionally writes status: 'PENDING' on the update branch. Sharing a board with someone who already accepted silently revokes their access until they re-accept, and there is no other way to change a collaborator's permission (the UI has no permission editor — see ShareBoardModal.tsx:146-168, only an email+permission form).
- **Evidenza:** sharing.service.ts:574-578 `prisma.sharedKanbanBoard.upsert({ where: { boardId_userId }, update: { permission, status: 'PENDING' }, create: {...} })`. Downstream, kanbanPermissions.ts:20 `if (!share || share.status !== 'ACCEPTED') throw new ForbiddenError` and board.service.ts:39 `where: { userId, status: 'ACCEPTED' }` — so the demoted user is instantly locked out of every kanban route and disappears from listBoards.
- **Impatto utente:** Owner wants to upgrade Bob from READ to WRITE, so re-shares the board with Bob's email. Bob, mid-edit, starts getting 403s on every action; KanbanBoardPage.tsx:66-69 then evicts him to /kanban and the board vanishes from his list at the next 30s syncPull. He gets a fresh 'board shared' notification and email and has to accept again. Nothing tells the owner this happened.
- **Fix:** Read the existing row first and keep ACCEPTED: `const existing = await prisma.sharedKanbanBoard.findUnique({ where: { boardId_userId: { boardId, userId: targetUser.id } } });` then `update: { permission, ...(existing?.status === 'ACCEPTED' ? {} : { status: 'PENDING' }) }`. Same one-line class of fix applies to the note/notebook/tasklist upserts (sharing.service.ts:48-51, :291-294) which carry the identical `status: 'PENDING'` reset plus an unresolved TODO comment at :36-39.
- **Correzione del verificatore:** Mechanism is exactly as described; severity is high rather than critical. There is no data loss and the demotion is self-healing: the same call fires a KANBAN_BOARD_SHARED notification (:588-601) and a SHARE_INVITATION email (:606-613), so the collaborator gets a re-accept path within seconds. It is an access outage plus a confusing state, not an unrecoverable one.

#### `medium` · permission · S — Group re-share fans the PENDING demotion across every member and re-emails all of them
`backend/src/routes/sharing.ts:315`

- **Problema:** The group fan-out loops shareKanbanBoard once per member, so a single 'share with group' click on a board whose members already accepted resets ALL of them to PENDING and sends each a new notification + invitation email. The per-member try/catch is correct (one failure does not abort the rest), but the whole loop inherits the demotion bug and there is no idempotency check.
- **Evidenza:** routes/sharing.ts:311-323 `for (const member of group.members) { ... const r = await sharingService.shareKanbanBoard(request.user.id, id, member.user.email, permission); ... }` — each call hits the `update: { permission, status: 'PENDING' }` upsert at sharing.service.ts:576 and then unconditionally runs notificationService.createNotification (:590) and emailService.sendNotificationEmail (:605).
- **Impatto utente:** A team of 10 is working on a shared board. The owner re-shares it to the group to bump the group permission to WRITE. All 10 lose access at once, get an email and a notification, and the board disappears from each of their board lists until every one of them re-accepts.
- **Fix:** Fixing the upsert (previous finding) fixes this loop too — that is the root cause. Optionally skip the notification/email when `existing?.status === 'ACCEPTED'` so a permission bump is silent.
- **Correzione del verificatore:** Accurate, but it is a fan-out of the previous finding's single root cause rather than an independent defect — its own fix section says so. The distinct new content (unconditional re-notification/re-email with no idempotency check at sharing.service.ts:588 and :606) is verified and worth keeping; treat this as an amplification note on finding 1, not a separate high-severity bug.

#### `medium` · ux · M — ShareBoardModal never shows PENDING invites; from the board list it shows no collaborators at all
`frontend/src/features/kanban/KanbanPage.tsx:138`

- **Problema:** ShareBoardModal has dedicated pending-invite UI (a Clock badge, ShareBoardModal.tsx:199-204) that can never render: KanbanBoardPage passes `board.shares` filtered to ACCEPTED, and KanbanPage passes no `sharedWith` prop at all, so the modal falls back to `sharedWith = []` and always says 'shared with no one'.
- **Evidenza:** KanbanPage.tsx:138-143 renders `<ShareBoardModal isOpen boardId boardTitle />` with no `sharedWith`; ShareBoardModal.tsx:26 defaults `sharedWith = []`. KanbanBoardPage.tsx:1105 `sharedWith={board.shares?.filter((s) => s.status === 'ACCEPTED')}`. The list-page data can't help either: board.service.ts:28 and :59 select `shares: { where: { status: 'ACCEPTED' } }` without the `status` field, and db.ts:114 `LocalKanbanBoard.shares` has no `status` at all.
- **Impatto utente:** Owner shares a board from the board-list card, reopens the Share modal, and sees 'shared with no one' — the invite looks lost. So they invite the same person again, which (per the upsert bug) demotes them if they had accepted, and re-emails them. There is also no way to see or cancel a pending kanban invite anywhere in the kanban UI; the only place is the separate /shared 'Sent' page.
- **Fix:** Two small edits: drop the `.filter(...)` at KanbanBoardPage.tsx:1105 (getBoard already returns every share with its status, board.service.ts:169-174), and pass `sharedWith={sharingBoard.shares}` at KanbanPage.tsx:138-143. For the list page to carry pending rows, also remove `where: { status: 'ACCEPTED' }` from the two `shares` selects in board.service.ts:28/:59, add `status: true` to the select, and add `status` to `LocalKanbanBoard.shares` in db.ts:114 (a type-only change, no Dexie version bump).
- **Correzione del verificatore:** The pending UI is not strictly unreachable: ShareBoardModal.tsx:60 appends the freshly-returned share (which carries status PENDING) to local state, so the Clock badge does render for the rest of that modal session right after a share. It vanishes on the next prop sync (the effect at :48-51 resets to the ACCEPTED-filtered prop after the invalidateQueries at :63). The persistent-state claim — reopen the modal and pending invites are invisible, and from KanbanPage no collaborator shows at all — is fully confirmed. Line drift: getBoard's unfiltered shares include is at board.service.ts:177-181, not 169-174.

#### `medium` · dataloss · M — Shared-board sync pull writes over dirty local rows and never prunes — all the guards the owned path has are missing
`frontend/src/features/sync/syncService.ts:467`

- **Problema:** The '/share/kanbans/accepted' pull bulkPuts columns and cards with `syncStatus: 'synced'` without checking dirty rows or pending deletes, and never removes rows the server no longer returns. The owned-board pull immediately above does all three. Since a shared board is fetched by BOTH pulls in the same cycle, the unguarded pass runs last and voids the guarded one.
- **Evidenza:** syncService.ts:459-467 and :473-480 — `db.kanbanColumns.bulkPut(columns)` / `db.kanbanCards.bulkPut(cards)` with `syncStatus: 'synced' as const`, no dirty/pending-delete filters and no delete pass. Compare the owned path: dirty sets at :362-369, pending-delete sets at :349-359, filters at :381/:396, and prune at :389/:411. Both paths run each 30s cycle (useSync.ts:52-55) and listBoards returns shared boards too (board.service.ts:89-105), so every shared board goes through the guarded path and is then overwritten by the unguarded one.
- **Impatto utente:** A collaborator drags a card or edits a title on a shared board while offline (kanbanService.ts:361/:376 mark the row `syncStatus: 'updated'` and queue it). The next syncPull rewrites that card with the stale server copy and flips it back to 'synced'. If the queued push then fails with a 400/422 — which v1.10.4 correctly marks `failed` instead of retrying — the dirty marker is already gone and the edit is silently lost with nothing left to retry. Symmetrically, a card deleted locally (kanbanService.ts:400) reappears as a zombie, and a card deleted or archived by someone else stays in the collaborator's mirror forever.
- **Fix:** Reuse the owned path's reconciliation for the shared branch: compute the same `dirtyCardIds` / `pendingCardDeleteIds` sets and `.filter()` before the bulkPuts, and add the missing prune of local synced rows not present in the response. Lazier root-cause option: extract lines 344-415 into a `reconcileBoardDetails(fullBoard)` helper and call it from both branches, since /share/kanbans/accepted already returns the same columns+cards shape.
- **Correzione del verificatore:** Two of the three impacts hold outright; the silent-data-loss narrative is overstated. syncPush replays `item.data` from the syncQueue, not the Dexie row (syncService.ts:688-697 `await api.put('/kanban/cards/${item.entityId}', item.data)`), so a pending edit still reaches the server even after the row is reverted to 'synced'. What the user actually sees is the edit visibly reverting in the UI for up to a push cycle, plus locally-deleted cards resurrecting as zombies until the queued DELETE lands, plus rows deleted/archived server-side never being pruned from a collaborator's mirror. True permanent loss only materialises in the narrower case the finding names last — the queued push failing 400/422 and being marked `failed` per v1.10.4 with the dirty marker already gone. The missing guards are real and the proposed reconcileBoardDetails extraction is the right fix.

#### `medium` · ux · S — Accept/decline of a share invitation swallows every error — a dead Accept button with no message
`frontend/src/features/sharing/SharedWithMePage.tsx:278`

- **Problema:** handleRespond catches all errors into console.error with no toast, while every sibling handler on the same page (handleResend :253, handleCancelOrRevoke :266) shows one. The backend's most likely failure here is a 404 for an invite that was revoked or already answered.
- **Evidenza:** SharedWithMePage.tsx:271-283 `try { await api.post('/share/respond-id', {...}); fetchData(); } catch (e) { console.error('Error responding', e); } finally { setRespondingId(null); }`. Backend paths that throw: sharing.service.ts:441-442 `if (!existing) throw new NotFoundError('errors.sharing.invitationNotFound')` after a revoke deleted the row (revokeKanbanBoardShare deletes rather than marks, :628-630). The i18n key already exists in both locales (en.json:1525, it.json:1525).
- **Impatto utente:** Owner cancels a kanban invite while the recipient has /shared open. The recipient clicks Accept: the button flickers through its loading state and nothing happens, no message, the invitation card stays on screen. Clicking again repeats forever. Same for any offline click.
- **Fix:** Add `toast.error(t(getApiErrorKey(e) ?? 'common.genericError'))` in the catch at SharedWithMePage.tsx:279 — or minimally `toast.error(t('sharing.cancelFailed'))`-style reuse of an existing key, matching handleCancelOrRevoke two functions above.

#### `medium` · ux · S — Group share reports success while silently discarding per-member failures
`frontend/src/features/kanban/components/ShareBoardModal.tsx:129`

- **Problema:** The backend returns `{ shared, errors }` and deliberately collects per-member failures, but the modal reads only `result.shared` and always shows a success toast. Failures are unreachable to the user.
- **Evidenza:** routes/sharing.ts:325 `return { shared: results.length, errors };` (errors pushed at :320-322). ShareBoardModal.tsx:128-129 `const result = await shareKanbanBoardWithGroup(...); toast.success(t('sharing.shareGroupSuccess', { count: result.shared }));` — `errors` never read. groupService.ts:98-101 returns `res.data` untyped, so TypeScript gives no hint the field exists.
- **Impatto utente:** Owner shares a board with a 10-person group; 7 fail (deleted account, self-share guard, DB hiccup). The toast says 'Shared with group (3 members notified)' with no indication that 7 people got nothing, and there is no per-member state anywhere in the kanban UI to notice it later.
- **Fix:** At ShareBoardModal.tsx:128-131, branch on the payload: `if (result.errors?.length) toast.error(t('sharing.shareGroupPartial', { ok: result.shared, failed: result.errors.length })) else toast.success(...)`, and add `shareGroupPartial` to en.json and it.json next to shareGroupSuccess (en.json:431 / it.json:529). Type the return of shareKanbanBoardWithGroup as `{ shared: number; errors: { userId: string; error: string }[] }` in groupService.ts:98.

#### `medium` · permission · S — Card assigneeId is never validated against board membership
`backend/src/routes/kanban.ts:50`

- **Problema:** updateCard accepts any user id as assignee — no check that the target is the owner or an ACCEPTED share — and the assignment notification path explicitly bypasses the membership filter when a specific user id is passed.
- **Evidenza:** routes/kanban.ts:50 `assigneeId: z.string().nullable().optional()`; card.service.ts:69 `if (data.assigneeId !== undefined) updateData.assigneeId = data.assigneeId;` with no membership query; notifications.ts:31-34 `if (specificUserId && specificUserId !== actorId) { await createNotification(specificUserId, ...); return; }` — returning before the `shares: { where: { status: 'ACCEPTED' } }` lookup at :40. The UI menu does filter correctly (CardContextMenu.tsx:138-149 `if (share.status === 'ACCEPTED')`), so the gate exists only client-side.
- **Impatto utente:** Any WRITE collaborator can POST an arbitrary user id and assign a card to a pending invitee, a revoked ex-member, or a stranger. That person receives a KANBAN_CARD_ASSIGNED notification deep-linking to /kanban?boardId=… which 403s and bounces them (KanbanBoardPage.tsx:66-69), and their name, email and avatar are then served to the whole board through getBoard's assignee select. An invalid id yields a Prisma FK 500 instead of a 400.
- **Fix:** One guard in card.service.ts updateCard, where every caller routes through: when `data.assigneeId` is non-null, `const ok = await prisma.kanbanBoard.findFirst({ where: { id: boardId, OR: [{ ownerId: data.assigneeId }, { shares: { some: { userId: data.assigneeId, status: 'ACCEPTED' } } }] } }); if (!ok) throw new BadRequestError('errors.kanban.assigneeNotMember');` plus the key in both locale files.

#### `medium` · ux · M — A collaborator can never leave a kanban board they accepted
`backend/src/services/sharing.service.ts:624`

- **Problema:** revokeKanbanBoardShare requires the caller to be the board owner, and there is no self-removal route or UI. Decline exists only while the invitation is PENDING; once accepted the board is permanent from the recipient's side.
- **Evidenza:** sharing.service.ts:624-627 `if (board.ownerId !== ownerId) throw new ForbiddenError('errors.common.notTheOwner')` — the only deletion path, reachable solely through routes/sharing.ts:298 `DELETE /kanbans/:id/:userId`. The full route list (routes/sharing.ts:26-360) contains no leave/self-remove endpoint for any type. In the UI, accepted items render as a bare `<Link>` with no actions (SharedWithMePage.tsx:530-545), and syncPull re-adds the board every 30s (syncService.ts:452) so hiding it locally is impossible.
- **Impatto utente:** A user accepts a board invite by mistake, or stays on a project they left. The board sits in their /kanban list forever, contributes to their sync traffic, and keeps generating comment/move notifications and emails (notifications.ts:81 includes every ACCEPTED share). Their only recourse is asking the owner to revoke.
- **Fix:** Allow self-removal in the same function: at sharing.service.ts:624, `if (board.ownerId !== ownerId && targetUserId !== ownerId) throw new ForbiddenError(...)` — the route already carries both ids, so no new endpoint is needed. Then surface it as a 'Leave' button on the accepted card in SharedWithMePage.tsx:530-545 reusing handleCancelOrRevoke.
- **Correzione del verificatore:** Confirmed as stated. One detail: the notification noise claim points at notifications.ts:81 for the ACCEPTED-share recipient set — that select is at notifications.ts:81 in notifyBoardUsersTiered and mirrored at :40 in notifyBoardUsers, so the claim holds for both paths.

#### `low` · bug · S — /share/kanbans/accepted returns archived cards, so collaborators' local mirror is polluted with them
`backend/src/routes/sharing.ts:336`

- **Problema:** The sync endpoint for shared boards selects cards with no `archivedAt` filter, while getBoard — the endpoint the board view uses — filters `archivedAt: null`. The two sources of truth for the same board disagree.
- **Evidenza:** routes/sharing.ts:333-345 `columns: { orderBy: { position: 'asc' }, include: { cards: { orderBy: { position: 'asc' }, include: {...} } } }` — no where clause. board.service.ts:169-172 `cards: { where: { archivedAt: null }, ... }`. syncService.ts:473-480 then bulkPuts whatever comes back into db.kanbanCards.
- **Impatto utente:** On a shared board with auto-archived completed cards, the collaborator's Dexie table accumulates archived cards that the board never shows. cardCount computed at syncService.ts:439 counts them, and kanbanService.ts:303-304 derives a new card's position from `max(position)` over that polluted set. For owned boards the guarded prune removes archived cards; for shared boards nothing ever does.
- **Fix:** Add `where: { archivedAt: null }` to the cards select at routes/sharing.ts:336, matching board.service.ts:171.
- **Correzione del verificatore:** Line drift: the cards select is at routes/sharing.ts:339, not :336, and cardCount is at syncService.ts:437 not :439. One mechanism claim is imprecise: 'for owned boards the guarded prune removes archived cards; for shared boards nothing ever does' — the guarded prune does run for shared boards too (listBoards returns them, so they go through the per-board detail loop), it is just undone by the unguarded shared pass that follows. Net effect on Dexie is as described. Real impact is confined to an inflated cardCount on the board card and an inflated position seed for new cards, neither of which is user-visible damage — low, not medium.

#### `low` · bug · S — DECLINED shares are counted in the tab badge but never rendered
`frontend/src/features/sharing/SharedWithMePage.tsx:380`

- **Problema:** The tab label counts the raw array from /share/kanbans (all statuses) while the panel body only renders PENDING and ACCEPTED, so declined rows inflate the badge over an empty list.
- **Evidenza:** SharedWithMePage.tsx:380 `{t('sidebar.kanban')} ({sharedKanbanBoards.length})` fed by `api.get<SharedKanbanBoard[]>('/share/kanbans')` (:214), which routes/sharing.ts:360-372 returns unfiltered `where: { userId: request.user.id }`. filterItems at :284-287 keeps only PENDING and ACCEPTED, and :417 then renders 'No kanban boards shared with you yet'. Same shape on the notes/notebooks/taskLists counts at :347/:358/:369.
- **Impatto utente:** User declines a kanban invite. The Kanban tab keeps showing '(1)' forever while the panel says nothing is shared — a permanent phantom badge with no way to clear it.
- **Fix:** Count the filtered set: `({filterItems(sharedKanbanBoards).pending.length + filterItems(sharedKanbanBoards).accepted.length})`, or simply filter DECLINED out in fetchData at :219 — one `.filter(x => x.status !== 'DECLINED')` fixes the badge and nothing else reads the declined rows.

#### `low` · ux · S — A declined invitation is a dead end in the owner's Sent view — no action buttons at all
`frontend/src/features/sharing/SharedWithMePage.tsx:818`

- **Problema:** Sent-view action buttons render only for PENDING (resend + cancel) and ACCEPTED (revoke). A DECLINED row shows its red status pill and nothing else, so the owner can neither clear it nor re-invite from there.
- **Evidenza:** SharedWithMePage.tsx:818 `{item.status === 'PENDING' && (<>resend + cancel</>)}` and :836 `{item.status === 'ACCEPTED' && (revoke)}` — no DECLINED branch. The backend would accept the cancel fine (revokeKanbanBoardShare deletes the row regardless of status, sharing.service.ts:628-630), while resend is correctly rejected for non-PENDING (:751 `errors.sharing.onlyPendingResend`).
- **Impatto utente:** Bob declines a board invite. The owner's Sent list keeps a 'Declined' card for it forever with no button, and re-inviting has to be done from the board's share modal — which (per the earlier finding) shows Bob nowhere either.
- **Fix:** Change the cancel button's guard at SharedWithMePage.tsx:826-833 from inside the PENDING block to `item.status !== 'ACCEPTED'`, so DECLINED rows can be cleared with the existing endpoint.

#### `low` · bug · S — Invitation email's highlight parameter is the share id, but the page highlights by board id
`backend/src/services/sharing.service.ts:609`

- **Problema:** The SHARE_INVITATION email deep link carries `highlight=<share.id>` while SharedWithMePage compares the highlight param against the board/note/notebook id, so the invitation is never highlighted or scrolled to. The in-app notification uses the entity id and works.
- **Evidenza:** sharing.service.ts:605-612 passes `shareId: share.id`; email.service.ts:123-125 builds `${FRONTEND_URL}/shared?tab=${data.tab}&highlight=${data.shareId}`. SharedWithMePage.tsx:422-430 getItemData returns `id: kb.board.id`, and the comparison at :475 / :501 is `highlightedId === id`. NotificationItem.tsx:38 by contrast emits `/shared?tab=kanbanBoards&highlight=${data.boardId}` — the id that actually matches. Identical mismatch for notes (:84), notebooks (:327) and tasklists (tasklist-sharing.service.ts:69).
- **Impatto utente:** A user with many shared items clicks 'Open invitation' in the email, lands on /shared with the right tab, and nothing is highlighted or scrolled into view — they have to hunt for the invite in the grid, and the 3s highlight animation the page implements is dead.
- **Fix:** Pass the entity id instead of the share row id: `shareId: boardId` at sharing.service.ts:609 (and the matching lines for the other three types), leaving the email template untouched.
- **Correzione del verificatore:** Line drift only: the highlight comparisons are at SharedWithMePage.tsx:474/477 and :525/528, not :475/:501.

#### `low` · ux · S — Revoked collaborators are never told — every other transition in the state machine notifies
`backend/src/services/sharing.service.ts:628`

- **Problema:** share → notification + email (:590, :605), accept/decline → notification back to the owner (:530-548), but revoke creates nothing for the person losing access. The share row is hard-deleted, so no trace remains on either side.
- **Evidenza:** sharing.service.ts:620-650 revokeKanbanBoardShare: ownership check, `prisma.sharedKanbanBoard.delete`, audit log, reminder cleanup, `return { success: true }` — no createNotification call anywhere in the function.
- **Impatto utente:** The board silently disappears from the ex-collaborator's list at the next 30s syncPull (syncService.ts:441-450 prunes it plus its columns and cards), or, if they have it open, the next query 403s and KanbanBoardPage.tsx:66-69 bounces them to the board list with no message. They cannot tell a revoke from a bug or a deleted board.
- **Fix:** One createNotification call before the return at sharing.service.ts:648, mirroring the shape used at :590, with a new `notifications.kanbanBoardRevoked` key added to en.json and it.json.

### linking-conversions

#### `critical` · permission · S — linkTaskListToBoard never checks the actor can access the task list, then writes to its items
`backend/src/services/kanban/linking.service.ts:376`

- **Problema:** `linkTaskListToBoard` fetches the task list and selects `userId`, but never compares it to the actor and never looks at `SharedTaskList`. The route only asserts WRITE on the *board*. The link then runs a sync loop that UPDATEs `TaskItem` rows of that task list.
- **Evidenza:** linking.service.ts:376-380 `const taskList = await prisma.taskList.findUnique({ where: { id: taskListId }, select: { id: true, title: true, userId: true } }); if (!taskList) throw new NotFoundError(...)` — `taskList.userId` is selected and then never used. Lines 437-442 then do `await prisma.taskItem.update({ where: { id: matchedItem.id }, data: { isChecked: true, checkedByUserId: userId } })`. Contrast with the sibling `linkNoteToBoard` (linking.service.ts:195) `if (note.userId !== actorId) throw new ForbiddenError('errors.kanban.onlyOwnerCanLink')`, and with `createBoardFromTaskList` (backend/src/services/kanban/board.service.ts:288-297) which does check owner-or-ACCEPTED-WRITE-share. `searchUserTaskLists` (linking.service.ts:489-499) restricts the picker to owned or WRITE-shared lists, so the intended invariant exists — it is just not enforced on the write path. backend/src/services/kanban/__tests__/linking.service.test.ts:496-630 has no "throws ForbiddenError if actor cannot access the task list" case, unlike every note-link describe block.
- **Impatto utente:** Any user who owns a board (trivially: creates one) and has a task list UUID they are not allowed to write to — e.g. a list shared with them READ-only, or one they were removed from — POSTs `/api/kanban/boards/:id/link-tasklist` and the server binds it and silently ticks that list's items to `isChecked: true` with `checkedByUserId` set to the attacker. The list owner sees their tasks marked done by someone with no write permission, and `board.taskList.title` is now exposed to every collaborator on the attacker's board.
- **Fix:** Reuse the check that already exists in `createBoardFromTaskList`: after the `findUnique` at linking.service.ts:376, if `taskList.userId !== userId`, load `sharedTaskList` for `(taskListId, userId)` and throw `ForbiddenError('errors.common.accessDenied')` unless it is `ACCEPTED` + `WRITE`. Better: extract that 8-line block from board.service.ts:288-297 into one `assertTaskListWriteAccess(taskListId, userId)` and call it from both.
- **Correzione del verificatore:** The unconditional damage is the unauthorized bind itself, not the item writes: the board is linked, `taskList.title` then ships to every board collaborator via getBoard (board.service.ts:184, unfiltered — see finding #6), and because `KanbanBoard.taskListId` is `@unique` (schema.prisma:469) plus unlink is linker-only (linking.service.ts:468), the victim's list is permanently occupied and cannot be converted or linked by its own owner. The `taskItem.update({ isChecked: true, checkedByUserId })` at linking.service.ts:437-442 is opportunistic, not automatic — it fires only for cards whose title matches an item text case-insensitively AND that sit in an `isCompleted` column, both of which an attacker controls on their own board, so it is reachable but requires knowing an item's text.

#### `high` · dataloss · S — Unlinking a task list leaves taskItemId on every card, so the board keeps mutating the list forever
`backend/src/services/kanban/linking.service.ts:470`

- **Problema:** `unlinkTaskListFromBoard` only nulls `taskListId`/`taskListLinkedById` on the board. The per-card `taskItemId` bindings written at link time survive. `moveCard` gates the task-item write on `card.taskItemId` alone, never on the board still being linked.
- **Evidenza:** linking.service.ts:470-473 `await prisma.kanbanBoard.update({ where: { id: boardId }, data: { taskListId: null, taskListLinkedById: null } })` — no touch of `kanbanCard`. Cards got bound at linking.service.ts:429-434 `if (!card.taskItemId) { await prisma.kanbanCard.update({ where: { id: card.id }, data: { taskItemId: matchedItem.id } }) }` (and at board.service.ts:354/373 for the conversion path). backend/src/services/kanban/card.service.ts:255-268 `if (card.taskItemId) { ... await prisma.taskItem.update({ where: { id: card.taskItemId }, data: { isChecked: true, checkedByUserId: actorId } }) }` — the only guard is `card.taskItemId`.
- **Impatto utente:** User unlinks a task list from a board expecting the two to stop talking. A collaborator later drags a card into the Done column and the supposedly-unlinked task list still gets its item ticked (or un-ticked), with no board shown anywhere in the task list UI to explain it. Second bite: `KanbanCard.taskItemId` is `@unique` (backend/prisma/schema.prisma:509), so re-linking that list to a different board hits P2002 inside the `try` at linking.service.ts:394-451 — the catch swallows it into a log line, the loop aborts, and the remaining cards silently never get bound or synced while the UI reports success.
- **Fix:** In `unlinkTaskListFromBoard`, before/with the board update: `await prisma.kanbanCard.updateMany({ where: { column: { boardId } }, data: { taskItemId: null } })`. One statement, and it kills both the phantom writes and the P2002-on-relink.

#### `high` · bug · M — The card<->taskItem link is one-way: checking, renaming, or deleting a task item never touches the card
`backend/src/services/tasklist.service.ts:309`

- **Problema:** `addTaskItem` creates a card on the linked board, and `moveCard` ticks the task item. But `updateTaskItem` and `deleteTaskItem` contain zero kanban logic, so three of the four task-item operations are silently dropped on the board side.
- **Evidenza:** tasklist.service.ts:225-303 is a 78-line `Auto-add card to linked kanban board (if any)` block inside `addTaskItem`. `updateTaskItem` (tasklist.service.ts:309-347) writes `isChecked`, `text`, `priority`, `dueDate` and then only calls `notifyCollaborators` — no `kanbanCard` reference anywhere in the function. `deleteTaskItem` (tasklist.service.ts:356-372) does `await prisma.taskItem.delete({ where: { id: itemId } })`; because `KanbanCard.taskItemId` is `onDelete: SetNull` (backend/prisma/schema.prisma:509-510) the card survives with `taskItemId = null` and no SSE broadcast. The offline path lands in the same place: frontend/src/features/tasks/taskListService.ts:121-155 queues `UPDATE TASK_ITEM`, which syncService.ts:626 sends to `PUT /tasklists/:id/items/:itemId` → this same `updateTaskItem`.
- **Impatto utente:** User ticks a task in the linked task list; the matching card sits in "To Do" forever. They drag the card to Done instead and the task ticks — so the feature looks bidirectional and they trust it, then get two divergent sources of truth. Renaming a task leaves a card with the stale text (and breaks the title-based re-link at linking.service.ts:425). Deleting a task leaves an orphan card on the board that no longer corresponds to anything and that nobody's SSE stream was told about.
- **Fix:** Smallest real fix: in `updateTaskItem`, after the `prisma.taskItem.update`, when `data.isChecked` changed, look up `kanbanCard.findUnique({ where: { taskItemId: itemId } })` and if found `moveCard(card.id, <first isCompleted column or first non-completed column of that board>, 0, userId, true)` — `moveCard` already broadcasts and already guards against re-writing the item. Mirror the same lookup in `deleteTaskItem` to delete the orphan card. Both reuse existing services; no new plumbing.

#### `high` · ux · S — Every link/unlink mutation has no onError, so 403s and 400s are completely silent clicks
`frontend/src/features/kanban/hooks/useKanbanMutations.ts:187`

- **Problema:** `linkNote`, `unlinkNote`, `linkBoardNote`, `unlinkBoardNote`, `linkTaskList`, `unlinkTaskList` are all declared with only `onSuccess: invalidateBoard`. There is no global mutation error handler either, so every backend rejection on the linking paths produces no toast, no message, no state change.
- **Evidenza:** useKanbanMutations.ts:143-190: each of the six mutations is `useMutation({ mutationFn: ..., onSuccess: invalidateBoard })` with no `onError`. frontend/src/lib/queryClient.ts has only `defaultOptions.queries` — no `MutationCache` with an `onError`. frontend/src/lib/api.ts's response interceptor handles 401 refresh only and re-rejects everything else. Meanwhile the server rejects these calls routinely: `errors.kanban.onlyLinkerCanUnlinkTaskList` (linking.service.ts:468), `onlyLinkerCanUnlinkNote` (linking.service.ts:144, 228), `onlyOwnerCanLink` (linking.service.ts:89, 195), `boardAlreadyLinkedTaskList` (linking.service.ts:374). The UI hands these clicks to users who are guaranteed to hit them: frontend/src/features/kanban/KanbanBoardPage.tsx:789-791 renders the unlink-task-list button for any `!readOnly` collaborator, and line 694 renders the menu item for any `isOwner` — but the server only lets `board.taskListLinkedById === userId` through.
- **Impatto utente:** A WRITE collaborator (or an owner who wasn't the one who linked) clicks Unlink on the task list banner. Nothing happens at all — no error, no spinner resolution, the banner stays. They click again. And again. Same for a user trying to link a note they don't own: the picker closes and the card shows no note, with no explanation.
- **Fix:** Add `onError` to the six mutations pointing at one shared handler that toasts the server's i18n key: `(e) => toast.error(t(e?.response?.data?.message ?? 'common.somethingWentWrong'))`. While there, gate the two unlink affordances on `user?.id === board.taskListLinkedById` the same way the note unlink at KanbanBoardPage.tsx:750 already gates on `board.noteLinkedById`.
- **Correzione del verificatore:** Two of the listed server errors are not reachable through the UI and should be dropped from the case: `onlyOwnerCanLink` cannot fire from the note picker because searchUserNotes only returns notes where `userId` is the actor (linking.service.ts:466-470), and `boardAlreadyLinkedTaskList` cannot fire from the menu because the link entry only renders when `!board.taskListId`. The real, routinely-hit case is the pair of unlink 403s (`onlyLinkerCanUnlinkTaskList`, `onlyLinkerCanUnlinkNote`) plus ConvertTaskListToKanbanModal.tsx:127-130, which swallows a failed linkTaskList into console.error and still shows the success toast.

#### `medium` · permission · S — SSE card:updated broadcasts the linked note's title to board members who cannot read the note
`backend/src/services/kanban/linking.service.ts:116`

- **Problema:** `getBoard` carefully nulls `card.note` for users without note access, but the SSE broadcast path does not. `linkNoteToCard` re-reads the card with `cardWithAssigneeSelect` (which includes the note object) and broadcasts one identical serialized payload to every connection on the board.
- **Evidenza:** linking.service.ts:116-122 `const rawUpdatedCard = await prisma.kanbanCard.findUnique({ where: { id: cardId }, select: cardWithAssigneeSelect }); ... broadcast(boardId, { type: 'card:updated', boardId, card: updatedCard })`. backend/src/services/kanban/helpers.ts:46 `note: { select: { id: true, title: true, userId: true } }`. backend/src/services/kanbanSSE.ts:97-107 `const data = \`data: ${JSON.stringify(event)}\n\n\`; for (const conn of connections.values()) { conn.res.write(data) }` — no per-user filtering, even though `conn.user.id` is right there. The filtering that should apply lives only in backend/src/services/kanban/board.service.ts:197-237 (`Filter note visibility: only show linked note data if requesting user has access`). Worse, the client persists it: frontend/src/features/kanban/hooks/useKanbanRealtime.ts:161-186 `case 'card:updated': ... await db.kanbanCards.put({ ..., note: card.note, ... })`.
- **Impatto utente:** Alice links her private note "Q3 layoffs plan" to a card on a board shared with Bob. The whole point of the `checkNoteSharing` / SharingGapModal flow is to ask Alice whether to grant Bob access first. Bob's open board tab receives the note title over SSE regardless of his answer, renders it, and writes `{id, title, userId}` into Bob's IndexedDB where it survives reloads and offline reads. The CardDetailModal "no access" branch (frontend/src/features/kanban/components/CardDetailModal.tsx:495-498) only kicks in after a refetch.
- **Fix:** Strip the note object from broadcast payloads: in `helpers.ts` add a `cardForBroadcast = transformCard(...)` that deletes `note` (keeping `noteId`/`noteLinkedById`), and use it in `linkNoteToCard`/`unlinkNoteFromCard`/`updateCard`. Clients already invalidate the board query on every card event (useKanbanRealtime.ts:60), so the correctly-filtered title arrives from `getBoard` a moment later — no per-subscriber filtering needed.
- **Correzione del verificatore:** The user-impact half is overstated. The board renders from the react-query getBoard result (useKanbanBoard.ts), not from Dexie, and KanbanCard.tsx:273 reads `card.note?.title` from that filtered data — the leaked title is never painted. It also does not survive: the same handler invalidates the board query (useKanbanRealtime.ts:62), and the refetch re-runs the Dexie hydration, which overwrites rows whose `syncStatus === 'synced'` (useKanbanBoard.ts:54-58) — and the SSE write sets exactly that (useKanbanRealtime.ts:183). Accurate statement: the note title is transmitted over the SSE stream to unauthorized clients (observable in devtools/network, and left in IndexedDB if the follow-up refetch fails), but it is not rendered and is normally overwritten within one refetch. Info disclosure, not a rendered leak.

#### `medium` · permission · S — getBoard access-filters the linked note but not the linked task list, leaking its title to all collaborators
`backend/src/services/kanban/board.service.ts:184`

- **Problema:** `getBoard` includes `taskList: { select: { id, title, userId } }` unconditionally. The visibility-filtering block right below it builds its accessible-id set purely from note ids and only nulls `card.note` and `board.note` — `board.taskList` is never considered.
- **Evidenza:** board.service.ts:184 `taskList: { select: { id: true, title: true, userId: true } }` inside the `findUnique` include. board.service.ts:197-237: `// Filter note visibility: only show linked note data if requesting user has access` — `const noteIds = board.columns.flatMap(...).map(c => c.noteId)...; if (board.noteId) noteIds.push(board.noteId);` then `(card as Record<string, unknown>).note = null` / `(board as Record<string, unknown>).note = null`. No `taskList` branch. It is rendered straight to every viewer: frontend/src/features/kanban/KanbanBoardPage.tsx:778-788 `{board.taskList && ( ... {board.taskList.title} ... )}`.
- **Impatto utente:** An owner links their private task list "Termination checklist - R. Neri" to a board shared with the team. Every ACCEPTED collaborator sees that title pinned in the board header and can click it (navigating to /tasks, where the list correctly isn't listed for them). The note path was deliberately hardened against exactly this; the task list path was missed.
- **Fix:** Extend the existing filter block: after the note pass, if `board.taskListId` and the requester is neither `board.taskList.userId` nor an ACCEPTED `sharedTaskList` row, set `(board as Record<string, unknown>).taskList = null`. The frontend's `{board.taskList && ...}` guard already degrades correctly.

#### `medium` · ux · S — Trashing a task list or note leaves the board permanently linked to an invisible entity
`backend/src/services/tasklist.service.ts:180`

- **Problema:** `deleteTaskList` is a soft delete (`isTrashed: true`) and clears no link. The board keeps `taskListId` set and keeps rendering the trashed list's title, and because the slot is still occupied the board can never be linked to another list — while only the original linker is allowed to unlink it. Notes behave the same way while trashed.
