- **Evidenza:** tasklist.service.ts:180-193 `return prisma.taskList.update({ where: { id }, data: { isTrashed: true } })` — no `kanbanBoard` update. `getBoard`'s include (board.service.ts:184) has no `isTrashed` filter, so the title still ships. `searchUserTaskLists` (linking.service.ts:490 `isTrashed: false`) hides it from the picker, so the user cannot even see what is holding the slot. `linkTaskListToBoard` (linking.service.ts:374) then throws `boardAlreadyLinkedTaskList` for any replacement attempt, and `unlinkTaskListFromBoard` (linking.service.ts:468) only permits `taskListLinkedById`. The conversion flow walks users straight into this: frontend/src/features/tasks/ConvertTaskListToKanbanModal.tsx:167-176 `handleRemoveAndNavigate` calls `taskListService.deleteTaskList(taskList.id)` immediately after `linkTaskList` succeeded at line 125.
- **Impatto utente:** User converts a task list to a board and picks "Remove task list" on the last step — the flow's own recommended path. The new board's header now permanently reads "Linked to: <the list they just deleted>", the link goes to /tasks where it no longer appears, and if the linker was a collaborator who has since left, nobody can clear it. Same shape for a card pointing at a trashed note.
- **Fix:** In `deleteTaskList`, add `await prisma.kanbanBoard.updateMany({ where: { taskListId: id }, data: { taskListId: null, taskListLinkedById: null } })` next to the soft delete (and the matching `kanbanCard.updateMany` from finding #2). For notes, add `isTrashed: false` to the `note` selects at board.service.ts:183 and helpers.ts:46 is not possible in a select — instead null it in the same filter block that handles access.
- **Correzione del verificatore:** 'Permanently' is too strong for the flow's own path: the user who converts is both owner and `taskListLinkedById`, so they can clear it from the board menu (KanbanBoardPage.tsx:694-703) — they just get no hint that they need to, and no indication the title is stale. The unrecoverable variant is narrower: the linker was a WRITE collaborator who has since lost access, in which case nobody satisfies the linker check at linking.service.ts:468. The durable defect is the dangling link plus the stale title after a soft delete.

#### `medium` · bug · S — Task-list conversion picks the last column as "done" instead of the isCompleted column
`frontend/src/features/tasks/ConvertTaskListToKanbanModal.tsx:109`

- **Problema:** `distributeCards` routes checked items by array position (`columns[columns.length - 1]`), while every other part of the system — the link-time sync, `moveCard`, the column UI — keys off the explicit `isCompleted` flag. On any board where the completed column is not last, the two disagree.
- **Evidenza:** ConvertTaskListToKanbanModal.tsx:107-112 `const columns = boardDetail.columns; const todoColumnId = columns[0].id; const doneColumnId = columns[columns.length - 1].id; ... const columnId = item.isChecked ? doneColumnId : todoColumnId;`. The authority everywhere else is the flag: linking.service.ts:400-402 `const completedColumnIds = new Set(columns.filter(c => c.isCompleted).map(c => c.id))`, card.service.ts:256 `const movedIntoCompleted = targetColumn.isCompleted && !card.column.isCompleted`. Users can set the flag on any column: frontend/src/features/kanban/components/KanbanColumn.tsx:198 `onToggleCompletion(column.id, !column.isCompleted)` → KanbanBoardPage.tsx:209 → `PUT` with `isCompleted` (backend/src/routes/kanban.ts:418). The auto-heal at board.service.ts:153-160 only marks the last column when *no* column is flagged, so it does not rescue this case.
- **Impatto utente:** A board laid out To Do / Doing / Done / Archive (with Done flagged completed) receives a converted task list: every already-checked task lands in Archive. Then the link-time sync at linking.service.ts:424 sees those cards are not in a completed column and leaves them alone, and the first time anyone drags one out of Archive, `moveCard` computes `movedOutOfCompleted = false` and the checkbox state stays wrong.
- **Fix:** `const doneColumn = columns.find(c => c.isCompleted) ?? columns[columns.length - 1];` — `isCompleted` is already on the FE column type and hydrated into Dexie (frontend/src/features/kanban/hooks/useKanbanBoard.ts:22).
- **Correzione del verificatore:** Worth noting the blast radius is bounded: boards created by the app default to To Do / In Progress / Done(isCompleted) (board.service.ts:127-131) and the two-column convert board is TODO / DONE(isCompleted) (board.service.ts:325-326), so the last column IS the completed one on every untouched board. The bug only bites boards whose columns were reordered or where a non-last column was flagged.

#### `low` · i18n · S — Hardcoded English in the note-link chip and task-list picker
`frontend/src/features/kanban/components/KanbanBoardLink.tsx:38`

- **Problema:** Two user-facing strings bypass `t()` entirely, in violation of the project rule that all user strings go through i18n in both locales. One of them has an existing key sitting unused.
- **Evidenza:** KanbanBoardLink.tsx:36-38 `: entry.cardTitles.length === 1 ? t('kanban.noteLink.cardLabel', {...}) : `${entry.cardTitles.length} cards (${entry.boardTitle})`;` — the singular branch is translated, the plural branch is not. frontend/src/locales/en.json:891 already defines `kanban.stats.cards: "{{count}} cards"` with it.json:1146 `"{{count}} card"`. Second: frontend/src/features/kanban/components/TaskListLinkPicker.tsx:98 `{tl.itemCount} {tl.itemCount === 1 ? 'item' : 'items'}` — `grep -n "items" frontend/src/locales/en.json` shows no count key for this at all.
- **Impatto utente:** An Italian user opens a note linked to two or more cards and reads "3 cards (Progetti)" instead of Italian; in the task-list picker every row reads "5 items". Both sit next to correctly translated text, so it reads as a half-finished feature.
- **Fix:** KanbanBoardLink.tsx:38 → `` `${t('kanban.stats.cards', { count: entry.cardTitles.length })} (${entry.boardTitle})` `` (key already exists in both locales). TaskListLinkPicker.tsx:98 → add `kanban.linking.itemCount: "{{count}} items"` / `"{{count}} elementi"` to en.json and it.json and call `t('kanban.linking.itemCount', { count: tl.itemCount })`.
- **Correzione del verificatore:** Two untranslated count labels with no functional consequence — this is polish, not degraded UX. Everything around them (titles, placeholders, empty states) is translated, so an Italian user sees two English words in otherwise Italian panels.

#### `low` · ux · S — getLinkedBoardsForNote returns archived cards, producing quick-links to cards that are not on the board
`backend/src/services/kanban/linking.service.ts:292`

- **Problema:** The card query has no `archivedAt: null` filter, but the board view it links into renders only non-archived cards. Archived cards therefore surface as clickable chips in the note editor that navigate to a highlight target that does not exist.
- **Evidenza:** linking.service.ts:292-299 `prisma.kanbanCard.findMany({ where: { noteId }, select: { id: true, title: true, column: {...} } })` — `where` is `{ noteId }` only. `getBoard` excludes them: board.service.ts:171 `cards: { where: { archivedAt: null }, ... }`. Archiving happens automatically — `archiveCompletedCards(boardId)` runs on every `getBoard` (board.service.ts:143). The chip navigates to a dead anchor: frontend/src/features/kanban/components/KanbanBoardLink.tsx:42 `` `/kanban?boardId=${entry.boardId}&highlightCards=${entry.cardIds.join(',')}` ``.
- **Impatto utente:** A user opens a note and sees "Card: Ship v2 (Roadmap)" in the link bar. Clicking it opens the board and nothing highlights, because the card was auto-archived weeks ago. The chip never goes away, so the note permanently advertises a card the user cannot find.
- **Fix:** Add `archivedAt: null` to the `where` at linking.service.ts:293, matching `getBoard`.

### offline-sync

#### `critical` · dataloss · M — Cards created on a board whose CREATE hasn't pushed yet are silently destroyed (stale local column id + 404 drop)
`frontend/src/features/sync/syncService.ts:651` · anche: `frontend/src/features/kanban/kanbanService.ts`, `frontend/src/components/editor/TransformToKanbanModal.tsx`, `backend/src/services/kanbanPermissions.ts`

- **Problema:** `createBoard` invents local uuid column ids; the server generates its own on `POST /kanban/boards`. syncPush reconciles local→server column ids in `db.kanbanColumns` and `db.kanbanCards` but NOT in the payloads already sitting in `syncQueue`. Any `KANBAN_CARD` CREATE queued before the board pushed still carries the dead local `columnId`, so it POSTs to `/kanban/columns/<local-uuid>/cards`, `getColumnWithAccess` throws NotFoundError → 404, and the 404 branch deletes the queue item. The card is gone from the server forever, with no error surfaced.
- **Evidenza:** syncService.ts:651-668 — the reconciliation transaction touches only `db.kanbanColumns` / `db.kanbanCards`; `db.syncQueue` is not even in the transaction scope. syncService.ts:686-687: `const columnId = item.data?.columnId; await api.post(`/kanban/columns/${columnId}/cards`, ...)`. backend/src/services/kanbanPermissions.ts:36 `if (!column) throw new NotFoundError(...)` → utils/errors.ts:9 status 404. syncService.ts:780-784: `if (status === 404 || status === 410) { ...db.syncQueue.delete(item.id) }` — a console.warn, nothing user-visible. kanbanService.ts:335-342 is where the local columnId is frozen into the payload.
- **Impatto utente:** Offline (or after `POST /kanban/boards` hits its 10/min rate limit): create a board, add 10 cards, come back online. The board and its columns appear on the server; all 10 cards vanish. Reachable online too — `TransformToKanbanModal` reads columns straight from Dexie (TransformToKanbanModal.tsx:47-52) and calls `createCard` with those local ids (line 119) while `flushSync()` from createBoard is still in flight (useKanbanMutations.ts:18 is fire-and-forget). The user gets a green "N cards created" toast, opens the board, and it is empty.
- **Fix:** Two parts, both at the root: (1) include `db.syncQueue` in the reconciliation transaction at syncService.ts:651 and, for each localId→serverId pair, rewrite queued items — `data.columnId === localId` → serverId, and `entity === 'KANBAN_COLUMN' && entityId === localId` → serverId (the same staleness kills a column rename queued before the push). (2) At syncService.ts:780, restrict the silent drop to `item.type !== 'CREATE'`; a 404 on a CREATE means the parent is missing, which must be marked `status:'failed'` so SyncStatusIndicator shows it instead of eating the data.
- **Correzione del verificatore:** The mechanism and the severity are right; the online trigger described is the weaker one. The `TransformToKanbanModal` race (TransformToKanbanModal.tsx:41-63, 119) usually loses to `flushSync()` because the wizard needs seconds of clicks while `POST /kanban/boards` takes milliseconds. The reliably reachable triggers are (a) creating a board and cards offline, and (b) the board CREATE failing in the same push run — e.g. the 10/min limit at routes/kanban.ts:124 returning 429 → `recordFailure` backoff — after which the loop continues straight into the card CREATEs, which 404 and are dropped while the board itself later succeeds.

#### `high` · dataloss · S — Column drag-reorder never reaches the server: backend PUT /columns/:id strips `position`
`frontend/src/features/kanban/kanbanService.ts:240` · anche: `frontend/src/features/sync/syncService.ts`, `backend/src/routes/kanban.ts`, `backend/src/services/kanban/column.service.ts`, `frontend/src/features/kanban/hooks/useBoardDnD.ts`

- **Problema:** `reorderColumns` enqueues one `KANBAN_COLUMN` UPDATE per column carrying only `{ position }`. syncPush sends that as `PUT /kanban/columns/:id`, but the backend's `updateColumnSchema` accepts only `{ title, isCompleted }` — Zod silently strips `position` and `updateColumn` builds an empty `updateData`. The write is a 200 no-op, so syncPush deletes the queue item as "successful". The dedicated `PUT /boards/:id/columns/reorder` endpoint that would work is never called from anywhere in the frontend.
- **Evidenza:** kanbanService.ts:240-247 enqueues `data: { position }`; syncService.ts:679-680 `await api.put(`/kanban/columns/${item.entityId}`, item.data)`; backend/src/routes/kanban.ts:416-419 `updateColumnSchema = z.object({ title..., isCompleted... })` (no position) and :424 `updateColumnSchema.parse(request.body)`; backend/src/services/kanban/column.service.ts:37-39 `if (data.title !== undefined) ...; if (data.isCompleted !== undefined) ...` — position is never assigned. `grep -rn "columns/reorder" frontend/src` returns nothing, so routes/kanban.ts:428-434 is dead code.
- **Impatto utente:** User drags a column to a new position. It moves optimistically, `invalidateBoard()` refetches from the server, and the column snaps back to its old place. Nothing is reported as failed — the sync queue reports success. On a shared board, no collaborator ever sees the new order. No e2e test covers this (`grep -n "reorder" frontend/e2e/kanban.spec.ts` → nothing).
- **Fix:** Two lines in the backend, so the payload the queue already produces works offline and online: add `position: z.number().int().min(0).optional()` to `updateColumnSchema` (routes/kanban.ts:416) and `if (data.position !== undefined) updateData.position = data.position;` in column.service.ts:38. The client always sends the full contiguous 0..n-1 ordering (useBoardDnD.ts:206), so replaying the per-column PUTs converges on the correct order. Then delete the unused `/boards/:id/columns/reorder` route.
- **Correzione del verificatore:** Accurate as written; only the severity label is off. Nothing the user authored is destroyed — the column order the user just set is silently discarded and reverts. That is a bug users hit in normal use, not data loss.

#### `high` · dataloss · S — WRITE collaborator's board rename is silently thrown away by the "never push shared boards" guard
`frontend/src/features/sync/syncService.ts:634` · anche: `frontend/src/features/kanban/KanbanBoardPage.tsx`, `frontend/src/features/kanban/kanbanService.ts`, `backend/src/routes/kanban.ts`

- **Problema:** syncPush drops every queued `KANBAN_BOARD` item whose local row has `ownership === 'shared'` — it deletes the queue entry without any API call. But the backend explicitly allows a WRITE share to update a board (`assertBoardAccess(id, user, 'WRITE')`), and the UI explicitly allows it (`readOnly` is false for an ACCEPTED WRITE share). The guard is also useless for the one case it should catch: `deleteBoard` removes the Dexie row *before* the queue item is processed, so `localBoard` is undefined and the DELETE goes out anyway.
- **Evidenza:** syncService.ts:632-638: `const localBoard = await db.kanbanBoards.get(item.entityId); if (localBoard?.ownership === 'shared') { await db.syncQueue.delete(item.id); continue; }`. backend/src/routes/kanban.ts:149-153 `PUT /boards/:id` → `assertBoardAccess(id, request.user.id, 'WRITE')` (only DELETE at :156-161 is owner-only). KanbanBoardPage.tsx:130 `readOnly = !isOwner && !(myShare?.status === 'ACCEPTED' && myShare?.permission === 'WRITE')` and :184-196 `handleStartEditTitle` / `handleSaveTitle` → `mutations.updateBoard.mutate(...)`. kanbanService.ts:152-163 deletes the board row before enqueuing the DELETE.
- **Impatto utente:** A collaborator with WRITE on a shared board renames it. The title changes locally, no error appears, and within 30 s the next syncPull overwrites it back to the old title. The server never saw the rename, so nobody else ever sees it. The user has no way to tell the write was discarded.
- **Fix:** Delete the guard (syncService.ts:632-638). The server is already the authority: a non-owner DELETE returns 403, and syncService.ts:797-806 already marks 403 items `failed` and surfaces them immediately. Keeping a client-side ownership check that only ever fires on the legitimate case (UPDATE) and never on the illegitimate one (DELETE) is strictly worse than no check.

#### `medium` · dataloss · S — Shared-board pull has no dirty guard and no tombstone guard — it clobbers unsynced local edits and resurrects deleted cards
`frontend/src/features/sync/syncService.ts:452` · anche: `backend/src/routes/sharing.ts`, `backend/src/services/kanban/board.service.ts`

- **Problema:** The `/share/kanbans/accepted` block bulkPuts boards, columns and cards unconditionally. Unlike the owned-board block 130 lines above, it computes no `dirtyIds`, no `dirtyCardIds`, and no `pendingCardDeleteIds`, so it overwrites rows the user just edited offline (resetting `syncStatus` to 'synced') and re-inserts rows whose DELETE is still queued. It is also entirely redundant: `GET /kanban/boards` already returns shared boards with `ownership:'shared'` and `permission`, and the detail loop at :342 already fetches every one of them — with the correct guards. So every shared board is pulled twice per cycle and the second, unguarded pass wins.
- **Evidenza:** syncService.ts:429-485 — the transaction opens with no syncQueue table and no dirty query; :452 `db.kanbanBoards.bulkPut(sharedBoardsMapped)`, :467 `db.kanbanColumns.bulkPut(columns)`, :480 `db.kanbanCards.bulkPut(cards)`. Contrast the owned path: :301-302 dirtyIds, :305-309/:349-359 pending-delete sets, :362-368 dirtyColumnIds/dirtyCardIds, :372/:396 filters. backend/src/services/kanban/board.service.ts:89-106 shows `GET /kanban/boards` already returns shared boards with `ownership:'shared'` + `permission`, and syncService.ts:342 iterates `serverBoards` (owned AND shared). Bonus: backend/src/routes/sharing.ts:339-341 has no `where: { archivedAt: null }` on `cards` (board.service.ts:171 does), so this path also writes archived cards into Dexie.
- **Impatto utente:** On a WRITE-shared board, edit a card title while the network is flaky. The 30 s pull lands before the queued push and silently resets the row to the server value plus `syncStatus:'synced'` — the offline-first "dirty rows are protected" invariant is void for every shared board. Delete a card and the same pull re-inserts it into Dexie, inflating the board tile's card count and re-flagging it as a duplicate in TransformToKanbanModal. Archived cards leak into the same views.
- **Fix:** Delete the block (syncService.ts:423-488). The owned pull at :295-421 already covers shared boards end to end with the correct dirty/tombstone handling; removing it kills the clobber, the resurrection, the archived-card leak, and halves the pull traffic in one deletion.
- **Correzione del verificatore:** The block is genuinely unguarded and redundant, but the 'clobbers unsynced local edits' impact is overstated. syncPush sends the queued payload, not the Dexie row, so an offline edit on a shared board still reaches the server — the cost is a stale local mirror and a `syncStatus` that lies, and the resurrected card self-heals on the next owned-path pull (syncService.ts:404-408 removes synced local cards absent from the server). The durable damage is the archived-card leak and the doubled fetch. The proposed fix also needs one more line: deleting the block removes the only path that prunes revoked shared boards, because the owned removal at syncService.ts:316-319 filters out `ownership === 'shared'` — relax that filter in the same change.

#### `medium` · perf · M — syncPull refetches every board's full detail every 30s, and each fetch runs writes on the server
`frontend/src/features/sync/syncService.ts:342` · anche: `frontend/src/hooks/useSync.ts`, `backend/src/services/kanban/board.service.ts`

- **Problema:** After listing boards, syncPull loops over ALL of them (owned + shared) and issues `GET /kanban/boards/:id` for each, unconditionally — no updatedAt comparison, no since-cursor. Worse, that endpoint is not a read: `getBoard` first runs `archiveCompletedCards` (SELECT + UPDATE many) and then auto-sets `isCompleted` on the last column (SELECT + possible UPDATE), plus an archived-count query. So a background pull mutates the database once per board per cycle.
- **Evidenza:** syncService.ts:342-344 `for (const board of serverBoards) { const boardRes = await api.get(`/kanban/boards/${board.id}`) }`. useSync.ts:52-55 runs `syncPull()` on mount and then `setInterval(runSync, 30000)`. backend/src/services/kanban/board.service.ts:143-144 `await archiveCompletedCards(boardId)`, :147-159 the isCompleted auto-set UPDATE, :190-195 the archived count. card.service.ts:374-394 is the SELECT + updateMany.
- **Impatto utente:** A user with 20 boards generates 21 HTTP requests and ~100 SQL statements every 30 s from a single tab — 42 requests/min per tab against the 600/min limit raised in v1.10.3, doubling with a second tab. The whole board payload (every column, every card) is transferred each cycle even when nothing changed.
- **Fix:** Skip the detail fetch when nothing changed: the list response already carries `updatedAt` per board (board.service.ts:81/:99), so compare it against the local `db.kanbanBoards.get(b.id)?.updatedAt` and only `GET /kanban/boards/:id` when it is newer or the board is missing locally. Combined with deleting the redundant shared block, a steady-state pull drops to one request. (The write side effects in `getBoard` deserve moving to the reminder cron, but the skip alone removes the load.)

#### `medium` · offline · M — Kanban board detail is online-only: opening a board offline bounces the user back to the list
`frontend/src/features/kanban/KanbanBoardPage.tsx:66` · anche: `frontend/src/features/kanban/hooks/useKanbanBoard.ts`, `frontend/src/features/kanban/hooks/useKanbanBoards.ts`

- **Problema:** Every kanban write goes through Dexie + syncQueue, but nothing renders from Dexie. `useKanbanBoard` fetches `GET /kanban/boards/:id` with `retry:false` and no Dexie fallback, and the page navigates away whenever that query has no data. With react-query v5's default `networkMode:'online'`, an offline query is `pending`/`paused`, so `isLoading` is false and `board` is undefined — the redirect fires. The offline write layer is therefore unreachable offline; it only ever runs during a blip while the page is already mounted.
- **Evidenza:** useKanbanBoard.ts:10-11 `queryFn: async () => { const board = await getBoard(boardId!) }` (kanbanService.ts:129-132 is a bare `api.get`), :73 `retry: false`. KanbanBoardPage.tsx:65-69 `if (!isLoading && (isError || !board)) navigate('/kanban', { replace: true })`. frontend/package.json:28 `@tanstack/react-query ^5.90.21` (isLoading = isPending && isFetching; a paused query is not fetching). By contrast useKanbanBoards.ts:6-8 reads the board list straight from Dexie, so the list renders fine offline.
- **Impatto utente:** Offline, the user sees all their boards in the list (served from Dexie), taps one, and is thrown straight back to the list with no message — looks like the board was deleted. Everything Dexie holds (columns, cards, positions) is right there and unused.
- **Fix:** Give the queryFn a fallback instead of a new caching layer: catch the network error and assemble the board from `db.kanbanColumns.where('boardId').equals(boardId)` + `db.kanbanCards.where('boardId').equals(boardId)` (the exact shape it already writes on the success path, lines 16-45), and only redirect on a real 404/403 rather than on any missing data.
- **Correzione del verificatore:** One detail is stated more confidently than the code proves: whether react-query reports `isLoading === false` for an offline paused query depends on v5's `networkMode` behavior, and no explicit `networkMode` is configured. The redirect is reachable regardless of that subtlety, because `retry: false` means the first network failure resolves to `isError: true` → `!isLoading && isError` → redirect. The finding's conclusion holds; its stated mechanism (paused/pending) is the less certain of the two routes to it.

#### `medium` · dataloss · S — duplicateCard silently loses the card's priority and puts the copy in a different place than the server does
`frontend/src/features/kanban/kanbanService.ts:477` · anche: `backend/src/routes/kanban.ts`, `backend/src/services/kanban/card.service.ts`

- **Problema:** The queued CREATE carries `priority`, but `createCardSchema` has no `priority` field, so Zod strips it and the backend `createCard` has no priority parameter at all — the copy is created with priority null and no error. The same payload has no position either: the local copy is inserted at `original.position + 1` (with siblings shifted in Dexie), while the server appends it at `maxPos + 1`.
- **Evidenza:** kanbanService.ts:472-479 queues `data: { id, columnId, title, description, priority: card.priority }` and :448 sets `priority: original.priority` locally. backend/src/routes/kanban.ts:41-45 `createCardSchema = z.object({ id, title, description })` and :448-449 `const { id: clientId, title, description } = createCardSchema.parse(...)` → `kanbanService.createCard(id, title, description, request.user.id, clientId)`. backend/src/services/kanban/card.service.ts:10-16 has no priority param; :23-30 computes `position = (maxPos._max.position ?? -1) + 1`, ignoring any client position. kanbanService.ts:459-463 does the local sibling shift that never reaches the server.
- **Impatto utente:** Duplicate a CRITICAL card: the copy shows CRITICAL and sits right under the original. The board refetches, and the copy is at the bottom of the column with no priority. Filtering by priority then misses it. Nothing indicates a field was dropped.
- **Fix:** Add `priority: z.enum([...]).nullable().optional()` to `createCardSchema` and pass it through `card.service.createCard` (the column already accepts it via updateCard) — that closes the whole "queued CREATE field the server can't express" class for cards. For the position, drop the local sibling shift at kanbanService.ts:459-463 and set the copy's position to the column's max + 1 so local and server agree; honoring an arbitrary insert position server-side is a much bigger diff for no user-visible gain.

#### `medium` · bug · S — Bulk move sends every card twice — once through the sync queue, once as a raw fire-and-forget PUT with errors swallowed
`frontend/src/features/kanban/KanbanBoardPage.tsx:360` · anche: `frontend/src/features/kanban/hooks/useBoardDnD.ts`, `frontend/src/features/kanban/kanbanService.ts`

- **Problema:** `handleBulkMove` calls `dnd.handleMoveCardToColumn` for each card — which already runs the full offline path (`mutations.moveCard` → Dexie write → syncQueue → syncPush) — and then fires a second, direct `api.put(.../move?silent=true)` for the same card with `.catch(() => {})`. Each card gets two server moves that both run the reindex transaction, and which one lands first is a race. The direct call touches neither Dexie nor the queue and reports nothing on failure.
- **Evidenza:** KanbanBoardPage.tsx:357-359 `for (const move of moves) dnd.handleMoveCardToColumn(...)`; useBoardDnD.ts:293-297 shows that helper already calls `mutations.moveCard.mutate({ cardId, toColumnId, position: 999 })`; KanbanBoardPage.tsx:360-365 then issues `api.put(`/kanban/cards/${move.cardId}/move?silent=true`, ...).catch(() => {})`. backend/src/services/kanban/card.service.ts:273 shows `silent` only skips the notification — the queued (non-silent) move still fires `notifyBoardUsersTiered` when it happens to win the race, on top of the grouped `bulk-move-notify` at KanbanBoardPage.tsx:368.
- **Impatto utente:** Marquee-select 10 cards and bulk-move them: 20 move requests, 20 reindex transactions, and — depending on which call lands first — either 10 individual push/email "card moved" notifications to every collaborator plus the grouped one, or no activity-log entries and no auto-assign at all. If the direct calls fail (offline, 403), nothing is shown; the optimistic UI holds until the next refetch snaps it back.
- **Fix:** Delete the second loop (KanbanBoardPage.tsx:360-365). `handleMoveCardToColumn` already persists through the queue, which is the offline-correct path. If suppressing per-card notifications is the point, add `silent: true` to the queued move payload in `kanbanService.moveCard` and have syncPush append `?silent=true` — one flag, one request per card.
- **Correzione del verificatore:** The duplicate request and the swallowed errors are real; the 'no activity-log entries and no auto-assign at all' impact is wrong. routes/kanban.ts:456-463 passes `request.user.id` as `actorId` on the silent call too, and card.service.ts:241-249 gates activity logging and auto-assign on `actorId && card.columnId !== toColumnId`, while only the notification block at :254+ is gated on `!skipNotification`. So whichever request lands first logs the activity and auto-assigns; the second sees the card already in the target column and no-ops. The actual cost is doubled writes, a nondeterministic winner, unreported failures, and — when the queued non-silent call wins — a per-card notification on top of the grouped `bulk-move-notify` at :368.

#### `medium` · bug · M — moveCard writes only the moved card's position to Dexie, leaving duplicate positions in the offline mirror
`frontend/src/features/kanban/kanbanService.ts:376` · anche: `frontend/src/features/kanban/hooks/useBoardDnD.ts`, `backend/src/services/kanban/card.service.ts`

- **Problema:** The server's move is insert-and-shift (it increments every card at >= newPosition and closes the gap at the old one). The client writes only `{ columnId, position }` for the moved card and never touches its siblings, so Dexie ends up with two or more cards sharing a position in the same column. The menu-driven move compounds it by sending the sentinel `position: 999`, which the server stores literally.
- **Evidenza:** kanbanService.ts:376 `await db.kanbanCards.update(cardId, { columnId: toColumnId, position, updatedAt: now, syncStatus: 'updated' })` — no sibling update, unlike duplicateCard which does shift siblings at :459-463. backend/src/services/kanban/card.service.ts:202-232 is the shift/close-gap transaction. useBoardDnD.ts:295 `mutations.moveCard.mutate({ cardId, toColumnId: targetColumnId, position: 999 })`; card.service.ts:212 writes that value as-is.
- **Impatto utente:** Low today only because nothing renders card order from Dexie — but the moment the board view gains an offline fallback (see the online-only board finding), the offline board renders in an arbitrary order for any card moved since the last pull. Two cards menu-moved into the same column also land in reverse order relative to the optimistic UI, because the second 999 shifts the first to 1000.
- **Fix:** In `moveCard`, do the same insert-and-shift the server does, inside the existing transaction: decrement siblings after the old position, increment siblings at >= the new position in the target column. ~8 lines, and it makes the Dexie mirror match the server's semantics instead of approximating them. Separately, replace the `999` sentinel in useBoardDnD.ts:295 with the target column's card count.
- **Correzione del verificatore:** The Dexie-duplicate half is latent as stated, but the `999` sentinel half is user-visible today, which the finding buries. Move card A then card B into the same column via the menu (or bulk move): A lands at 999; B's move first runs `updateMany where position >= 999 increment 1`, pushing A to 1000, then writes B at 999. The board refetch orders by position ascending, so B renders above A — the reverse of the optimistic order the user just saw.

#### `low` · bug · S — Zombie-prevention queue scans in syncPull are not filtered by userId
`frontend/src/features/sync/syncService.ts:305`

- **Problema:** The pending-DELETE sets that suppress server rows from being re-inserted are built from the entire syncQueue, across all users, while syncPush deliberately filters the same table by `item.userId === currentUserId`. On a shared device, one account's stuck deletes silently suppress another account's boards, columns and cards from every pull.
- **Evidenza:** syncService.ts:305-309 `db.syncQueue.where('entity').equals('KANBAN_BOARD').and(item => item.type === 'DELETE')` — no userId predicate; same at :349-353 and :355-359. Compare syncService.ts:565-566 in syncPush: `const queue = allQueue.filter(item => item.userId === currentUserId)`. The queue is explicitly user-scoped by design (db.ts:156 `userId: string; // Added for data isolation`).
- **Impatto utente:** User A deletes a board offline and logs out without syncing (the item stays queued). User B logs in on the same device; if B has access to a board with that id (a board shared with both), it is filtered out of every pull and disappears from B's board list until A logs back in and the delete flushes.
- **Fix:** Add `.and(item => item.userId === currentUserId)` to the three pending-delete queries, taking `currentUserId` from `useAuthStore.getState().user?.id` at the top of syncPull — the same source syncPush already uses.
- **Correzione del verificatore:** The mechanism is right; the stated user impact overshoots. The unfiltered tombstone excludes the id from `boardsToPut` (syncService.ts:310-315) but the removal step at :318-321 also skips `pendingBoardDeleteIds`, so a board already cached in Dexie does not disappear from B's list — it silently stops being updated (stale title, stale counts, stale columns/cards). A board B has not cached yet simply never appears. Staleness, not vanishing.

#### `low` · bug · S — A card DELETE that fails permanently keeps the card hidden from Dexie forever
`frontend/src/features/sync/syncService.ts:356`

- **Problema:** `pendingCardDeleteIds` is built from every queued DELETE regardless of `status`. A DELETE marked terminal `failed` (403 on a READ-shared board, or 5 exhausted retries) is never pruned from the queue, so it keeps suppressing that card from every future pull — the card exists on the server but can never re-enter the local mirror.
- **Evidenza:** syncService.ts:355-359 `const pendingCardDeletes = await db.syncQueue.where('entity').equals('KANBAN_CARD').and(item => item.type === 'DELETE').toArray()` — no `status` predicate, while syncPush at :570 skips those same items as terminal (`if (item.status === 'failed') continue`). syncService.ts:797-806 sets `status:'failed'` on 403 and never deletes the row; retryFailedSyncItems (:827-839) only re-arms it on an explicit user click.
- **Impatto utente:** A READ-permission user right-clicks delete on a shared card: it disappears locally, the retry banner shows a permanent failure, and retrying 403s again. The card is now permanently absent from the board tile's count and from the Dexie-backed views (TransformToKanbanModal duplicate detection), for the life of that browser profile.
- **Fix:** Add `&& item.status !== 'failed'` to the three pending-delete predicates — a terminal item is no longer a tombstone, it is a reported failure, so the server row should be allowed back in.
- **Correzione del verificatore:** Two things are overstated. 'Forever' is wrong: useKanbanBoard.ts:52-57 re-inserts any server card missing locally (`if (!local || local.syncStatus === 'synced') put(card)`), so simply opening the board restores it to Dexie — the stale tombstone only suppresses background pulls between board visits. And the READ-share 403 route is largely UI-guarded (KanbanBoardPage.tsx:307 `if (readOnly) return` on the context menu, :130 per-user readOnly), so the reachable trigger in practice is MAX_RETRIES exhaustion on network errors (syncService.ts:534-537), or a WRITE→READ demotion mid-session.

### perf-scale

#### `high` · perf · M — syncPull refetches the FULL payload of every kanban board every 30s (client-side N+1)
`frontend/src/features/sync/syncService.ts:341` · anche: `frontend/src/hooks/useSync.ts`, `backend/src/services/kanban/board.service.ts`, `backend/prisma/schema.prisma`

- **Problema:** syncPull loops over every board returned by GET /kanban/boards and issues a separate GET /kanban/boards/:id for each one, sequentially, with no since/If-None-Match/updatedAt gate. useSync runs syncPull on an unconditional 30s interval, so the entire card corpus of every board the user can see is re-downloaded and re-diffed twice a minute, forever, whether or not anything changed.
- **Evidenza:** syncService.ts:340-344 `// Pull full board details (columns + cards) for each board` / `for (const board of serverBoards) {` / `const boardRes = await api.get<KanbanBoard>('/kanban/boards/${board.id}')` — no conditional. useSync.ts:55 `const intervalId = setInterval(runSync, 30000);` with runSync calling syncPull (useSync.ts:37). Each of those GETs runs board.service.ts:142-250: archiveCompletedCards (findMany columns + updateMany, card.service.ts:378-394), a second findMany columns + conditional update (board.service.ts:148-158), a findUnique with 5 nested include levels (board.service.ts:164-186), a count of archived cards (board.service.ts:190-195), and 2 more findMany for note-visibility filtering (board.service.ts:211-221). The generator block (schema.prisma:4-6) has no relationJoins preview, so Prisma's default 'query' strategy emits one SQL statement per relation level: ~15-18 statements per board GET.
- **Impatto utente:** A user with 20 boards holding 200 cards each: every 30 seconds one open tab fires 21 HTTP requests and ~300 SQL statements and pulls back ~2 MB of card JSON it already has. Fifty concurrent users = ~500 queries/sec of pure no-op churn on a box that also has to serve real traffic — and this is exactly the load shape that produced the v1.10.3 rate-limit spiral. On mobile it burns battery and metered data continuously in the background.
- **Fix:** Make the board's updatedAt a real cache key and gate the detail GET on it. broadcast() (kanbanSSE.ts:102) is already called on every mutating kanban operation and already receives boardId — add one fire-and-forget `prisma.kanbanBoard.update({ where: { id: boardId }, data: { updatedAt: new Date() } }).catch(()=>{})` there so a card/column/comment change bumps the board row. Then in syncService.ts:341 skip the detail fetch when the local copy is current: `const local = await db.kanbanBoards.get(board.id); if (local && local.syncStatus === 'synced' && new Date(local.updatedAt) >= new Date(board.updatedAt)) continue;`. Two small diffs, removes ~95% of the traffic.
- **Correzione del verificatore:** Real and correctly diagnosed, but not critical by the stated calibration: it is pure waste (bandwidth/CPU/battery), not data loss or a permission hole. Also note the proposed cache key does not exist yet — KanbanBoard.updatedAt is never bumped by card/column mutations, so the fix genuinely requires the broadcast()-side touch the finding proposes, not just the client guard.

#### `high` · perf · M — Every SSE event triggers a full board refetch, on top of the targeted Dexie patch it already applied
`frontend/src/features/kanban/hooks/useKanbanRealtime.ts:62` · anche: `backend/src/services/kanbanSSE.ts`, `frontend/src/features/kanban/hooks/useKanbanMutations.ts`

- **Problema:** The SSE handler computes and applies a precise, per-entity patch to Dexie (updateDexieFromSSE, lines 136-238) and then unconditionally invalidates the whole board query anyway, forcing a full GET /kanban/boards/:id. broadcast() echoes events back to the originating connection too, and every mutation already invalidates the board on its own onSuccess — so a single card drag costs the actor two full board refetches and every other viewer one.
- **Evidenza:** useKanbanRealtime.ts:59-62: `updateDexieFromSSE(event, boardId!).catch(() => {});` immediately followed by `queryClient.invalidateQueries({ queryKey: queryKeys.kanban.board(boardId!) });`. kanbanSSE.ts:102-112 `broadcast()` writes to every connection in the board's map with no originator exclusion. useKanbanMutations.ts:109-113 `moveCard` onSuccess also calls `invalidateBoard()` (line 10-14).
- **Impatto utente:** Five people working a 200-card board during a standup: each card move fires 5 full board GETs (~15 SQL statements and ~100 KB each) = ~75 queries and 500 KB per drag. Ten moves in a minute = 750 queries. Cards visibly flicker and re-sort as the refetch lands after the optimistic update, and on a slow link the board momentarily reverts.
- **Fix:** Replace the blanket invalidate with the patch the handler already knows how to compute: `queryClient.setQueryData(queryKeys.kanban.board(boardId!), applyEvent)` mirroring updateDexieFromSSE's switch for card:created/updated/moved/deleted and column:*. Keep invalidateQueries only for comment:added/deleted (which change commentCount on a card the patch doesn't carry). Cheap half-measure if you want it in one line first: add `actorId` to the KanbanEvent union (kanbanSSE.ts:28-42) and early-return in handleEvent when it equals the current user — that alone kills the self-echo refetch.
- **Correzione del verificatore:** The redundancy and self-echo are exactly as described, but the flicker/revert mechanism is misdiagnosed. The mutation is offline-first: kanbanService.moveCard (kanbanService.ts:372-385) only writes Dexie + syncQueue, and flushSync() (useKanbanMutations.ts:17-19) is fire-and-forget. So invalidateBoard() fires while the server still holds the PRE-move state — the refetch can legitimately return stale data and snap the card back until syncPush lands, which is worse than 'a redundant fetch'. Fixing it by patching the cache instead of invalidating (the finding's own proposal) removes both problems.

#### `high` · perf · S — Notification fan-out is a per-recipient N+1 with awaited SMTP inside the mutation request path
`backend/src/services/kanban/notifications.ts:99` · anche: `backend/src/services/kanban/card.service.ts`, `backend/src/services/notification.service.ts`, `backend/src/services/email.service.ts`, `backend/src/routes/kanban.ts`

- **Problema:** notifyBoardUsersTiered loops over recipients issuing one prisma.user.findUnique each, then createNotification (which issues a second findUnique for the same user plus a web-push send), then sendNotificationEmail (which issues a THIRD findUnique on the same user and awaits a real SMTP round trip). The whole loop is awaited by moveCard, which the route awaits before replying.
- **Evidenza:** notifications.ts:104 `const recipient = await prisma.user.findUnique({ where: { id: uid }, ... })` inside `for (const uid of recipientIds)` (line 99); line 113 `await createNotification(...)`; line 122 `await emailService.sendNotificationEmail(...)`. notification.service.ts:28 re-fetches the same user. email.service.ts:78 fetches the user a third time by email to re-check `emailNotificationsEnabled` that notifications.ts:116 already checked, then email.service.ts:47 `await transporter.sendMail(...)`. Call chain: routes/kanban.ts:464 `await kanbanService.moveCard(...)` → card.service.ts:280 `await notifyBoardUsersTiered(...)`. Same shape duplicated in comments-chat.service.ts:209-260.
- **Impatto utente:** Drag a card across columns on a board shared with 10 people who aren't currently viewing it: the PUT /cards/:id/move blocks on ~30 SQL statements plus up to 10 sequential Office365 SMTP sends (~300 ms each). The request takes 3-10 seconds; useBoardDnD holds isMoveInFlight the whole time (useBoardDnD.ts:259-263), which blocks the localColumns↔server resync (useBoardDnD.ts:51), so the board is frozen mid-drag. If SMTP is slow or down, the drag appears to hang.
- **Fix:** Two lines of laziness. (1) Hoist the lookup: replace the per-uid findUnique with one `prisma.user.findMany({ where: { id: { in: [...recipientIds] } }, select: {...} })` before the loop and read from a Map. (2) Get it off the request path: at card.service.ts:280 and comments-chat.service.ts drop the await — `void notifyBoardUsersTiered(...).catch(err => logger.warn(...))`. The SSE broadcast already went out on line 234, so the client has its realtime update; nobody is waiting on the email.
- **Correzione del verificatore:** The N+1 and the awaited SMTP are real; the user-facing impact is wrong. The drag is never blocked: kanbanService.moveCard (kanbanService.ts:372-385) writes only Dexie + syncQueue, so the mutation resolves immediately and useBoardDnD's isMoveInFlight clears at once. The PUT is issued later by syncPush, which walks the queue strictly sequentially (`for (const item of queue)` at syncService.ts:568, every branch awaited) — so one move blocked on 10 sequential Office365 sends stalls EVERY other pending sync item (notes, task lists, other boards) behind it. That is the accurate impact, and it argues for the same fix (batch the user lookup, drop the await on notifyBoardUsersTiered).

#### `high` · perf · S — Board chat polls every 3s even when closed, and its pagination returns the OLDEST 50 messages forever
`frontend/src/features/kanban/hooks/useKanbanChat.ts:12` · anche: `frontend/src/features/kanban/kanbanService.ts`, `backend/src/services/kanban/comments-chat.service.ts`, `backend/src/routes/kanban.ts`, `frontend/src/features/kanban/components/BoardChatSidebar.tsx`

- **Problema:** The chat query has refetchInterval: 3000 and its component is mounted on any owned-or-shared board regardless of whether the panel is open — while SSE already invalidates that exact query key on chat:message, so the poll is pure redundancy. Worse, the client sends no pagination and the server pages ascending from page 1, so past 50 messages the sidebar is permanently pinned to the oldest 50 and never shows anything new.
- **Evidenza:** useKanbanChat.ts:12 `refetchInterval: 3000`. BoardChatSidebar.tsx:34-35 `// Always fetch chat — sound/badge must work even when sidebar is closed` with the hook called unconditionally; KanbanBoardPage.tsx:1072 mounts it whenever `(isOwner || isShared)`. kanbanService.ts:525-528 `api.get('/kanban/boards/${boardId}/chat')` with no params, so routes/kanban.ts:69-72 defaults page=1 limit=50, and comments-chat.service.ts:156-164 uses `orderBy: { createdAt: 'asc' }, skip: (page - 1) * limit, take: limit`. The SSE path that makes the poll redundant is useKanbanRealtime.ts:33-34. The unread badge compares `messages.length` (BoardChatSidebar.tsx:52), which also stops changing once the window is frozen.
- **Impatto utente:** Every open board tab issues 20 chat GETs per minute forever, for data it already gets over SSE. And on any board whose chat has passed 50 messages, users see a year-old conversation, cannot see new messages, and get no sound or unread badge — the feature silently dies at scale.
- **Fix:** Flip the page direction and drop the poll. In comments-chat.service.ts:156-164 use `orderBy: { createdAt: 'desc' }` and return `rows.reverse()` so page 1 is the newest 50; delete `refetchInterval: 3000` from useKanbanChat.ts:12 since useKanbanRealtime.ts:34 already invalidates the key on every chat:message. Add `@@index([boardId, createdAt])` — already present at schema.prisma:563 — so the desc scan is index-served with no sort.
- **Correzione del verificatore:** If anything understated. The ascending-page-1 bug is a silent functional break, not a perf issue: on any board whose chat exceeds 50 messages the sidebar stops showing new messages and the sound/badge stop firing entirely. That is a bug users hit in normal use.

#### `medium` · perf · M — Card move rewrites every row in the column, resetting the 7-day archive clock on all of them
`backend/src/services/kanban/card.service.ts:204` · anche: `backend/prisma/schema.prisma`

- **Problema:** moveCard shifts positions with `position: { gte: newPosition }` and `position: { gt: oldPosition }`, so dropping a card near the top of a column issues an UPDATE against nearly every card in that column. Prisma applies the `@updatedAt` field on updateMany, so all of those rows get a fresh updatedAt — which is the exact column archiveCompletedCards filters on.
- **Evidenza:** card.service.ts:204-207 `await tx.kanbanCard.updateMany({ where: { columnId: toColumnId, position: { gte: newPosition } }, data: { position: { increment: 1 } } })` and lines 217-230 for the source column. KanbanCard declares `updatedAt DateTime @updatedAt` (schema.prisma:517 region). card.service.ts:387-394 archives on `updatedAt: { lte: cutoffDate }` with ARCHIVE_AFTER_DAYS = 7 (line 368). Also note card.service.ts:244-247 issues a separate `update` for assigneeId outside the transaction, a second write round trip. Index support is `@@index([columnId])` only (schema.prisma:522) — no `(columnId, position)` composite, so the shift predicate reads every card of the column.
- **Impatto utente:** Reorder a card at the top of a 200-card DONE column: 200 UPDATEs inside a transaction for a one-card move, and all 200 cards now have updatedAt = now, so nothing in that column is eligible for lazy archiving for another 7 days. Users who tidy a done column regularly will find archiving never fires and the board payload keeps growing — which then feeds finding #1.
- **Fix:** Shift only the rows between the old and new position instead of the whole tail. For a same-column move that is a bounded range: when moving down, `where: { columnId, position: { gt: oldPos, lte: newPos } }, data: { position: { decrement: 1 } }`; when moving up, `{ gte: newPos, lt: oldPos }` with increment. Turns O(column) into O(|delta|), which is 1-2 rows for a normal drag. Add `@@index([columnId, position])` to KanbanCard (schema.prisma:522) so both the shift and the per-column `orderBy: { position: 'asc' }` in board.service.ts:172 are index-served.
- **Correzione del verificatore:** The perf half is overstated: this is ONE UPDATE statement touching N rows, not '200 UPDATEs inside a transaction' — a single set-based update over a few hundred rows is cheap. The load-bearing defect is the side effect: dropping a card at/near the top of a completed column bumps updatedAt on every card at or after that position, so archiveCompletedCards can never fire for them and the DONE column grows unbounded. Fix as proposed (bounded range shift + composite index) but rank it as a correctness/housekeeping bug, not a hot-path perf bug.

#### `medium` · perf · S — Shared boards are pulled twice per sync cycle, and the second copy re-inserts archived cards the first pass just deleted
`backend/src/routes/sharing.ts:339` · anche: `frontend/src/features/sync/syncService.ts`, `backend/src/services/kanban/board.service.ts`

- **Problema:** listBoards already returns shared boards (with ownership:'shared' and permission), so syncPull's detail loop fetches them via /kanban/boards/:id. Then a second block fetches /share/kanbans/accepted, which returns the same boards' full column+card trees again — but without the `archivedAt: null` filter that /kanban/boards/:id applies. The two passes fight: the first deletes local archived cards, the second puts them back, every 30 seconds.
- **Evidenza:** sharing.ts:336-346 includes `columns: { include: { cards: { orderBy: { position: 'asc' }, include: {...} } } }` with NO archivedAt filter, versus board.service.ts:170-173 `cards: { where: { archivedAt: null }, ... }`. board.service.ts:89-106 shows listBoards already emits shared boards with `ownership: 'shared'` and `permission`. syncService.ts:341 iterates ALL serverBoards (owned + shared) doing the detail GET; syncService.ts:405-411 then deletes local synced cards not present in that non-archived payload; the shared block at syncService.ts:426-481 runs immediately after and bulkPuts the archived ones straight back (line 481).
- **Impatto utente:** A shared board with 300 archived cards: every 30 seconds IndexedDB does 300 deletes followed by 300 inserts, forever, and the shared board's entire card tree crosses the wire twice. On mobile this is a visible battery and storage-write drain, and the local kanbanCards table permanently disagrees with what /kanban/boards/:id says exists.
- **Fix:** Delete the whole 'Shared Kanban Boards Pull' block (syncService.ts:423-488) — /kanban/boards + the detail loop already cover shared boards completely — and adjust the ownership filter at syncService.ts:319-321 to stop excluding shared boards from the stale-delete pass. That is a ~65-line net deletion that removes the duplicate fetch and the archived-card leak in one go. If you'd rather keep the endpoint, the one-line version is adding `where: { archivedAt: null }` to sharing.ts:339.
- **Correzione del verificatore:** Correct diagnosis, but 'high' overstates the observed harm: nothing in the board view reads those Dexie rows (useKanbanBoard renders server data), so the visible consequence is limited to duplicated traffic, constant IndexedDB write churn, and stale archived cards leaking into the few Dexie readers (e.g. TransformToKanbanModal.tsx:55). No server-side data loss, no permission issue.

#### `medium` · perf · S — Board hydration does a sequential get+put per card inside one Dexie transaction, on every board fetch
`frontend/src/features/kanban/hooks/useKanbanBoard.ts:48` · anche: `frontend/src/features/sync/syncService.ts`

- **Problema:** The query function walks every card one at a time — await get(id), then await put(card) — inside a single rw transaction, instead of the bulk pattern the sync engine already uses two files over. This runs on mount and on every one of the many board invalidations (mutations + SSE, findings #2 and #1).
- **Evidenza:** useKanbanBoard.ts:48-59: `for (const card of serverCards) { const local = await db.kanbanCards.get(card.id); if (!local || local.syncStatus === 'synced') { await db.kanbanCards.put(card); } }` (and the same shape for columns at lines 48-53). The fast equivalent already exists: syncService.ts:394-413 builds a dirty-id Set from one indexed query and then calls `db.kanbanCards.bulkPut(allServerCards)` once.
- **Impatto utente:** Opening a 200-card board issues up to 400 sequential IndexedDB round trips before the promise settles; on a mid-range Android that is several hundred ms of blocked main thread per board fetch, repeated on every card move because the SSE echo re-invalidates the query.
- **Fix:** Copy the pattern already in syncService.ts:405-413: one `const dirty = new Set((await db.kanbanCards.where('boardId').equals(board.id).filter(c => c.syncStatus !== 'synced').toArray()).map(c => c.id))`, then `await db.kanbanCards.bulkPut(serverCards.filter(c => !dirty.has(c.id)))`. Same for columns. Two round trips instead of four hundred.
- **Correzione del verificatore:** Mechanism slightly off: IndexedDB work is not 'blocked main thread' — these are async round trips to the IDB thread that add latency and hold the rw transaction (and its table locks) open longer, not CPU stalls. Real inefficiency with a trivially lazy fix, but the user-visible effect is added latency on board open, not a frozen UI.

#### `medium` · perf · S — memo() on KanbanCard is dead: KanbanColumn is unmemoized and every prop feeding the cards is a fresh identity
`frontend/src/features/kanban/components/KanbanColumn.tsx:30` · anche: `frontend/src/features/kanban/KanbanBoardPage.tsx`, `frontend/src/features/kanban/components/KanbanCard.tsx`

- **Problema:** KanbanCard is wrapped in memo, but nothing upstream preserves prop identity: KanbanColumn is a plain function component, it rebuilds the sorted card array and the SortableContext id array on every render, and the page passes an inline arrow as onCardSelect. So any state change anywhere on the page re-renders every card.
- **Evidenza:** KanbanColumn.tsx:30 `export default function KanbanColumn({` — no memo. Line 71 `const sortedCards = [...column.cards].sort((a, b) => a.position - b.position);` and line 254 `items={sortedCards.map((c) => c.id)}` — both new arrays every render. KanbanBoardPage.tsx:873 and :915 `onCardSelect={(cardId) => setSelectedCardId(cardId)}` — new closure every render. KanbanCard.tsx:36 `export default memo(function KanbanCard(...)` is therefore never able to bail out; each instance re-runs useTranslation, useIsMobile, useSortable and getDueDateStatus (KanbanCard.tsx:29-34, 37-54, 61).
- **Impatto utente:** On a 200-card board, every SSE presence:update (useKanbanRealtime.ts:32), every 2s highlight-timer expiry (useKanbanRealtime.ts:47-55), every keystroke in the filter bar, and every card click re-renders all 200 cards plus 200 useSortable subscriptions. Drag-and-drop stutters and the filter input lags behind typing.
- **Fix:** Three small edits: wrap KanbanColumn's export in React.memo; wrap its two derived arrays in useMemo keyed on column.cards; and hoist the page's onCardSelect into `const handleCardSelect = useCallback((id: string) => setSelectedCardId(id), [])` used at both call sites. That restores the memo firewall so a presence tick only re-renders the header.
- ⚠️ *non verificato in contraddittorio*

#### `medium` · perf · S — ganttExport does two full date passes per card on the main thread with no loading state
`frontend/src/features/kanban/ganttExport.ts:353` · anche: `frontend/src/features/kanban/KanbanBoardPage.tsx`

- **Problema:** createGanttSheet walks the 120-day window twice for every card — once to build the row values and once to style the cells — calling isWithinInterval on both passes and isDateToday on every single day cell (each of which allocates a fresh Date). The whole export is synchronous ExcelJS work followed by a synchronous writeBuffer, and the button that starts it has no disabled or spinner state.
- **Evidenza:** ganttExport.ts:230 `const displayDays = days.length > 120 ? days.slice(days.length - 120) : days;`. First pass ganttExport.ts:353-360 `for (let di = 0; di < displayDays.length; di++) { ... isWithinInterval(day, {...}) }`. Second pass ganttExport.ts:395-437 repeats the same isWithinInterval at line 400-402 plus `if (isDateToday(day))` at line 430 for every cell. Both nested inside `for (let rowIdx = 0; rowIdx < sorted.length; rowIdx++)` (line 339). Caller: KanbanBoardPage.tsx:393-397 `async function handleExportGantt() { const { exportGanttXLSX } = await import('./ganttExport'); await exportGanttXLSX(board, t, i18n.language); }` — no isExporting state anywhere.
- **Impatto utente:** A 200-card board with due dates produces 24,000 styled day cells: ~48,000 isWithinInterval calls, 24,000 isDateToday allocations, 24,000 ExcelJS cell objects, then the zip write. The tab freezes for several seconds with zero feedback — no spinner, no disabled button — so users click Export again and start a second run on top of the first.
- **Fix:** The day loop doesn't need date math at all: compute `const todayIdx = displayDays.findIndex(isDateToday)` once before the row loop, and per card compute `startIdx`/`endIdx` into displayDays with differenceInDays (already imported at line 3), then fill by index comparison instead of interval-testing each cell. Merge the two passes into one while you're there. Then add an `isExporting` state on the button in KanbanBoardPage.tsx:393 so the click is disabled and labelled while it runs.
- **Correzione del verificatore:** The two-pass date math is the smaller half; the concrete user-facing defect is the missing disabled/pending state on a multi-second synchronous action, which is the part worth fixing first. Export is also a deliberate, infrequent action, so this is degraded UX rather than a hot-path perf problem.

#### `low` · perf · S — useIsMobile attaches one unthrottled window resize listener per component instance, and KanbanCard calls it
`frontend/src/hooks/useIsMobile.ts:6` · anche: `frontend/src/features/kanban/components/KanbanCard.tsx`

- **Problema:** The hook registers its own window 'resize' listener in every instance and each handler reads window.innerWidth (a forced layout read) and calls setState. It is used inside KanbanCard, so the listener count scales with the number of cards rendered — and with 26 call sites across the app the same multiplication happens elsewhere.
- **Evidenza:** useIsMobile.ts:6-14 `useEffect(() => { const checkMobile = () => { setIsMobile(window.innerWidth < 768); }; checkMobile(); window.addEventListener('resize', checkMobile); ... }, [])`. KanbanCard.tsx:38 `const isMobile = useIsMobile();` inside the per-card component. `grep -rn useIsMobile frontend/src` returns 53 hits across 26 files.
- **Impatto utente:** A 200-card board holds 200 resize listeners. Dragging the browser window fires resize at ~60 Hz, so ~12,000 innerWidth reads and up to 12,000 setState calls per second; on Android, rotating the device or the keyboard opening/closing triggers the same storm and the board visibly locks up for a beat.
- **Fix:** One module-level source of truth, which fixes all 26 call sites at once: `const mq = window.matchMedia('(max-width: 767px)'); const sub = (cb) => { mq.addEventListener('change', cb); return () => mq.removeEventListener('change', cb); }; export const useIsMobile = () => useSyncExternalStore(sub, () => mq.matches, () => false);`. Same signature, one listener for the whole app, no innerWidth layout read.
- **Correzione del verificatore:** Substantially overstated. React bails out of re-rendering when setState is called with an identical value (Object.is), so a resize storm produces at most one render per component on an actual breakpoint crossing — not '12,000 setState calls per second' and no board lockup. The residual cost is N forced innerWidth reads per resize event, during an interaction (window drag / rotation) that is rare and already janky. Real cleanup with a one-line matchMedia + useSyncExternalStore fix, but polish-tier.

#### `low` · perf · S — getUserKanbanReminders returns every reminder ever created for the user — no isDone filter, no limit
`backend/src/services/kanbanReminder.service.ts:129` · anche: `backend/src/routes/kanban.ts`, `backend/prisma/schema.prisma`

- **Problema:** Reminders are created for every board participant times every card with a dueDate, and again in bulk whenever someone joins a board. The read endpoint fetches all of them for a user with no pagination and no isDone filter, expanding each row through card -> column -> board. The (userId, isDone) index exists but nothing ever uses the isDone half.
- **Evidenza:** kanbanReminder.service.ts:129-150 `prisma.kanbanReminder.findMany({ where: { userId }, select: { ..., card: { select: { title: true, column: { select: { title: true, board: { select: { title: true, avatarUrl: true } } } } } } }, orderBy: { dueDate: 'asc' } })` — no take, no isDone. Row creation: kanbanReminder.service.ts:35-43 one per board participant per dated card, plus the catch-up bulk at lines 101-119 for every new participant. Route: routes/kanban.ts:90-104 returns the whole array mapped. Index available but unexploited: schema.prisma:617 `@@index([userId, isDone])`.
- **Impatto utente:** A user in 10 boards with 200 dated cards each accumulates ~2,000 reminder rows, all serialised on every visit to the reminders page, including everything already ticked done years ago. The payload and the join fan-out grow monotonically and nothing ever prunes them.
- **Fix:** Use the index that already exists: `where: { userId, isDone: false }` plus `take: 200`. One line, and it turns the index at schema.prisma:617 from decorative into load-bearing.
- **Correzione del verificatore:** The missing `take` is the valid part; the proposed `isDone: false` fix is WRONG and would break the UI. RemindersPage.tsx:113 builds a Done section from these rows (`const done = unified.filter((r) => r.isDone)`) and renders them with a checked icon and line-through at :144 and :164 — filtering isDone server-side makes ticked kanban reminders vanish from the page and become un-untickable. Fix is a bounded `take` (and/or a dueDate floor), not the isDone predicate. Also the query is user-triggered on page visit (useKanbanReminders.ts:17-25, no polling), so the cost is per-visit, not continuous.

#### `low` · perf · S — KanbanComment lacks the (cardId, createdAt) composite index its sibling models both have
`backend/prisma/schema.prisma:536` · anche: `backend/src/services/kanban/comments-chat.service.ts`

- **Problema:** getComments filters by cardId and orders by createdAt with offset pagination, but KanbanComment only indexes cardId — so Postgres fetches every comment on the card and sorts in memory before applying skip/take. KanbanBoardChat and KanbanCardActivity, which run the identical query shape, both declare the composite. The invariant is applied inconsistently.
- **Evidenza:** Query: comments-chat.service.ts:22-30 `prisma.kanbanComment.findMany({ where: { cardId }, orderBy: { createdAt: 'asc' }, skip: (page - 1) * limit, take: limit, ... })`. Index available: schema.prisma:536 `@@index([cardId])` only. Compare schema.prisma:563 `@@index([boardId, createdAt])` on KanbanBoardChat and schema.prisma:600 `@@index([cardId, createdAt])` on KanbanCardActivity — same access pattern, composite present.
- **Impatto utente:** A heavily discussed card with several hundred comments makes every open of the card detail modal do a full fetch-and-sort of that card's comment set to hand back 50 rows; deep pages re-scan from the start each time. Small today, but it is the one model in the group that will degrade first.
- **Fix:** Add `@@index([cardId, createdAt])` to KanbanComment (schema.prisma:536) and generate the migration. One line, matches the two sibling models.

### permissions-sharing

#### `critical` · permission · S — moveCard never checks that toColumnId belongs to the card's board — cross-board card theft
`backend/src/services/kanban/card.service.ts:194`

- **Problema:** The route guards the SOURCE card (getCardWithAccess(id, user, 'WRITE') in routes/kanban.ts:461) but the service resolves toColumnId with a bare findUnique and never compares targetColumn.boardId with the card's boardId. Any column id in the whole database is accepted as a move target.
- **Evidenza:** card.service.ts:194-200: `const targetColumn = await prisma.kanbanColumn.findUnique({ where: { id: toColumnId }, select: { boardId, title, position, isCompleted } }); if (!targetColumn) throw new NotFoundError(...); const boardId = card.column.boardId;` — targetColumn.boardId is read but only used for title/isCompleted; there is no `targetColumn.boardId === boardId` guard anywhere in the function. The transaction at line 210 then does `tx.kanbanCard.update({ where: { id: cardId }, data: { columnId: toColumnId } })`, and the SSE broadcast at line 234 goes to the SOURCE board only.
- **Impatto utente:** Mallory has WRITE on Alice's shared board and owns her own board. She calls PUT /api/kanban/cards/<alice-card>/move with toColumnId = a column of her own board. Alice's card — title, description, comments, activity history — leaves Alice's board permanently and appears on Mallory's board. Alice sees the card vanish (card:moved event pointing at a column that isn't on her board) with no way to get it back and no audit trail on her side. The same call also lets a WRITE member push a card into any board id they can guess.
- **Fix:** One guard after the target lookup in moveCard: `if (targetColumn.boardId !== card.column.boardId) throw new ForbiddenError('errors.common.accessDenied');` (ForbiddenError is already imported in the sibling services; add it to the existing import from '../../utils/errors'). Root fix — every caller (route move, bulk move, sync push move) funnels through moveCard.
- **Correzione del verificatore:** Accurate as written. Two refinements: (a) it is not a disclosure escalation — the attacker already had READ on that card as a board member; the real damage is irreversible relocation (the owner loses the card, its comments and activity, and cannot reach the destination board), and (b) 'push a card into any board id they can guess' is impractical (uuid), so the realistic vector is the attacker's own board or a board they were previously shared. Still data loss on the victim's side, so critical stands.

#### `high` · permission · S — linkTaskListToBoard accepts any taskListId with no ownership check and then writes to the victim's task items
`backend/src/services/kanban/linking.service.ts:376`

- **Problema:** POST /kanban/boards/:id/link-tasklist only asserts board WRITE. The service then loads the task list by id with no ownership or share check and, in the post-link sync block, updates TaskItem rows of that list (isChecked: true, checkedByUserId: actor). createBoardFromTaskList does perform this check (board.service.ts:288-298), so the invariant is enforced in one path and missing in the other.
- **Evidenza:** linking.service.ts:376-380 `const taskList = await prisma.taskList.findUnique({ where: { id: taskListId }, select: { id, title, userId } }); if (!taskList) throw new NotFoundError(...)` — taskList.userId is selected and never compared to `userId`. Then lines 437-441: `await prisma.taskItem.update({ where: { id: matchedItem.id }, data: { isChecked: true, checkedByUserId: userId } })`. Contrast board.service.ts:289-298 which checks owner OR ACCEPTED+WRITE SharedTaskList.
- **Impatto utente:** Bob has WRITE on Alice's board, which is linked to Alice's private task list; getBoard hands Bob `taskList: { id, title, userId }` (board.service.ts:184, unfiltered). Bob copies that id and POSTs it to link-tasklist on his OWN board. The link succeeds; the title-matching sync immediately ticks off Alice's task items (checkedByUserId = Bob) for any of Bob's cards sitting in a completed column, and every later card move into Done keeps flipping Alice's checkboxes. Alice never shared the list with anyone.
- **Fix:** Copy the check that already exists in createBoardFromTaskList into linkTaskListToBoard right after the task list is loaded: if `taskList.userId !== userId`, require an ACCEPTED SharedTaskList with permission WRITE, else throw ForbiddenError('errors.common.accessDenied').
- **Correzione del verificatore:** Real, but not reachable through the UI: the link entry point is owner-gated (KanbanBoardPage.tsx:692 `{isOwner && (`) and TaskListLinkPicker is fed by searchUserTaskLists (linking.service.ts:479-495), which only returns lists the user owns or has ACCEPTED+WRITE on. This is an API-level authorization gap requiring a crafted POST, not something a collaborator stumbles into. The write to another user's TaskItem rows is nonetheless real and unauthorized.

#### `high` · permission · S — SSE broadcast ships the linked note's title to board members who have no access to that note
`backend/src/services/kanbanSSE.ts:102`

- **Problema:** getBoard carefully nulls out `card.note` for users without access to the linked note (board.service.ts:197-237), but the realtime path has no such filter: broadcast() writes one identical JSON payload to every connection on the board, and the card payload embeds the note object.
- **Evidenza:** helpers.ts:46 `note: { select: { id: true, title: true, userId: true } }` inside cardWithAssigneeSelect; linking.service.ts:116-122 and card.service.ts:76-85 both re-select the card with that select and call `broadcast(boardId, { type: 'card:updated', boardId, card })`; kanbanSSE.ts:102-113 `const data = JSON.stringify(event); for (const conn of connections.values()) conn.res.write(data)` — no per-connection filtering even though each BoardConnection carries conn.user.id. Frontend useKanbanRealtime.ts:166-184 then persists `note: card.note` into IndexedDB.
- **Impatto utente:** Alice links her private note 'Q3 layoffs' to a card and deliberately UNCHECKS Bob in the SharingGapModal so he gets no access. The link itself broadcasts card:updated containing `note: { id, title }` to Bob's open board tab; so does every later edit of that card. Bob reads the note title in the network tab or in IndexedDB, and it is written to his local Dexie card row — defeating the entire sharing-gap flow the modal exists to run.
- **Fix:** Strip the relation in the one broadcast chokepoint rather than at each call site: in kanbanSSE.ts broadcast(), before serialising, `if ((event.type === 'card:created' || event.type === 'card:updated') && event.card) event = { ...event, card: { ...event.card, note: undefined } }`. Clients already invalidate the board query on every SSE event, so the access-filtered note comes back from GET /kanban/boards/:id.
- **Correzione del verificatore:** Accurate. Scope worth stating precisely: the leak is note id, title and ownerId — not note content — and only on card:created/card:updated (card:moved, board:updated and column events carry none).

#### `medium` · permission · S — reorderColumns updates columns by raw id with no board scoping
`backend/src/services/kanban/column.service.ts:54`

- **Problema:** PUT /kanban/boards/:id/columns/reorder asserts WRITE on :id, then the service writes positions for whatever column ids the body contains, with no check that those columns belong to that board. Same trust-the-client-id class as moveCard.
- **Evidenza:** column.service.ts:58-65 `prisma.$transaction(items.map((item) => prisma.kanbanColumn.update({ where: { id: item.id }, data: { position: item.position } })))` — boardId is a parameter of the function (line 55) but is used only for the broadcast at line 67, never in the where clause. The Zod schema (routes/kanban.ts:37-39) validates shape only.
- **Impatto utente:** A user with WRITE on any board can POST arbitrary column ids from a board they were never shared and scramble that board's column order. The victim's board reorders itself with no SSE event (the broadcast goes to the attacker's board id) and no audit record.
- **Fix:** Scope the write: replace `prisma.kanbanColumn.update({ where: { id: item.id }, ... })` with `prisma.kanbanColumn.updateMany({ where: { id: item.id, boardId }, data: { position: item.position } })` — boardId is already in scope.
- **Correzione del verificatore:** Real but the exploit needs the victim's column uuids, so in practice only an ex-collaborator with stale Dexie rows (or a client bug crossing boards) can trigger it, and the blast radius is column ordering only — no content read or write. Medium is the right ceiling.

#### `medium` · dataloss · S — WRITE collaborator's board rename is silently discarded by syncPush
`frontend/src/features/sync/syncService.ts:631`

- **Problema:** The UI grants board title/description editing to any non-readOnly user (i.e. owner OR ACCEPTED+WRITE share), but syncPush unconditionally drops KANBAN_BOARD queue items for boards whose local ownership is 'shared'. Column and card items have no such guard, so only board-level edits vanish.
- **Evidenza:** syncService.ts:631-638 `if (localBoard?.ownership === 'shared') { await db.syncQueue.delete(item.id); clearFailure(item.id); continue; }` — the item is deleted, not failed, so nothing surfaces in SyncStatusIndicator. kanbanService.ts:141-149 enqueues the UPDATE for every board regardless of ownership. KanbanBoardPage.tsx:184-196 gates title editing on `readOnly` only, and readOnly is false for an ACCEPTED WRITE share (line 130). Compare syncService.ts:675-701: KANBAN_COLUMN/KANBAN_CARD have no ownership guard and push fine.
- **Impatto utente:** Bob has WRITE on Alice's board. He double-clicks the board title, renames it, sees the new name, and everything looks saved (no error, no failed-sync banner). The queue item is deleted without ever hitting the API; on the next syncPull the shared-board pass overwrites the row and the old title snaps back. His edit is gone with zero feedback — while his card and column edits on the same board do save.
- **Fix:** Make the sync layer agree with the UI: allow the push for shared boards with permission WRITE (`if (localBoard?.ownership === 'shared' && localBoard.permission !== 'WRITE')` keep the drop) — the backend already enforces the real rule via assertBoardAccess(id, user, 'WRITE') and a 403 is now marked 'failed' and surfaced (syncService.ts:797-806). Alternatively gate the title editor on isOwner, but that removes a capability the server grants.
- **Correzione del verificatore:** One timing detail is wrong: the old title reappears immediately, not 'on the next syncPull' — updateBoard's onSuccess calls invalidateBoard (useKanbanMutations.ts:38-40) and useKanbanBoard refetches GET /kanban/boards/:id, which still returns the server value. The loss is still silent (the item is deleted from the queue, never marked 'failed', so SyncStatusIndicator shows nothing).

#### `medium` · permission · S — Only-the-linker unlink rule strands the board owner, and the button is shown to users who can't use it
`backend/src/services/kanban/linking.service.ts:468`

- **Problema:** unlinkTaskListFromBoard and unlinkNoteFromBoard/unlinkNoteFromCard allow only the user who created the link. Since linking requires just board WRITE (task list) or note ownership (note), a collaborator can attach a link to someone else's board that the board owner can never remove — and re-linking is blocked because boardAlreadyLinked* throws while noteId/taskListId is set.
- **Evidenza:** linking.service.ts:468 `if (board.taskListLinkedById !== userId) throw new ForbiddenError('errors.kanban.onlyLinkerCanUnlinkTaskList')` (same at :228 for the board note, :144 for the card note) with no `|| isOwner` escape. Frontend KanbanBoardPage.tsx:789 renders the unlink button on `{!readOnly && ...}` — every WRITE user, not just the linker — while the note unlink at line 750 correctly checks `user?.id === board.noteLinkedById`. types.ts:12 declares `taskListLinkedBy` which the API never returns (getBoard sends the scalar taskListLinkedById), so the component has no field to check even if it wanted to.
- **Impatto utente:** Bob (WRITE) links his own task list to Alice's board. Alice sees the linked-list row and an unlink button (she is not readOnly); clicking it does nothing — the server returns 403 onlyLinkerCanUnlinkTaskList and no toast is shown. She cannot link her own list either ('board already linked'). Her board is permanently bound to a list she cannot open or detach.
- **Fix:** Allow the board owner as a second authorised actor in the three unlink functions: `if (linkedById !== actorId && board.ownerId !== actorId) throw ...` (select ownerId in the existing findUnique). Then, on the frontend, gate the line-789 button the way line 750 already does: `{!readOnly && (isOwner || user?.id === board.taskListLinkedById) && ...}`.
- **Correzione del verificatore:** The cited scenario ('Bob (WRITE) links his own task list to Alice's board') is not UI-reachable — the link/unlink menu entry is owner-gated at KanbanBoardPage.tsx:692 — so for task lists it needs the API gap from finding 2. What IS reachable today: (a) the :789 unlink button shown to any WRITE collaborator who then gets a silent 403, and (b) the same only-linker rule on CARD notes, where a WRITE collaborator can link their own note via the UI (routes/kanban.ts:538-543 + linking.service.ts:86) and the board owner is then permanently unable to detach it — CardDetailModal.tsx:484 does not even render the button for her. Also note `taskListLinkedById` does arrive at runtime (getBoard spreads all board scalars); it is merely missing from the TS type, so the frontend check is one type-line away, not impossible.

#### `medium` · ux · S — Share modal opened from the board grid shows no existing collaborators, and re-inviting silently un-shares them
`frontend/src/features/kanban/KanbanPage.tsx:137`

- **Problema:** ShareBoardModal takes `sharedWith` (default []); the board-grid call site omits it, so the 'Shared with' list is always empty there and revoke is unreachable. The natural workaround — retyping the collaborator's email to change their permission — resets an ACCEPTED share back to PENDING on the server, silently cutting off access.
