- **Evidenza:** KanbanPage.tsx:137-144 renders `<ShareBoardModal boardId=... boardTitle=... />` with no sharedWith, while the board page passes it (KanbanBoardPage.tsx:1105 `sharedWith={board.shares?.filter(s => s.status === 'ACCEPTED')}`). The list data is available: db.ts:114 types `shares` on LocalKanbanBoard and listBoards returns it (board.service.ts:85). ShareBoardModal.tsx:26 `sharedWith = []` and :176 renders `t('kanban.share.noOne')`. sharing.service.ts:574-579 `upsert({ update: { permission, status: 'PENDING' } })` — an ACCEPTED share is knocked back to PENDING with no warning, and there is no permission-update endpoint for kanban shares.
- **Impatto utente:** Alice opens Share from the boards grid on a board shared with three people; it says 'shared with no one', so she can't revoke anyone and assumes the shares are gone. To upgrade Bob from READ to WRITE she re-enters his email: Bob instantly loses all access to the board (status PENDING, dropped from /kanban/boards and /share/kanbans/accepted, his local Dexie copy deleted by syncPull) until he notices and re-accepts the invitation.
- **Fix:** Two small changes: pass the data at the grid call site — `sharedWith={sharingBoard.shares?.map(s => ({ ...s }))}` (LocalKanbanBoard.shares already carries userId/permission/user, and listBoards only returns ACCEPTED ones); and in shareKanbanBoard, keep an already-ACCEPTED share accepted when only the permission changes: read the existing row first and pass `status: existing?.status === 'ACCEPTED' ? 'ACCEPTED' : 'PENDING'` in the upsert update branch.
- **Correzione del verificatore:** 'Silently' is half right. Two mitigations exist: the grid has a separate read-only view-shares affordance (KanbanPage.tsx:145-151 → SharedUsersModal, display-only, no revoke), so collaborators are visible somewhere; and ShareBoardModal renders an amber PENDING badge for the returned share (:196-200), so after re-inviting Alice can see the status is pending — she just is not warned that an ACCEPTED share was knocked back. The unreachable revoke and the access-dropping re-invite are both real. Minor nit on the proposed fix: LocalKanbanBoard.shares carries no `id`/`status`, and the modal keys on `share.id`, so the mapping needs those filled in.

#### `medium` · ux · M — READ-only members get an unarchive button, and every kanban 403 fails silently
`frontend/src/features/kanban/components/ArchivedCardsModal.tsx:65`

- **Problema:** ArchivedCardsModal is rendered without a readOnly prop, so a READ-permission member sees an Unarchive action the server rejects (POST /cards/:id/unarchive requires WRITE, routes/kanban.ts:574). Nothing in the kanban feature translates a server error key, so this and every other permission failure is a dead click — and one key the backend throws doesn't even exist in the locale files.
- **Evidenza:** KanbanBoardPage.tsx:1151-1158 renders `<ArchivedCardsModal ... />` with no readOnly; the archive button itself (line 586) is correctly ungated for READ. ArchivedCardsModal.tsx:28-33 `async function handleUnarchive(cardId) { await kanbanService.unarchiveCard(cardId); ... }` — no try/catch, so a 403 is an unhandled rejection. No component under frontend/src/features/kanban reads `response.data.message` (grep returns nothing), and api.ts only special-cases 401. BulkArchiveDialog.tsx:50-76 uses try/finally with no catch. `errors.kanban.ownerOnly`, thrown at routes/kanban.ts:584 and :595, is absent from BOTH frontend/src/locales/en.json and it.json (all 20 other errors.kanban.* keys are present).
- **Impatto utente:** A READ-only member opens Archived cards, clicks Unarchive on each of five cards, and nothing happens — no error, no toast, no state change; the same silence hits the task-list unlink 403 and the bulk-archive owner-only 403 for a non-owner. Users retry, then report the board as broken.
- **Fix:** Pass and honour the permission that already exists: add `readOnly` to ArchivedCardsModalProps, pass `readOnly={readOnly}` at KanbanBoardPage.tsx:1151, and render the Unarchive button only when `!readOnly`. Separately, add the missing `"ownerOnly"` key to errors.kanban in en.json and it.json, and give the link/unlink/unarchive mutations an onError that toasts `t(err.response?.data?.message ?? 'common.genericError')` so the keys the backend already returns actually reach the user.
- ⚠️ *non verificato in contraddittorio*

#### `low` · permission · S — DELETE /comments/:id performs no board access check — revoked members can still delete and notify
`backend/src/routes/kanban.ts:516`

- **Problema:** Every other kanban mutation goes through assertBoardAccess/getCardWithAccess; this route calls the service directly, and the service only checks comment authorship. Board membership is never revalidated.
- **Evidenza:** routes/kanban.ts:516-520 `fastify.delete('/comments/:id', async (request) => { await kanbanService.deleteComment(id, request.user.id); ... })` — no permission helper. comments-chat.service.ts:96-107 checks only `if (comment.authorId !== userId) throw new ForbiddenError('errors.kanban.notYourComment')`, then broadcasts comment:deleted (line 113) and fires notifyBoardUsersTiered to every current participant (line 123).
- **Impatto utente:** Alice revokes Bob's share (or re-shares to change his permission, which resets his status to PENDING). Bob's client still holds the comment ids. He can delete his old comments on Alice's board and every remaining member receives an in-app notification plus an email 'Bob deleted a comment on <card>' from someone who is no longer on the board. A member downgraded to READ keeps the same power.
- **Fix:** Add the missing check inside deleteComment (one place, covers the route and any future caller): after loading the comment, `await assertBoardAccess(comment.card.column.boardId, userId, 'READ')` before the authorship check.
- **Correzione del verificatore:** Overstated as medium. The authorship check means the actor can only delete comments they themselves wrote on a board they were once a member of — no access to other users' content, no read path, no state they could not already have removed while a member. The residual harm is post-revocation self-deletion plus a notification/email to remaining members from a non-member. Real missing check, but polish-grade next to findings 1-3.

### realtime-sse

#### `high` · bug · S — SSE stream that ends cleanly (or opens non-2xx) never reconnects — realtime dies silently for the session
`frontend/src/features/kanban/hooks/useKanbanRealtime.ts:91`

- **Problema:** Only a *thrown* fetch error schedules a reconnect. A clean end-of-stream (`done: true`) breaks the read loop and returns normally, and a non-OK response returns immediately — both without scheduling any retry. Realtime + presence are then permanently dead for the rest of the page's lifetime, with no UI signal.
- **Evidenza:** Line 91: `if (!response.ok || !response.body) return;` — no retry.
Lines 97-99: `while (true) { const { done, value } = await reader.read(); if (done) break; ...}` — after `break` execution falls out of the try, the catch is skipped, `connect()` returns, nothing is rescheduled.
Lines 116-119 are the ONLY retry path: `catch (err) { if (AbortError) return; reconnectTimeout = setTimeout(connect, 5000); }`.
The hook's return type (lines 8-11) exposes only `presenceUsers` and `highlightedCardIds` — there is no connection state, so nothing can render a "disconnected" indicator.
- **Impatto utente:** A pm2 restart or an IIS/ARR app-pool recycle ends every open SSE response gracefully (done: true). Every user sitting on a Kanban board loses realtime and presence permanently: cards moved by teammates never appear, the presence avatar row freezes on whoever was online at restart time, and the board query (staleTime 5 min, no refetchInterval) only refreshes if the user happens to blur and refocus the tab. Same outcome if the very first /events request returns 401/429/500 — the board looks fine but is frozen forever.
- **Fix:** Make every exit path go through the retry. Wrap the body in `try { ... } finally { if (!abortController.signal.aborted) reconnectTimeout = setTimeout(connect, delay); }` and delete the bare `return` on line 91, so a clean `done`, a non-OK response and a thrown error all reschedule. Add a trivial backoff (`delay = Math.min(delay * 2, 30000)`, reset to 5s on a successful open) so a down backend isn't hammered every 5s by every open tab.
- **Correzione del verificatore:** Real, but severity is high, not critical: no data is lost — mutations still write Dexie + syncQueue and syncPush still runs (useSync.ts:14-28, 55), and the board query still refetches on window focus once past the 5-min staleTime. Also, the guaranteed-dead path is the non-OK response at line 91 (401/429/502 at connect ⇒ permanently dead, no UI signal) and any clean EOF; an abrupt pm2 kill mid-chunked-response typically surfaces as a thrown read error and DOES hit the retry, so 'every pm2 restart kills realtime forever' is not established by the code. Secondary mitigation the finding misses: the 3s chat poll (useKanbanChat.ts:12) keeps board chat alive even with SSE dead — which is why finding #7's fix must not land before this one.

#### `high` · permission · S — Revoking a board share does not close the revoked user's SSE stream — they keep receiving live card, comment and chat content
`backend/src/services/sharing.service.ts:625`

- **Problema:** `GET /boards/:id/events` authorizes once at connect time and the connection then lives indefinitely. `revokeKanbanBoardShare` deletes the SharedKanbanBoard row but never closes or invalidates the open stream, so authorization is never re-checked for the life of the socket.
- **Evidenza:** backend/src/routes/kanban.ts:363 — `await assertBoardAccess(id, userId, 'READ');` runs once, then `addConnection(id, reply.raw, {...})` (line 378) stores the raw response in the module-level `boardConnections` map.
`broadcast()` (backend/src/services/kanbanSSE.ts:102-113) iterates that map and writes to every stored socket with no permission check.
backend/src/services/sharing.service.ts:625-654 `revokeKanbanBoardShare`: deletes the share row (633), logs an audit event, deletes reminders — the file has no `kanbanSSE` import at all (imports, lines 1-11), so no broadcast and no disconnect.
- **Impatto utente:** Owner revokes a collaborator who has the board open. That user's tab keeps streaming `card:created` / `card:updated` (full title + description + linked-note title), `comment:added` (full comment body and author), and `chat:message` (full board chat) for as long as the tab stays open — and they remain visible in everyone's presence avatar row as if still a member. Their board query is never invalidated, so nothing kicks them out.
- **Fix:** Add `export function disconnectUser(boardId: string, userId: string)` to kanbanSSE.ts (~8 lines): iterate `boardConnections.get(boardId)`, `conn.res.end()` for matching `conn.user.id` — the existing `res.on('close')` handler already clears the heartbeat, removes the entry and re-broadcasts presence. Call it from `revokeKanbanBoardShare` after the delete. Same call fixes a WRITE→READ downgrade if you also broadcast `board:updated` there.
- **Correzione del verificatore:** Accurate; one bound worth stating: the revoked user is eventually ejected when their board query refetches (window focus, or after the 5-min staleTime) — assertBoardAccess then 403s, useKanbanBoard has retry:false, and KanbanBoardPage.tsx:65-69 redirects. So the leak lasts as long as the tab stays open without a refetch (can be hours on an idle focused tab), not literally forever. By the stated rubric ('critical = permission hole') this is arguably critical rather than high.

#### `medium` · dataloss · S — No missed-event recovery: 'connected' is a no-op, so every event during a disconnect is lost forever
`frontend/src/features/kanban/hooks/useKanbanRealtime.ts:35`

- **Problema:** SSE here has no replay (no event ids, no Last-Event-ID, no server-side backlog), and the client does nothing on (re)connect to catch up. Any mutation that happened while the stream was down is simply never applied to the React Query cache or to Dexie.
- **Evidenza:** Lines 35-37: `} else if (event.type === 'connected') { // No action needed }`.
The backend writes exactly one `data: {"type":"connected"}` frame (backend/src/routes/kanban.ts:384) and keeps no per-board event log — `broadcast()` (backend/src/services/kanbanSSE.ts:102-113) writes straight to live sockets and drops the event for anyone not connected.
The board query has `staleTime: 1000*60*5` (frontend/src/lib/queryClient.ts:6) and no `refetchInterval` (useKanbanBoard.ts:8-74), so nothing else refreshes it.
- **Impatto utente:** The 5s reconnect window (and any longer outage) is a black hole: a teammate moves three cards and deletes a column during it, and the reconnected user keeps seeing the pre-outage board until they blur/refocus the tab past the 5-minute staleTime. Worse, they can drag a card that no longer exists server-side; the push 404s and is dropped silently (frontend/src/features/sync/syncService.ts:780).
- **Fix:** Turn the no-op into the catch-up: on `connected`, `queryClient.invalidateQueries({ queryKey: queryKeys.kanban.board(boardId!) })` (plus the boardChat key). One line, runs on the first connect too (harmless — the query is already fresh), and it is the entire missed-event recovery story this design needs.
- **Correzione del verificatore:** Category is wrong: this is a stale-view bug, not data loss. The offline copy self-heals — syncPull runs every 30s (useSync.ts:55) and rehydrates kanbanColumns/kanbanCards from the server (syncService.ts:383-413, preserving dirty rows), and the react-query view refetches on window focus or on any local mutation's invalidateBoard. The proposed one-line invalidate on 'connected' is still the right fix, but the gap is a stale rendered board (which can lead to a drag of a server-deleted card → 404 dropped at syncService.ts:780-784), not permanent loss.

#### `medium` · bug · S — Board-level mutations emit no SSE event at all: rename, delete, cover, avatar, bulk-archive
`backend/src/services/kanban/board.service.ts:271`

- **Problema:** Five state-changing paths never call `broadcast()`, so other viewers of the same board see nothing. The board-delete case is the damaging one: other users keep editing a board that no longer exists and their queued writes are then discarded silently.
- **Evidenza:** No `broadcast` call in any of:
- board.service.ts:252-269 `updateBoard` (title/description)
- board.service.ts:271-273 `deleteBoard` — `return prisma.kanbanBoard.delete({ where: { id: boardId } });`
- routes/kanban.ts:168 (cover upload), :224 (cover delete), :249 (avatar upload), :305 (avatar delete) — these `prisma.kanbanBoard.update(...)` inline in the route
- card.service.ts:482-500 `executeBulkArchive` (owner bulk-archives N cards)
board.service.ts imports only prisma/logger/errors/helpers (lines 1-5) — kanbanSSE is not imported.
Downstream: frontend/src/features/sync/syncService.ts:780 `if (status === 404 || status === 410) { console.warn(...); if (item.id) await db.syncQueue.delete(item.id); }` — a queued edit to a deleted board is dropped with only a console warning.
- **Impatto utente:** Owner deletes a shared board. A collaborator with it open sees no change (KanbanBoardPage's redirect at lines 65-69 only fires when the board query errors, and nothing invalidates it), keeps typing card titles and dragging cards for up to 5 minutes; every one of those edits 404s in syncPush and is deleted from the queue with no toast. Milder but constant: renaming a board or changing its cover is invisible to everyone else, and after a bulk archive other users still see and can drag the archived cards until their next refetch makes them vanish mid-interaction.
- **Fix:** `board:updated` already exists in the event union (kanbanSSE.ts:40) and the client already handles it (falls into the else branch → invalidates the board query). Emit it from `updateBoard`, the four cover/avatar routes and `executeBulkArchive` — five one-line `broadcast(boardId, { type: 'board:updated', boardId })` calls. For delete, add a `board:deleted` member and broadcast it before `prisma.kanbanBoard.delete` (the client's else branch already refetches → 404 → the existing redirect at KanbanBoardPage.tsx:65-69 fires).
- **Correzione del verificatore:** Overstated as high. All five paths self-heal on the next board refetch (window focus, any local mutation's invalidateBoard, or any other SSE event on that board), and in the delete case the discarded syncQueue items target a board that no longer exists — no server-side data is lost, the cost is a stale view plus wasted typing. Practical impact is degraded collaboration UX, i.e. medium.

#### `medium` · race · S — Optimistic move is clobbered by a refetch that races its own push (mutations resolve on the Dexie write, not the server)
`frontend/src/features/kanban/hooks/useKanbanMutations.ts:17`

- **Problema:** Kanban mutations resolve as soon as the Dexie row + syncQueue entry are written; the HTTP call happens later inside the fire-and-forget `syncPush()`. `onSuccess` fires `invalidateBoard()` immediately, so the board GET races ahead of its own PUT and returns pre-move server state, which then overwrites the optimistic `localColumns`.
- **Evidenza:** useKanbanMutations.ts:17-19 `function flushSync(): void { syncPush().catch(() => {}); }` — not awaited; :109-112 `moveCard.onSuccess: () => { flushSync(); invalidateBoard(); }`.
frontend/src/features/kanban/kanbanService.ts:372-385 `moveCard` only does `db.kanbanCards.update(...)` + `db.syncQueue.add(...)` — no HTTP.
frontend/src/features/sync/syncService.ts:565-568 processes the whole queue serially with `await api.put(...)` per item, so the move PUT is the *last* request, behind every other pending item.
useBoardDnD.ts:50-54 `if (board?.columns && !activeCardId && !activeColumnId && !isMoveInFlight) setLocalColumns(board.columns);` — and `isMoveInFlight` is cleared by `onSettled` (lines 245, 262, 296), which fires in the same tick as the Dexie write, long before the PUT.
- **Impatto utente:** Drag a card to another column: it lands, then snaps back to the old column, then jumps forward again a round-trip later. With a backlog in the sync queue (or a slow link) the snap-back sticks for seconds and users re-drag, queueing a second conflicting move. Any SSE event from a teammate arriving in that window causes exactly the same clobber, since it also calls `invalidateQueries(board)` (useKanbanRealtime.ts:62).
- **Fix:** Never refetch server state before the local change has been pushed: make `flushSync` return the promise (`const flushSync = () => syncPush().catch(() => {})`) and chain the invalidation for the Dexie-backed mutations — `onSuccess: () => { flushSync().then(invalidateBoard); }` for moveCard/updateCard/createCard/deleteCard/columns. Server-only mutations (cover, avatar, link*) keep invalidating directly.
- **Correzione del verificatore:** Real race, but it self-corrects rather than sticking: when the PUT lands the server broadcasts card:moved back to the actor (kanbanSSE.ts:102-113 has no self-exclusion — see finding #6), which invalidates the board again and restores the new layout. So the normal presentation is a snap-back flicker of one round-trip, becoming multi-second only when the push is queued behind a backlog or syncPush was already in flight. Degraded UX, not a persistent wrong state.

#### `medium` · perf · M — Events carry no actorId, so every actor gets their own event echoed back (extra full board GET + self-highlight)
`backend/src/services/kanbanSSE.ts:102`

- **Problema:** `broadcast()` writes to every connection on the board including the one that caused the change, and no event in the union carries an actor id, so the client cannot filter its own echo. Each echo triggers a full `GET /kanban/boards/:id` — a request that itself performs writes.
- **Evidenza:** kanbanSSE.ts:102-113 `broadcast` iterates all connections with no exclusion parameter (contrast chatWebSocket.ts:71 `broadcastToConversation(conversationId, event, excludeUserId?)`, which does have one).
The `KanbanEvent` union (kanbanSSE.ts:27-42) has no `actorId` on any member.
useKanbanRealtime.ts:59-67 — every non-presence event does `updateDexieFromSSE(...)` + `invalidateQueries(board)`.
That GET runs `archiveCompletedCards(boardId)` (an `updateMany`) and an `isCompleted` `kanbanColumn.update` on every call (board.service.ts:144-158), plus the shares/owner/note includes and an archived-card `count`.
Self-highlight: useKanbanRealtime.ts:39-55 pulses the card for 2s on `card:moved` regardless of who moved it.
- **Impatto utente:** Every card move you make pulses your own card as if a teammate had moved it, and costs you an extra full board refetch on top of the one your mutation already triggered. On a board with 5 people connected, a marquee bulk-move of 10 cards (KanbanBoardPage.tsx:356-364 fires 10 parallel PUTs) produces 50 event deliveries and a burst of full board GETs — each doing DB writes — which is exactly the shape that produced the v1.10.3 rate-limit spiral.
- **Fix:** Add `actorId?: string` to the event payloads that have an actor and pass it through from the services that already receive `actorId` (card.service.ts:34/85/234/349, comments-chat.service.ts:57/113/179). In `handleEvent`, `if (event.actorId === useAuthStore.getState().user?.id) return;` before the Dexie write/invalidate. Alternatively give `broadcast` an `excludeUserId` param mirroring chatWebSocket.ts:71 — but the client-side filter is preferable so the actor's other tabs still update.
- **Correzione del verificatore:** Two overstatements. (1) react-query dedupes concurrent refetches of the same key, so a 10-card marquee move (KanbanBoardPage.tsx:356-364) does not produce 10 board GETs per client — it produces a handful as the separate PUTs land. (2) The isCompleted column update in getBoard (board.service.ts:145-160) only fires when NO column is flagged completed, not on every call; the archiveCompletedCards updateMany does run every time, so 'the GET performs writes' still stands. The core claim — self-echo doubles the refetch per action and pulses your own card as if a teammate moved it — is accurate and the client-side actorId filter is the right fix.

#### `medium` · bug · S — An open SSE connection suppresses the recipient's DB notification, but the client surfaces nothing for comment/move events
`backend/src/services/kanban/notifications.ts:100`

- **Problema:** `notifyBoardUsersTiered` treats "has an SSE connection to this board" as "the frontend will show them" and skips the notification entirely — no DB record, no email. The frontend's SSE handler has no toast, sound, or badge for `comment:added`, `comment:deleted` or `card:moved`, so the notification is lost permanently.
- **Evidenza:** backend/src/services/kanban/notifications.ts:93-101: `const activeOnBoard = new Set(getPresenceUsers(boardId).map(u => u.id)); ... for (const uid of recipientIds) { // Tier 1: User is viewing board → skip\n if (activeOnBoard.has(uid)) continue;` — `continue` skips both `createNotification` (line 113) and the email.
Used by comment add/delete (comments-chat.service.ts) and card move (card.service.ts:280, 540).
Frontend: useKanbanRealtime.ts:29-68 handles `presence:update`, `chat:message` (invalidate) and everything else via invalidate + a 2s card pulse. There is no notification, toast or badge path for comments or moves — the only visible trace is the comment-count badge on a card.
The board chat path is fine by contrast: BoardChatSidebar tracks `messages.length` and calls `onNewMessage` → `incrementUnread` (useBoardModals.ts:26-28), rendering a badge.
- **Impatto utente:** A user leaves a board open in a background tab all afternoon. Teammates comment on cards and move cards: they receive no in-app notification (the bell stays empty — nothing was written to the DB), no email, and nothing visible on screen. Close the tab and the events are gone; there is no record they ever happened.
- **Fix:** Presence should suppress only the noisy tier. Replace the `continue` at notifications.ts:101 with a flag: always `await createNotification(...)`, and gate only the email branch on `!activeOnBoard.has(uid)`. Same edit in comments-chat.service.ts:205-210 for board chat, where the badge justifies skipping email but not the DB record.
- **Correzione del verificatore:** 'Nothing visible on screen' is wrong for card moves: useKanbanRealtime.ts:39-55 pulses the card for 2s and the invalidate re-renders it in its new column live, and comments do bump the card's comment-count badge. The Tier-1 skip is also deliberate and documented (notifications.ts:60-64). The accurate statement is narrower: presence is connection-based rather than visibility-based, so a board left open in a background tab silently suppresses the persistent DB notification — once the tab is closed there is no record the comment/move ever happened.

#### `medium` · dataloss · S — column:deleted wipes locally-pending cards from Dexie, losing an unpushed card
`frontend/src/features/kanban/hooks/useKanbanRealtime.ts:229`

- **Problema:** The `column:deleted` handler deletes every local card in that column unconditionally, ignoring the `syncStatus !== 'synced'` guard that every other branch of the same function applies. A card created locally but not yet pushed is destroyed, while its syncQueue CREATE entry survives and then 404s.
- **Evidenza:** useKanbanRealtime.ts:226-230: `case 'column:deleted': await db.kanbanColumns.delete(event.columnId); await db.kanbanCards.where('columnId').equals(event.columnId).delete();` — no guard.
Every sibling branch guards: line 165 `if (local && local.syncStatus !== 'synced') break;`, line 192 (card:moved), line 215 (column:updated), line 234 (columns:reordered).
The follow-up: syncPush POSTs to `/kanban/columns/<deleted id>/cards` (syncService.ts:686-688) → 404 → `if (status === 404 || status === 410) { ... await db.syncQueue.delete(item.id); }` (syncService.ts:780-784) — dropped with only a console.warn.
- **Impatto utente:** User A types a new card into a column while their push is still queued; user B deletes that column in the same window (the backend allows it because the card doesn't exist server-side yet — column.service.ts:76 only counts server rows). A's card vanishes from the board and from IndexedDB, the queued create is silently discarded, and A gets no message. The typed content is unrecoverable.
- **Fix:** Apply the same guard the rest of the function uses: replace the bulk delete with `const cards = await db.kanbanCards.where('columnId').equals(event.columnId).toArray(); await db.kanbanCards.bulkDelete(cards.filter(c => c.syncStatus === 'synced').map(c => c.id));`. The pending card then survives locally and its create fails visibly through the existing failed-item surfacing instead of disappearing.
- **Correzione del verificatore:** Accurate as written. Two refinements: the window is the lifetime of the queued CREATE (normally sub-second, but widened arbitrarily by an offline period or a backlogged/failed queue), and the loss is genuinely unrecoverable since the content never reached the server. The proposed guarded bulkDelete is the minimal fix; worth pairing with surfacing the 404-dropped CREATE instead of console.warn, otherwise the user still gets no message.

#### `low` · perf · S — Board chat polls every 3 seconds on top of SSE, from a sidebar that is mounted even when closed
`frontend/src/features/kanban/hooks/useKanbanChat.ts:12`

- **Problema:** `chat:message` is already an SSE event that invalidates the chat query, yet the chat query also sets a 3s `refetchInterval`. And `BoardChatSidebar` (which owns the hook) is rendered unconditionally for any shared board — `isOpen` only short-circuits its render output — so the poll runs for the whole time the board page is open, closed drawer or not.
- **Evidenza:** useKanbanChat.ts:8-13: `useQuery({ queryKey: ..., queryFn: () => getBoardChat(boardId!), enabled: !!boardId, refetchInterval: 3000 })`.
useKanbanRealtime.ts:33-34 already handles it: `else if (event.type === 'chat:message') { queryClient.invalidateQueries({ queryKey: queryKeys.kanban.boardChat(boardId!) }); }`.
KanbanBoardPage.tsx:1072-1085: `{(isOwner || isShared) && (<BoardChatSidebar boardId={boardId} isOpen={modals.isChatOpen} ... />)}` — mounted regardless of `isChatOpen`; the early-out is inside the component (BoardChatSidebar.tsx:85 `if (!isOpen) return null;`), after `useKanbanChat` has already run.
- **Impatto utente:** Every user with a shared board open issues ~1,200 `GET /api/kanban/boards/:id/chat` per hour they leave the tab open, none of which is needed — SSE already pushes new messages. Five people on one board is ~100 requests/minute of pure waste against the same rate limiter that caused the v1.10.3 spiral, and it keeps mobile radios awake.
- **Fix:** Delete `refetchInterval: 3000` — the SSE `chat:message` invalidation covers it, and finding #2's `connected`-invalidate covers the reconnect gap. If you want belt-and-braces while the drawer is open, make it `refetchInterval: isOpen ? 15000 : false` and pass `isOpen` into the hook.
- **Correzione del verificatore:** Materially overstated, and the fix as written is unsafe today. (1) react-query's refetchInterval does not run while the tab is backgrounded (refetchIntervalInBackground defaults to false), so '~1,200 requests per hour you leave the tab open' and 'keeps mobile radios awake' are wrong — the poll only runs on a focused/visible tab. (2) Mounting the sidebar while closed is deliberate and required, not a defect: BoardChatSidebar.tsx:34 says so and the sound/badge effect at 49-66 depends on messages.length growing while closed. (3) Deleting the poll must come AFTER finding #1 — right now it is the only thing that keeps chat updating once the SSE stream dies silently. Net: wasted requests on a focused tab; polish.

#### `low` · bug · S — card:created broadcasts an untransformed Prisma row, so remote clients write commentCount: undefined into Dexie
`backend/src/services/kanban/card.service.ts:34`

- **Problema:** Every other card broadcast passes the row through `transformCard()` (which maps `_count.comments` → `commentCount`); `createCard` broadcasts the raw selected row. The SSE payload therefore carries `_count` and no `commentCount`, and the client's Dexie writer reads the field that isn't there.
- **Evidenza:** card.service.ts:29-38: `const card = await prisma.kanbanCard.create({ ..., select: cardWithAssigneeSelect }); broadcast(column.boardId, { type: 'card:created', boardId: column.boardId, card }); ... return transformCard(card);` — the HTTP response is transformed, the broadcast is not.
Contrast card.service.ts:81-85 (`const card = transformCard(rawCard); broadcast(boardId, { type: 'card:updated', boardId, card });`) and linking.service.ts:120-122.
helpers.ts:49-53 `transformCard` is what produces `commentCount`.
The event type is `card: Record<string, unknown>` (kanbanSSE.ts:29) so TypeScript cannot catch the mismatch; the client then does `commentCount: card.commentCount` (useKanbanRealtime.ts:154) → undefined.
- **Impatto utente:** A card created by a teammate is written into every other viewer's IndexedDB with `commentCount: undefined` and a stray shape mismatch. Harmless the instant it is created (0 comments), but if the follow-up board refetch fails — offline, or the 404/redirect path — the offline copy of that card carries a missing field where the rest of the table has a number.
- **Fix:** `broadcast(column.boardId, { type: 'card:created', boardId: column.boardId, card: transformCard(card) });` and reuse the value for the return. One line. Tightening the event type from `Record<string, unknown>` to the transformed card shape would make the next instance a compile error.
- **Correzione del verificatore:** Impact is even smaller than stated: the same event's invalidate refetches the board, whose queryFn rewrites the card into Dexie with the correct commentCount (useKanbanBoard.ts:40, allowed because the SSE row was written syncStatus:'synced'), and syncPull rehydrates every 30s regardless. The real justification for the one-line fix is consistency and not shipping `_count`/`archivedAt`/`taskItemId` over the wire — not user-visible breakage.

#### `low` · architecture · M — The SSE wire protocol is declared twice by hand and has already drifted: two backend events the client type doesn't know
`frontend/src/features/kanban/types.ts:170`

- **Problema:** `KanbanEvent` (backend) and `KanbanSSEEvent` (frontend) are two independently maintained unions for the same wire format. They are already out of sync by two members, and the drift is invisible because the client's dispatch ends in a catch-all `else`.
- **Evidenza:** backend/src/services/kanbanSSE.ts:27-42 declares 14 members including `card:unarchived` (line 33) and `board:updated` (line 40).
frontend/src/features/kanban/types.ts:170-183 declares 13 members and has neither `card:unarchived` nor `board:updated` (it has `connected`, which the backend union lacks — it is written ad hoc at routes/kanban.ts:384).
Consequence in the Dexie writer: `updateDexieFromSSE` (useKanbanRealtime.ts:136-238) has no `case 'card:unarchived'`, so unarchiving a card never restores it to the local copy — only the incidental board refetch does.
- **Impatto utente:** Adding an event backend-side compiles and ships with no client handling and no type error — it just silently falls into the else branch and triggers a full board refetch. Today that means `card:unarchived` never updates the offline copy: if the follow-up board GET fails (offline, or a slow link), the unarchived card stays missing from IndexedDB until the next successful fetch.
- **Fix:** One source of truth: move the union into a shared file (or have types.ts re-export the backend type) and add the missing `case 'card:unarchived'` to `updateDexieFromSSE` (`db.kanbanCards.update(event.cardId, { syncStatus: 'synced' })` is not enough — the card was filtered out of getBoard, so put the card payload in the event and upsert it). Making `updateDexieFromSSE`'s switch exhaustive over the shared type turns future drift into a compile error.
- **Correzione del verificatore:** The claimed user-visible consequence does not hold. card:unarchived falls into the else branch, which invalidates the board query; that queryFn rewrites every server card into Dexie (useKanbanBoard.ts:14-60), and syncPull redoes it every 30s. And you cannot be offline and still receive an SSE event, so 'the unarchived card stays missing from IndexedDB' needs SSE alive + the HTTP GET failing + no syncPull for 30s. This is a genuine structural fragility (silent protocol drift, no compile-time guard) with no live bug behind it today.

### reminders-notifications

#### `high` · bug · S — Self-assigning a card notifies the whole board with "X assigned you to ..."
`backend/src/services/kanban/notifications.ts:31` · anche: `backend/src/services/kanban/card.service.ts`

- **Problema:** notifyBoardUsers treats `specificUserId === actorId` as "no specific user" and falls through to the broadcast branch instead of returning. Self-assignment therefore fans a personally-worded notification out to every other board participant.
- **Evidenza:** notifications.ts:31 `if (specificUserId && specificUserId !== actorId) { await createNotification(specificUserId, ...); return; }` — no `else return`. When the guard is false execution continues to lines 37-58, which build `recipientIds` from owner + ACCEPTED shares, `recipientIds.delete(actorId)`, and createNotification for each. The only caller is card.service.ts:154-170, which passes `specificUserId = data.assigneeId` with message `` `${assignerName} assigned you to "${cardTitle}"` `` and `localizationKey: 'notifications.kanbanCardAssigned'` ("{{assignerName}} assigned you to ..."). There is no test for notifications.ts (backend/src/services/kanban/__tests__/ contains board/card/column/comments-chat/linking only) and card.service.test.ts:33 mocks notifyBoardUsers, so nothing covers this branch.
- **Impatto utente:** Alice opens a card on a 12-person board and assigns it to herself. The other 11 members each get an in-app notification and (if offline >5 min) a push saying "Alice assigned you to 'Fix login'". Nobody was assigned anything. On an active board this is the dominant source of false notifications.
- **Fix:** Return unconditionally when `specificUserId` is set: change line 31 to `if (specificUserId) { if (specificUserId !== actorId) await createNotification(...); return; }`. Add one unit test for notifications.ts asserting zero notifications when specificUserId === actorId.

#### `high` · i18n · S — Kanban push notifications render raw {{authorName}} / {{senderName}} / {{boardTitle}} placeholders
`backend/src/services/kanban/comments-chat.service.ts:79` · anche: `backend/src/utils/notificationI18n.ts`, `frontend/src/features/notifications/NotificationItem.tsx`

- **Problema:** Three kanban call sites pass localizationArgs whose key names do not match the placeholders in the corresponding backend template. `interpolate()` emits the literal `{{key}}` for any missing arg, so the push body ships placeholder syntax to the user.
- **Evidenza:** notificationI18n.ts:137 template is `'{{authorName}} commented on "{{cardTitle}}" in board "{{boardTitle}}"'` but comments-chat.service.ts:79 passes `{ commenterName, cardTitle }` — no authorName, no boardTitle. Same at :135 (`{ deleterName, cardTitle }` vs template at notificationI18n.ts:153 needing `{{authorName}}`/`{{boardTitle}}`) and :235 (`{ authorName, boardTitle }` vs notificationI18n.ts:179 needing `{{senderName}}`). notificationI18n.ts:212 `interpolate` = `template.replace(/\{\{(\w+)\}\}/g, (_, key) => args[key] ?? `{{${key}}}`)`. The frontend normalizer buildArgs (NotificationItem.tsx:82-136) whitelists only exact names — it has no commenterName→authorName or authorName→senderName mapping — so in-app the guard at NotificationItem.tsx:203 (`!localizedMessage.includes('{{')`) discards the translation and falls back to the hardcoded English `notification.message`.
- **Impatto utente:** An offline user commented on gets a push reading: `{{authorName}} commented on "Fix login" in board "{{boardTitle}}"`. An Italian user reading the same event in the bell menu gets an Italian title ("Nuovo Commento", which needs no args) over an English body — mixed-language notification on every kanban comment, comment deletion and board-chat message.
- **Fix:** Rename the args at the three call sites to match the templates: comments-chat.service.ts:79 → `{ authorName: commenterName, cardTitle: card.title, boardTitle }` (fetch board title alongside the card at :36-43), :135 → `{ authorName: deleterName, cardTitle, boardTitle }`, :235 → `{ senderName: authorName, boardTitle: board.title }`. Cheap guard against regressions: a test that iterates every `localizationKey` used in backend and asserts each `{{placeholder}}` in the template has a matching arg key.
- **Correzione del verificatore:** Substance is right; two cited line numbers are off — interpolate is at notificationI18n.ts:194-196 (not 212) and the in-app fallback guard is NotificationItem.tsx:208 (not 203, which is the title branch). buildArgs is 81-135. Note buildArgs merges top-level data over localizationArgs (`{ ...data.localizationArgs, ...data }`, line 85), so the board-chat case does get boardTitle — only {{senderName}} is missing there, which is still enough to trip the guard.

#### `high` · bug · S — Bulk move sends an empty email with a dead board link, and its push is never localized
`backend/src/services/kanban/card.service.ts:553` · anche: `backend/src/services/email.service.ts`, `backend/src/utils/notificationI18n.ts`, `frontend/src/features/notifications/NotificationItem.tsx`

- **Problema:** bulkMoveNotify reuses the KANBAN_CARD_MOVED email template but supplies a completely different data shape (count/summary/boardTitle instead of cardTitle/fromColumn/toColumn/boardId), and its localizationKey `notifications.kanbanBulkMove` is missing from the backend i18n table.
- **Evidenza:** card.service.ts:553-566 passes `data: (_email, locale) => ({ actorName, count, summary, boardTitle, locale })`. email.service.ts:455-460 reads `data.cardTitle`, `data.fromColumn`, `data.toColumn` and builds `const boardLink = `${FRONTEND_URL}/kanban?boardId=${data.boardId || ''}`` — all four are undefined here, so `escapeHtml(undefined || '')` yields empty strings. Separately, `grep kanbanBulkMove backend/src/utils/notificationI18n.ts` returns nothing (the key exists only in frontend en.json:763 and it.json:1018), so resolveNotification (notificationI18n.ts:196) returns the hardcoded English fallback. The in-app path fails too: buildArgs (NotificationItem.tsx:82-136) whitelists no `count` or `summary`, so `{{count}}` survives and NotificationItem.tsx:203 falls back to the raw English message.
- **Impatto utente:** A user bulk-moves 8 cards. Every offline board member receives an email titled `Card moved: "" - Notiq` whose body reads `Alice moved "" from  to .` with an "Open Board" button pointing at `/kanban?boardId=` (the empty board list). Italian users get the whole bulk-move notification in English in-app and in push.
- **Fix:** Two lines: add `'notifications.kanbanBulkMove'` + `_TITLE` to notificationI18n.ts mirroring en.json:763/it.json:1018, and add `count`/`summary` to buildArgs' whitelist in NotificationItem.tsx. For the email, add a `KANBAN_BULK_MOVE` case in email.service.ts using actorName/count/summary/boardId (and pass `boardId` in the data callback) rather than reusing the single-card template.

#### `high` · bug · S — Clicking a kanban reminder navigates to a non-existent route and dumps the user on /notes
`frontend/src/features/reminders/RemindersPage.tsx:129` · anche: `frontend/src/App.tsx`, `frontend/src/features/kanban/KanbanPage.tsx`

- **Problema:** The reminders page navigates to a path-param URL for kanban boards, but the router only declares a flat `kanban` route that reads boardId from the query string. The URL falls through to the catch-all redirect.
- **Evidenza:** RemindersPage.tsx:129 `navigate(`/kanban/${item.boardId}`)`. App.tsx:64 declares `<Route path="kanban" element={<KanbanPage />} />` with no child route and no splat; App.tsx:90 is `<Route path="*" element={<Navigate to="/notes" replace />} />`. KanbanPage.tsx:22-23 reads `searchParams.get('boardId')`. Every other navigation in the codebase uses the query form — `grep -rn "kanban/\${" frontend/src` returns this line and nothing else; KanbanPage.tsx:62, NotificationItem.tsx:38 and email.service.ts:460 all use `/kanban?boardId=`.
- **Impatto utente:** A user opens Reminders, sees an overdue card, clicks it, and lands on the Notes page with no explanation. The primary affordance of a kanban reminder — get me to the card — is dead for every kanban row on the page.
- **Fix:** RemindersPage.tsx:129 → ``navigate(`/kanban?boardId=${item.boardId}`)``. While there, the reminder knows `item.cardId`; appending `&cardId=${item.cardId}` and opening the detail modal would land the user on the actual card, but the one-token URL fix removes the defect.

#### `medium` · offline · M — Kanban reminders silently disappear offline; checking one off is a silent no-op
`frontend/src/hooks/useKanbanReminders.ts:17` · anche: `frontend/src/features/reminders/RemindersPage.tsx`, `frontend/src/features/kanban/kanbanService.ts`

- **Problema:** Kanban is offline-first (Dexie kanbanBoards/Columns/Cards) but its reminders are a network-only react-query fetch with no Dexie source and no error surface, while note and task reminders on the same page are Dexie live queries. The toggle mutation has no onError either.
- **Evidenza:** useKanbanReminders.ts:17-24 is a bare `useQuery` over `api.get('/kanban/reminders')` — no `networkMode`, no offline fallback. Compare useReminders.ts and useTaskReminders.ts, both `useLiveQuery` over Dexie. RemindersPage.tsx:106 computes `const isLoading = !noteReminders && !kanbanReminders && !taskReminders`, so once the two Dexie queries resolve the page renders as "loaded" with `kanbanReminders === undefined`; RemindersPage.tsx:72 `if (kanbanReminders)` then simply skips the whole kanban block. Nothing reads `isError`. useKanbanReminders.ts:28-36 `useToggleKanbanReminder` defines only `onSuccess`.
- **Impatto utente:** Offline (or during any /kanban/reminders 500), the Reminders page renders normally showing only note and task reminders — the user's kanban due dates vanish with zero indication, in an app whose whole promise is offline-first. Tapping the circle on a kanban reminder while offline does nothing at all: no optimistic tick, no toast, no queued retry.
- **Fix:** Smallest root fix: derive kanban reminders from Dexie like the other two. `db.kanbanCards` already holds dueDate and boardId, and the per-user isDone is the only server-side bit — persist it into the existing Dexie card row so `useKanbanReminders` becomes a `useLiveQuery`, and route the toggle through the existing syncQueue (kanbanService.updateCard pattern) instead of a raw api.put. If that is too large for now, at minimum surface `isError` on the page and add an `onError` toast to the mutation so the failure is not silent.
- **Correzione del verificatore:** Accurate, but the impact is narrower than 'kanban due dates vanish': the cards and their dueDates are still in Dexie and visible on the board itself (KanbanCard.tsx:245-257), so this is a degraded/silently-failing Reminders page, not data loss. The sharpest half is the toggle: no optimistic update, no error toast, no queued retry.

#### `medium` · bug · S — Reminders survive card archiving and completion; a new board member is flooded with fake overdue reminders
`backend/src/services/kanbanReminder.service.ts:130` · anche: `backend/src/services/kanban/card.service.ts`, `backend/prisma/schema.prisma`

- **Problema:** The reminder read query filters only by userId — never by the card's archivedAt or its column's isCompleted. The auto-complete write only fires on one narrow path, so several routes leave live reminders pointing at cards that are done or invisible.
- **Evidenza:** kanbanReminder.service.ts:130 `where: { userId }` with no archivedAt/column filter. Auto-complete exists only at card.service.ts:315-321, inside the `if (actorId && card.columnId !== toColumnId)` block opened at card.service.ts:241 — so a card that gets its dueDate set while already sitting in a completed column (updateCard → createRemindersForCard, kanbanReminder.service.ts:26-46) is never completed. Archiving never touches reminders: archiveCompletedCards (card.service.ts:363-400) and executeBulkArchive (card.service.ts:479-500) only set archivedAt. And createRemindersForNewBoardUser (kanbanReminder.service.ts:100-107) selects `where: { column: { boardId }, dueDate: { not: null } }` — no archivedAt filter, no isCompleted filter — and inserts them all with the schema default `isDone false` (schema.prisma:611).
- **Impatto utente:** A user accepts a share on a two-year-old board and their Reminders page immediately fills with dozens of red "overdue" rows for cards that were finished and archived long ago. Existing members see the same rot from any card whose dueDate was set after it reached the Done column, and archived cards keep generating reminder rows that link to cards no longer on the board.
- **Fix:** One filter at the read boundary fixes every write path at once — kanbanReminder.service.ts:130 → `where: { userId, card: { archivedAt: null, column: { isCompleted: false } } }`. Mirror the same predicate in the createRemindersForNewBoardUser query (line 102-105) so the rows are never created in the first place.

#### `medium` · perf · M — Notification fan-out runs N sequential queries plus N SMTP sends inside the request
`backend/src/services/kanban/notifications.ts:99` · anche: `backend/src/services/kanban/comments-chat.service.ts`, `backend/src/services/notification.service.ts`, `backend/src/services/kanban/card.service.ts`

- **Problema:** notifyBoardUsersTiered (and the copy of it in createBoardChatMessage) loops recipients sequentially, doing a per-user findUnique, then createNotification which does another findUnique and a push, then an awaited sendNotificationEmail which does a third findUnique — all awaited before the HTTP response returns.
- **Evidenza:** notifications.ts:99-133: `for (const uid of recipientIds) { const recipient = await prisma.user.findUnique(...); await createNotification(...); ... await emailService.sendNotificationEmail(...) }`. notification.service.ts:28-31 does a second `prisma.user.findUnique({ where: { id: userId } })` for the same user. email.service.ts:75-85 does a third, `findUnique({ where: { email: to } })` for emailNotificationsEnabled — which notifications.ts:115 already checked. comments-chat.service.ts:203-244 duplicates the whole loop for board chat. All of it is awaited by createComment (comments-chat.service.ts:67) before `return comment`, and by moveCard (card.service.ts:280) before returning.
- **Impatto utente:** On a 15-member board, posting one comment blocks the HTTP response on ~45 sequential Postgres round-trips plus up to 15 sequential SMTP handshakes. The commenter watches a spinner for seconds; one slow SMTP connection stalls the response for everyone. Board chat has the same cost on every message sent.
- **Fix:** Two small changes, no framework: replace the per-user findUnique with one `prisma.user.findMany({ where: { id: { in: [...recipientIds] } }, select: {...} })` before the loop, and stop awaiting the fan-out at the call sites — `void notifyBoardUsersTiered(...).catch(err => logger.warn(...))` in createComment/deleteComment/moveCard/bulkMoveNotify, since the function already swallows its own errors and the response does not depend on it.
- ⚠️ *non verificato in contraddittorio*

#### `medium` · bug · S — Date-only kanban due dates are shown with a fabricated clock time and drift a day for negative UTC offsets
`frontend/src/features/reminders/RemindersPage.tsx:173` · anche: `frontend/src/features/kanban/components/CardDetailModal.tsx`, `backend/src/services/kanban/card.service.ts`

- **Problema:** Kanban due dates are captured by an `<input type="date">` and stored as UTC midnight, but the reminders page formats them with a time pattern and every bucketing helper reinterprets them in local time.
- **Evidenza:** CardDetailModal.tsx:388-390 is `<input type="date" value={card.dueDate ? formatDueDate(card.dueDate) : ''} onChange={handleDueDateChange}` and handleDueDateChange (:132-139) sends the bare `"YYYY-MM-DD"`; card.service.ts:71 does `new Date(data.dueDate)` → UTC midnight. RemindersPage.tsx:173 then formats kanban rows with `item.type === 'task' ? 'PPP' : 'PPP p'` — the `p` renders a clock time that was never entered. The overdue/today/upcoming split at RemindersPage.tsx:109-111 uses `isPast/isToday/isFuture(new Date(item.dueDate))` in local time, as does isDueOverdue at CardDetailModal.tsx:34-37.
- **Impatto utente:** A card due 31 Aug shows as "August 31st, 2026 at 2:00 AM" in Italy (1:00 AM in winter — the same card appears to change time at the DST boundary). For any user west of UTC the stored instant lands on the previous local day: the card is filed under Today on the 30th and flagged red Overdue all day on the 31st, the day it is actually due.
- **Fix:** Drop the `p` — RemindersPage.tsx:173 → `format(new Date(item.dueDate), 'PPP', { locale: dateLocale })` for kanban too, since the field carries no time. For the day drift, compare on the date string rather than the instant: the value is already `YYYY-MM-DD` after `formatDueDate`, so bucket with `parseISO(item.dueDate.slice(0, 10))` (parsed as local midnight) instead of `new Date(item.dueDate)`.

#### `medium` · bug · S — Editing a due date re-opens everyone's completed reminder, but moving a card out of Done never re-opens it
`backend/src/services/kanbanReminder.service.ts:57` · anche: `backend/src/services/kanban/card.service.ts`

- **Problema:** The reminder isDone flag is reset in one direction only. A due-date edit force-clears isDone for every board member regardless of the card's state, while the inverse of the auto-complete rule — moving a card back out of a completed column — leaves all reminders stuck at done.
- **Evidenza:** kanbanReminder.service.ts:57-60 `updateMany({ where: { cardId }, data: { dueDate: newDueDate, isDone: false } })`, called from card.service.ts:118 on any update whose payload includes a non-null dueDate when one already existed — with no check on the card's column. The asymmetry is visible three lines apart in moveCard: the linked TaskItem is reset in both directions (card.service.ts:256-268, `movedIntoCompleted` → isChecked true, `movedOutOfCompleted` → isChecked false), but the reminder block at card.service.ts:315-321 only has the `targetColumn.isCompleted` half.
- **Impatto utente:** Someone nudges the due date on a card sitting in Done and the card re-appears as a pending (probably overdue) reminder for every member who had already ticked it off. Conversely a card dragged from Done back to In Progress because it was reopened stays silently checked-off on everyone's Reminders page — the one case where they most need the reminder back.
- **Fix:** Add the missing half next to the existing one at card.service.ts:315-321: `else if (card.column.isCompleted && !targetColumn.isCompleted) { await prisma.kanbanReminder.updateMany({ where: { cardId, isDone: true }, data: { isDone: false } }); }` (the isCompleted flags are already selected at :180 and :186). For the reset, only clear isDone when the date actually moved forward — guard the call at card.service.ts:118 with `new Date(data.dueDate).getTime() !== currentCard.dueDate.getTime()`.

#### `low` · bug · S — Moving a card across columns silently reassigns it to the mover with no notification and no ASSIGNED activity
`backend/src/services/kanban/card.service.ts:243`

- **Problema:** Cross-column moves overwrite assigneeId with the actor via a direct Prisma update that bypasses updateCard, so neither the notification nor the ASSIGNED activity-log entry that every other assignment change produces is emitted.
- **Evidenza:** card.service.ts:243-247 `await prisma.kanbanCard.update({ where: { id: cardId }, data: { assigneeId: actorId } })`, followed at :249 by `logCardActivity(cardId, actorId, 'MOVED', ...)` and nothing else. Compare the normal path: updateCard logs `ASSIGNED` (card.service.ts:88-92) and calls notifyBoardUsers with the new assignee (card.service.ts:154-170). The move path emits only the tiered KANBAN_CARD_MOVED notification (card.service.ts:280), whose text says nothing about assignment; the previous assignee gets no notification at all, and if they are currently viewing the board the tiered helper skips them entirely (notifications.ts:100-101).
- **Impatto utente:** Bob owns the card "Ship release". Alice drags it one column right to unblock the board and the card is now assigned to Alice. Bob is never told, and opening the card's activity history shows only "Alice moved this card" — nothing explains why his name disappeared from it.
- **Fix:** In the cross-column block, only reassign when the card is unassigned (`if (!card.assigneeId)` — fetch assigneeId in the select at card.service.ts:174-183), or, if the auto-assign is intended, log `ASSIGNED` alongside the MOVED entry and notify the displaced assignee. The guard is the smaller and less surprising change.
- **Correzione del verificatore:** The reassignment itself is intentional, not a slip: the comment at card.service.ts:242 says 'log activity + auto-assign card to the mover' and card.service.test.ts:508 asserts 'auto-assigns card to mover on cross-column move'. So the proposed `if (!card.assigneeId)` guard would regress documented, tested behavior. The genuine residual defect is only the missing audit trail — no ASSIGNED activity row and no notice to the displaced assignee — which makes this a low-severity polish item, not a bug.

#### `low` · perf · S — Email debounce Maps grow forever in the long-lived server process
`backend/src/services/kanban/notifications.ts:9` · anche: `backend/src/services/kanban/comments-chat.service.ts`, `backend/src/utils/metrics.ts`

- **Problema:** Two module-level Maps key debounce timestamps by user+board (and notification type) and are only ever written, never pruned, in a process that runs for weeks under pm2.
- **Evidenza:** notifications.ts:9-10 `export const boardChatEmailDebounce = new Map<string, number>(); export const cardActionEmailDebounce = new Map<string, number>();`. Writes at notifications.ts:126 (`cardActionEmailDebounce.set(`card:${type}:${uid}:${boardId}`, Date.now())`) and comments-chat.service.ts:241. No `delete`, no size cap, no timer — `grep -n 'Debounce.delete\|Debounce.clear' backend/src` finds nothing. The codebase already has the prune pattern it needs: utils/metrics.ts:55-58 runs `setInterval(() => this.prune(), PRUNE_INTERVAL_MS)`.
- **Impatto utente:** Not user-visible until it is: the key space is (users x boards x 3 notification types) and entries are pinned for the process lifetime, so a growing installation slowly leaks heap in the backend that pm2 keeps alive between deploys.
- **Fix:** Copy the metrics.ts pattern — one `setInterval` in notifications.ts that walks both Maps and deletes entries older than the longest debounce window (BOARD_CHAT_EMAIL_DEBOUNCE_MS, 30 min), with `.unref()` so it does not hold the process open during graceful shutdown.

### routes-validation

#### `critical` · permission · S — PUT /cards/:id/move never checks toColumnId belongs to the card's board — cross-board card injection
`backend/src/routes/kanban.ts:461` · anche: `backend/src/services/kanban/card.service.ts`

- **Problema:** The move route authorizes only the SOURCE board (getCardWithAccess on the card), and moveCardSchema declares toColumnId as a bare z.string(). The service fetches the target column but never compares its boardId to the card's boardId, so a card can be relocated into any column in the database by id.
- **Evidenza:** kanban.ts:56-59 `const moveCardSchema = z.object({ toColumnId: z.string(), position: z.number().int().min(0) })` — no relation constraint. kanban.ts:460-464: `await getCardWithAccess(id, request.user.id, 'WRITE'); const { toColumnId, position } = moveCardSchema.parse(request.body); await kanbanService.moveCard(id, toColumnId, position, ...)` — the only authorization is on the card's own board. card.service.ts:194-198 fetches `targetColumn` WITH `boardId` selected, then card.service.ts:200 does `const boardId = card.column.boardId;` and card.service.ts:210-213 writes `data: { columnId: toColumnId, position: newPosition }` — targetColumn.boardId is read but never compared. card.service.ts:204-207 also shifts every card at `position >= newPosition` in the victim's column. The broadcast at card.service.ts:234 uses the SOURCE boardId, so the victim board gets no SSE event. The correct scoping pattern already exists 280 lines away in the same file: executeBulkArchive at card.service.ts:486-491 uses `where: { id: { in: cardIds }, column: { boardId }, ... }`.
- **Impatto utente:** A user with READ-only share on board B can enumerate B's column ids from GET /api/kanban/boards/B (they are returned in the board payload), then call PUT /api/kanban/cards/<a card on their own board A>/move with toColumnId = one of B's columns. The card is inserted into B — a board they cannot write to — and every card below the insert position in that column is renumbered. B's owner sees nothing in realtime (broadcast went to A) and discovers a foreign card on next refetch. The same request also silently removes the card from A. Applies equally to a revoked collaborator who still has the old column ids.
- **Fix:** One comparison in backend/src/services/kanban/card.service.ts right after the targetColumn fetch (line 198), where both boardIds are already in hand: `if (targetColumn.boardId !== card.column.boardId) throw new BadRequestError('errors.kanban.columnNotInBoard');` — add that key to en.json and it.json. Putting it in the service (not the route) also covers bulkMoveNotify and any future caller.

#### `high` · dataloss · S — Column reorder is silently dropped by the API — Zod strips `position`, dedicated reorder endpoint is dead code
`frontend/src/features/kanban/kanbanService.ts:234` · anche: `backend/src/routes/kanban.ts`, `backend/src/services/kanban/column.service.ts`, `frontend/src/features/sync/syncService.ts`

- **Problema:** Dragging a column enqueues an UPDATE KANBAN_COLUMN carrying only `{ position }`, which syncPush sends to PUT /kanban/columns/:id. That route's updateColumnSchema accepts only title and isCompleted, so Zod (non-strict by default) strips `position`, the service builds an empty update object, Prisma performs a no-op, and the server returns 200. The order is never persisted. Meanwhile the endpoint that WOULD persist it has no caller.
- **Evidenza:** Frontend: kanbanService.ts:234 `export async function reorderColumns(_boardId: string, ...)` — the boardId parameter is prefixed `_` because it is unused; lines 240-247 enqueue `{ type: 'UPDATE', entity: 'KANBAN_COLUMN', data: { position } }`. syncService.ts:678-679 routes that to `await api.put(`/kanban/columns/${item.entityId}`, item.data)`. Backend: kanban.ts:416-419 `const updateColumnSchema = z.object({ title: ...optional(), isCompleted: ...optional() })` — no `position`. column.service.ts:37-44: `const updateData: Record<string, unknown> = {}; if (data.title !== undefined) ...; if (data.isCompleted !== undefined) ...; await prisma.kanbanColumn.update({ where: { id }, data: updateData })` → updateData is `{}`. The correct route exists at kanban.ts:428-434 (`PUT /boards/:id/columns/reorder` → column.service.ts:54-68, which DOES write position in a transaction) and `grep -rn "columns/reorder" frontend/src` returns zero hits. Backend test kanban.route.test.ts:330 covers the dead endpoint, not the path the app actually uses.
- **Impatto utente:** User drags a column to a new position. useKanbanMutations.ts:74-78 calls invalidateBoard() on success, which refetches GET /kanban/boards/:id from the server — which still has the old positions — so the column visibly snaps back. On a second device, or after any reload, the order is always the original one. The reorder feature has never worked against the server.
- **Fix:** Point the client at the endpoint that already works: in kanbanService.ts:234-250, replace the per-column syncQueue enqueue with the local Dexie position writes plus a single `api.put(`/kanban/boards/${boardId}/columns/reorder`, { columns })` (matching the server-only pattern used by getBoard/getBoardChat), and delete the now-unused `_` prefix. Belt-and-braces: also add `position: z.number().int().min(0).optional()` to updateColumnSchema (kanban.ts:416) and the matching `if (data.position !== undefined)` line in column.service.ts:39, so a stale queued item from an old client still applies instead of silently succeeding.

#### `high` · bug · S — Board chat and card comments return the OLDEST 50 rows — chat breaks permanently past 50 messages
`backend/src/services/kanban/comments-chat.service.ts:158` · anche: `frontend/src/features/kanban/hooks/useKanbanChat.ts`, `frontend/src/features/kanban/kanbanService.ts`

- **Problema:** getBoardChat and getComments order ascending and page from the start, while the frontend calls them with no pagination params and has no load-more UI. The server default (page 1, limit 50) therefore returns the 50 oldest rows forever. Nothing in the client re-sorts or appends.
- **Evidenza:** comments-chat.service.ts:156-160 `prisma.kanbanBoardChat.findMany({ where: { boardId }, orderBy: { createdAt: 'asc' }, skip: (page - 1) * limit, take: limit, ... })`; same shape for comments at comments-chat.service.ts:22-26. Route defaults: kanban.ts:69-72 `paginationSchema` = page 1 / limit 50, applied at kanban.ts:396 and kanban.ts:505. Client sends nothing: kanbanService.ts:525-528 `api.get(`/kanban/boards/${boardId}/chat`)`, kanbanService.ts:487-490 for comments. useKanbanChat.ts:8-13 is a plain useQuery with no page state and, critically, `refetchInterval: 3000`. BoardChatSidebar.tsx:100 renders `messages.map(...)` in array order with no sort. Contrast getCardActivities at card.service.ts:357 which correctly uses `orderBy: { createdAt: 'desc' }`.
- **Impatto utente:** On any board whose chat has passed 50 messages: the sidebar shows only ancient history. A user types a message, it POSTs successfully, useKanbanChat.ts:18 invalidates, the refetch returns the same oldest 50 — and their message disappears from the panel. The 3s poll and the SSE invalidate at useKanbanRealtime.ts:34 keep overwriting the view with stale rows, so incoming messages from teammates never render either. The chat looks broken/frozen while the data is actually being written fine. Card comment threads past 50 have the same failure.
- **Fix:** Flip both queries to newest-first and hand the client back chronological order — `orderBy: { createdAt: 'desc' }` in comments-chat.service.ts:158 and :24, then `.then(rows => rows.reverse())` on the findMany. Page 1 then means "the most recent 50", which is what both callers already assume, and no client change is needed. Older pages remain reachable via the existing ?page= param if a load-more is added later.

#### `medium` · permission · S — reorderColumns updates columns by bare id with no board scoping and an unbounded array
`backend/src/services/kanban/column.service.ts:61` · anche: `backend/src/routes/kanban.ts`

- **Problema:** The route authorizes board :id, but the service then updates each column by primary key only. Nothing ties the submitted column ids to the authorized board, so ids belonging to other users' boards are accepted. The Zod array also has no length cap.
- **Evidenza:** kanban.ts:428-434: `await assertBoardAccess(id, request.user.id, 'WRITE'); const { columns } = reorderColumnsSchema.parse(request.body); await kanbanService.reorderColumns(id, columns);`. kanban.ts:37-39: `const reorderColumnsSchema = z.object({ columns: z.array(z.object({ id: z.string(), position: z.number().int().min(0) })) })` — no `.max()` on the array, no uuid on the id. column.service.ts:58-65: `prisma.$transaction(items.map((item) => prisma.kanbanColumn.update({ where: { id: item.id }, data: { position: item.position } })))` — the `boardId` parameter is used only for the broadcast at line 67, never in a where clause. Same root cause as the moveCard finding; executeBulkArchive (card.service.ts:489) shows the intended `column: { boardId }` pattern.
- **Impatto utente:** Any authenticated user who owns one board can POST that board's id plus column ids harvested from a board they only have READ on, and scramble that board's column order. The victim gets a `columns:reordered` SSE for their own board only if the attacker guessed their boardId; otherwise the order silently changes under them on next load. A large `columns` array also opens one request into an unbounded Prisma transaction.
- **Fix:** Scope the write to the authorized board in column.service.ts:58-65 — swap the per-id `update` for `prisma.kanbanColumn.updateMany({ where: { id: item.id, boardId }, data: { position: item.position } })` inside the same $transaction (boardId is already a parameter), and add `.max(50)` to the array at kanban.ts:38.

#### `medium` · permission · M — assigneeId accepts any user id — notification helper bypasses the board participant check
`backend/src/routes/kanban.ts:50` · anche: `backend/src/services/kanban/card.service.ts`, `backend/src/services/kanban/notifications.ts`

- **Problema:** updateCardSchema types assigneeId as a plain nullable string with no format and no membership constraint, the service writes it through unvalidated, and the notification helper short-circuits its participant lookup whenever a specific user id is supplied.
- **Evidenza:** kanban.ts:50 `assigneeId: z.string().nullable().optional()`. card.service.ts:69 `if (data.assigneeId !== undefined) updateData.assigneeId = data.assigneeId;` — written straight to Prisma with no lookup against the board's owner/shares. card.service.ts:154-169 then calls `notifyBoardUsers(actorId, boardId, 'KANBAN_CARD_ASSIGNED', ..., data.assigneeId)`, and notifications.ts:31-34 is `if (specificUserId && specificUserId !== actorId) { await createNotification(specificUserId, type, title, message, data); return; }` — it returns BEFORE the board owner/ACCEPTED-shares query at notifications.ts:37-43 that every other path uses. The notification payload built at card.service.ts:160-167 contains boardId, boardTitle and cardTitle.
- **Impatto utente:** A board member crafts PUT /api/kanban/cards/:id with assigneeId set to any user id in the system. That stranger receives an in-app KANBAN_CARD_ASSIGNED notification disclosing the card title and board title of a board they have no access to, and the card now shows an assignee who is not a participant (breaking the assignee filter at KanbanFilterBar.tsx:54). If the id is not a real user, Prisma raises a foreign-key P2003 which is not P2025, so app.ts:109-111 returns 500 for what is a client error — and syncService only marks 400/422 and 403 as permanently failed, so a 500 keeps the queue item retrying in backoff.
- **Fix:** Validate membership once where the write happens — in card.service.ts:69, when `data.assigneeId` is non-null, check it against the board's owner + ACCEPTED shares (the same query notifications.ts:37-43 already runs) and throw `BadRequestError('errors.kanban.assigneeNotParticipant')` otherwise; add the key to en.json and it.json. Tighten kanban.ts:50 to `z.string().uuid().nullable().optional()` so malformed ids are a 400 rather than a Prisma 500.

#### `medium` · i18n · S — errors.kanban.ownerOnly missing from both locale files, and the caller swallows the 403 with no catch
`backend/src/routes/kanban.ts:584` · anche: `frontend/src/locales/en.json`, `frontend/src/locales/it.json`, `frontend/src/features/kanban/components/BulkArchiveDialog.tsx`

- **Problema:** The two bulk-archive routes throw ForbiddenError('errors.kanban.ownerOnly'), a key that exists in neither en.json nor it.json. The frontend that calls them has try/finally with no catch, so the 403 becomes an unhandled promise rejection and the user gets no feedback at all.
- **Evidenza:** kanban.ts:584 `if (!isOwner) throw new ForbiddenError('errors.kanban.ownerOnly');` and kanban.ts:595 the same. I resolved all 21 distinct `errors.kanban.*` keys used across routes/kanban.ts, services/kanban/, kanbanPermissions.ts and kanbanReminder.service.ts against both locale files with a node lookup: `ownerOnly` is the only one missing, and it is missing from BOTH (every other key including onlyOwnerCanDelete, coverTooLarge, columnHasCards, reminderNotFound resolves in en and it). Caller: BulkArchiveDialog.tsx:50-61 `try { const res = await api.post(...bulk-archive-preview...) } finally { setIsPreviewing(false) }` and BulkArchiveDialog.tsx:63-76 the same shape for the archive POST — no catch, no toast.
- **Impatto utente:** When ownership changes hands or the board query data is stale (isOwner at KanbanBoardPage.tsx:123 is derived from the cached board), a user reaches the dialog, clicks Preview or Archive, the spinner stops and absolutely nothing happens — no error, no explanation, and the console shows an unhandled rejection. Even once a catch is added, the message renders as the literal string 'errors.kanban.ownerOnly'.
- **Fix:** Add `ownerOnly` under errors.kanban in both frontend/src/locales/en.json and it.json (or just reuse the existing `onlyOwnerCanDelete` wording), and wrap both api.post calls in BulkArchiveDialog.tsx:50-76 with a catch that surfaces `t(err.response?.data?.message)` through the toast helper the rest of the feature uses.

#### `medium` · perf · S — Board chat polls every 3s on top of SSE that already pushes chat:message
`frontend/src/features/kanban/hooks/useKanbanChat.ts:12` · anche: `frontend/src/features/kanban/hooks/useKanbanRealtime.ts`

- **Problema:** The chat query sets refetchInterval: 3000 while the SSE stream already invalidates the exact same query key on every incoming message. Every open board burns 20 requests/minute/user against a rate-limit budget that has already caused one production outage.
- **Evidenza:** useKanbanChat.ts:8-13 `useQuery({ queryKey: queryKeys.kanban.boardChat(boardId!), queryFn: () => getBoardChat(boardId!), enabled: !!boardId, refetchInterval: 3000 })`. The same key is invalidated by the realtime stream at useKanbanRealtime.ts:33-34 `else if (event.type === 'chat:message') { queryClient.invalidateQueries({ queryKey: queryKeys.kanban.boardChat(boardId!) }) }`, and again by the send mutation at useKanbanChat.ts:18. The chat POST route carries a 30/min per-route limit (kanban.ts:400) but the GET falls under the global 600/min (app.ts:121-131), whose comment at app.ts:123-127 documents the previous 100/min spiral that 'wedged the whole kanban'.
- **Impatto utente:** Two people with a board open in two tabs each consume 80 req/min of the shared 600/min per-IP budget doing nothing but re-reading a chat SSE already delivered. Behind a corporate NAT or the IIS reverse proxy (where many users share a source IP) this is the same shape as the v1.10.3 incident: the global limiter starts returning 429s for ordinary sync traffic.
- **Fix:** Delete `refetchInterval: 3000` from useKanbanChat.ts:12 — the SSE handler at useKanbanRealtime.ts:33-34 and the send mutation's own invalidate already cover both directions. If a fallback is wanted for a dropped stream, make it `refetchInterval: 30000` rather than 3s.

#### `low` · perf · S — GET /boards/:id/archived has no pagination or cap while every sibling list endpoint does
`backend/src/services/kanban/card.service.ts:407` · anche: `backend/src/routes/kanban.ts`

- **Problema:** The archived-cards endpoint is the only kanban list route that neither parses paginationSchema nor passes a take, so it returns every archived card on the board with the full card projection plus per-card comment counts.
- **Evidenza:** kanban.ts:566-570 `fastify.get('/boards/:id/archived', ...)` — note the absence of the `const { page, limit } = paginationSchema.parse(request.query)` line that kanban.ts:396 (chat), :496 (activities) and :505 (comments) all have. card.service.ts:407-417 `prisma.kanbanCard.findMany({ where: { column: { boardId }, archivedAt: { not: null } }, orderBy: { archivedAt: 'desc' }, select: { ...cardWithAssigneeSelect, column: ... } })` — no skip, no take. The feature that fills this table is deliberately bulk: POST /boards/:id/bulk-archive (kanban.ts:592-599) archives an arbitrary list of cardIds in one click, and bulkArchiveExecSchema (kanban.ts:590) has no array cap either.
- **Impatto utente:** On a long-lived board an owner bulk-archives a few hundred completed cards a couple of times; opening the archived panel then pulls the whole set — title, description, assignee relation and a comment count subquery per row — in one unbounded response, stalling the panel and the DB for a payload the user only scrolls the top of.
- **Fix:** Mirror the sibling routes: parse paginationSchema at kanban.ts:568 and pass page/limit into getArchivedCards, adding `skip: (page - 1) * limit, take: limit` at card.service.ts:412. Add `.max(500)` to bulkArchiveExecSchema's cardIds array at kanban.ts:590 while you are in the file.

### tests-quality

#### `critical` · test · M — Offline board+card creation silently loses cards: syncQueue is never reconciled to server column IDs
`frontend/src/features/sync/syncService.ts:646` · anche: `frontend/src/features/kanban/kanbanService.ts`, `frontend/src/features/sync/__tests__/syncService.test.ts`

- **Problema:** syncService.test.ts's `kanban push` block (lines 856-953) only covers CREATE board, CREATE column, CREATE card, the shared-board skip and a 403. The interaction between the board-CREATE column-ID reconciliation and an already-queued card CREATE is untested — and it drops cards on the floor.
- **Evidenza:** `kanbanService.createBoard` (kanbanService.ts:90-118) mints local column UUIDs and queues one KANBAN_BOARD CREATE carrying `_localColumnIds`; no per-column CREATE items. syncPush's reconciliation (syncService.ts:646-670) rewrites `db.kanbanColumns` and `db.kanbanCards.columnId` to the server IDs — the transaction is opened on `db.kanbanColumns, db.kanbanCards` only (line 648), so `db.syncQueue` is deliberately outside it. The KANBAN_CARD CREATE branch then reads the column from the QUEUE ITEM, not Dexie: `const columnId = (item.data...)?.columnId; await api.post(\`/kanban/columns/${columnId}/cards\`, ...)` (lines 686-687) — a dead local UUID. Backend `getColumnWithAccess` throws NotFoundError → 404 → the 404 handler at line 786-790 deletes the queue item and moves on with only a `console.warn`. Queue order is `orderBy('createdAt')` (line 565), so the board CREATE always runs first, guaranteeing the stale ID.
- **Impatto utente:** User creates a board offline (plane/tunnel), adds three cards, comes back online. The board and its columns sync; the cards never reach the server. They stay in IndexedDB with syncStatus 'created' (so syncPull's dirty-filter keeps them visible), so the user sees them and believes they're saved — collaborators see nothing, and the cards vanish permanently on cache clear or on any other device.
- **Fix:** Add a syncPush test: queue [KANBAN_BOARD CREATE with `_localColumnIds:['loc-1']`, KANBAN_CARD CREATE with `data.columnId:'loc-1'`], have the board POST return `columns:[{id:'srv-1',position:0}]`, and assert the card POST hits `/kanban/columns/srv-1/cards`. Fix: include `db.syncQueue` in the reconciliation transaction (line 648) and rewrite queued items' `data.columnId` from `localId` to `serverId` in the same loop — one extra `where('entity').equals('KANBAN_CARD')` update alongside the existing card rewrite.
- **Correzione del verificatore:** Real, but the trigger is narrower and the visible symptom different from the write-up. (a) createBoard.onSuccess calls flushSync() immediately (useKanbanMutations.ts:21-23), so when online the board POST and the Dexie reconciliation normally land before the user picks a column — the loss needs the board CREATE still queued, i.e. genuinely offline or a push stuck in backoff. (b) 'the user sees them and believes they're saved' is wrong: KanbanBoardPage.tsx:61 renders exclusively from useKanbanBoard, whose queryFn is the server-only GET (kanbanService.getBoard), so the orphaned cards are invisible on the board — what stays wrong is the Dexie cardCount on the board list. (c) The proposed fix is incomplete: syncPush snapshots the queue into `allQueue` at line 565 before the loop, so rewriting only the Dexie syncQueue rows would not fix the current run — the in-memory item.data.columnId must be rewritten too.

#### `high` · test · M — moveCard same-column reorder is untested — and the position math is provably wrong
`backend/src/services/kanban/__tests__/card.service.test.ts:423` · anche: `backend/src/services/kanban/card.service.ts`, `frontend/src/features/kanban/hooks/useBoardDnD.ts`

- **Problema:** All six `moveCard` tests (card.service.test.ts:430-597) set up a cross-column move, and the only structural assertion is `expect(prismaMock.$transaction).toHaveBeenCalled()` (line 452) — the `updateMany` shift arguments are never asserted.
