The same-column branch (card.service.ts:216-224) has zero coverage, and it is broken: it produces duplicate positions on any downward reorder.
- **Evidenza:** card.service.ts:204-231 runs (1) `updateMany({columnId: toColumnId, position: {gte: newPosition}}, increment 1)`, (2) `update(card → newPosition)`, (3) same-column: `updateMany({position: {gt: card.position}, id: {not: cardId}}, decrement 1)`. Step 3's predicate uses the ORIGINAL `card.position` but is evaluated against the ALREADY-INCREMENTED rows from step 1. Trace [A=0,B=1,C=2,D=3], move A to position 2: step1 → C=3,D=4; step2 → A=2; step3 (`pos>0`, not A) → B=0, C=2, D=3. Final: B=0, A=2, C=2, D=3 — two cards at position 2, nothing at 1. Upward moves happen to work, which is why this survived. `useBoardDnD.ts:243-246` sends exactly this call (`toColumnId: currentColId` = same column, `position: newIdx`), so it is a live path, and `board.service.ts:174` orders cards by `position` only, so the tie breaks arbitrarily.
- **Impatto utente:** A user drags a card DOWN within a column. It appears correct optimistically, but on reload/SSE refresh the two colliding cards render in arbitrary order — the card lands in the wrong slot, or swaps with its neighbour. Every subsequent drag in that column compounds the corruption because the position sequence now has gaps and duplicates.
- **Fix:** Add a `moveCard` test that drives the same-column branch and asserts the three `updateMany`/`update` calls' arguments (not just `$transaction` was called), e.g. downward move from 0→2 must leave a contiguous 0..n-1 permutation. It fails today. Root-cause fix: in the same-column case, skip step 1 entirely and instead do a single directional shift — downward (`newPosition > card.position`): decrement rows in `(card.position, newPosition]`; upward: increment rows in `[newPosition, card.position)` — then set the card. That removes the increment/decrement double-pass that the stale `card.position` predicate cannot survive.
- **Correzione del verificatore:** Everything factual holds. Only the severity is overstated: nothing is deleted or unrecoverable — positions collide and the card renders in an arbitrary slot until the next drag, which is an ordering-corruption bug users hit in normal use, not data loss.

#### `medium` · test · M — Kanban board share / accept / revoke service logic has zero tests
`backend/src/services/__tests__/sharing.service.test.ts:585` · anche: `backend/src/services/sharing.service.ts`, `backend/src/routes/__tests__/sharing.route.test.ts`

- **Problema:** `grep -c kanban backend/src/services/__tests__/sharing.service.test.ts` returns 0. `shareKanbanBoard` (sharing.service.ts:557), `revokeKanbanBoardShare` (620) and the KANBAN branch of `respondToShareById` (439-465) are completely untested, while the NOTE and NOTEBOOK equivalents each have 6-9 tests (describes at lines 75, 222, 387, 511, 586, 656).
- **Evidenza:** sharing.route.test.ts:676-905 exercises the kanban share endpoints but `vi.mock`s the whole service, so it proves only that the route forwards arguments. The untested service branches carry real logic: the owner check `board.ownerId !== ownerId → ForbiddenError` (sharing.service.ts:568), the self-share guard (570), the `status !== 'PENDING' → early return` idempotency guard (443), the re-share upsert that resets an ACCEPTED share back to PENDING (571-576), and two side effects swallowed by bare `catch {}` — `createRemindersForNewBoardUser` on accept (457-462) and `deleteRemindersForUserOnBoard` on revoke (641-647).
- **Impatto utente:** This is exactly the code path the v1.10.2 shared-board bugs came out of, and it is still unguarded. A regression in the `ownerId` check at line 568 or 630 is a permission hole (any collaborator could share or revoke someone else's board) that no test would catch; a regression in the reminder cleanup at 641 leaves a revoked user receiving kanban reminders for a board they can no longer open.
- **Fix:** Mirror the existing NOTE describes for KANBAN in sharing.service.test.ts: `shareKanbanBoard` (happy path, not-owner → Forbidden, self-share → BadRequest, re-share resets to PENDING), `revokeKanbanBoardShare` (deletes + calls deleteRemindersForUserOnBoard, not-owner → Forbidden, missing share → still success), and a `respondToShareById` KANBAN describe (accept calls createRemindersForNewBoardUser; already-ACCEPTED returns early without re-updating). The prisma mock and factories already exist — it is copy-adapt, not new scaffolding.
- **Correzione del verificatore:** Accurate as a coverage gap, but the impact is hypothetical, not live: the owner checks (568, 630), the self-share guard (572) and the PENDING guard (444) are all present and correct in the current code, so there is no permission hole today — only nothing standing in the way of a future regression. A couple of cited lines are off by 1-3 (self-share is 572 not 570, upsert 574-579 not 571-576, reminders 459-463 and 642-646). This is fragility, not a bug users hit.

#### `medium` · test · M — Bulk archive and bulk-move-notify: no service tests, and the route test's service mock cannot even reach them
`backend/src/services/kanban/__tests__/card.service.test.ts:677` · anche: `backend/src/services/kanban/card.service.ts`, `backend/src/routes/__tests__/kanban.route.test.ts`, `backend/src/routes/kanban.ts`

- **Problema:** Three exported functions in card.service.ts have zero tests — `previewBulkArchive` (line 456), `executeBulkArchive` (482), `bulkMoveNotify` (504) — and they are also absent from the route test's `vi.mock('../../services/kanban/index')` factory (kanban.route.test.ts:6-39), so their three endpoints cannot be tested without editing the mock first.
- **Evidenza:** card.service.test.ts's describes stop at createCard/updateCard/moveCard/deleteCard/getCardActivities/archiveCompletedCards/getArchivedCards/unarchiveCard. The route test mock lists 33 functions; `previewBulkArchive`, `executeBulkArchive` and `bulkMoveNotify` are not among them. The uncovered logic is not trivial: `executeBulkArchive` relies on `column: { boardId }` in its `updateMany` where-clause (card.service.ts:487) as the ONLY thing preventing a caller from archiving arbitrary card IDs from another board, and the two routes' owner-only gates (`if (!isOwner) throw new ForbiddenError('errors.kanban.ownerOnly')`, kanban.ts:586 and 597) are the only owner-only checks in the whole kanban route file besides board delete — the one place that IS tested (kanban.route.test.ts:268-279).
- **Impatto utente:** A WRITE-shared collaborator posting to `/boards/:id/bulk-archive` should get 403; nothing verifies that. Worse, if the `column: { boardId }` scope in executeBulkArchive were ever dropped in a refactor, a board owner could pass card IDs belonging to someone else's board and mass-archive them — a cross-board data-destruction hole with no test standing in the way.
- **Fix:** Add a `bulk archive` describe to card.service.test.ts asserting `executeBulkArchive` scopes its updateMany to `column: { boardId }` and `archivedAt: null`, returns 0 on an empty `cardIds`, and that `previewBulkArchive` returns [] when no column is `isCompleted`. Then add the three names to the route-test mock factory and two route tests: non-owner → 403 on `/bulk-archive` and `/bulk-archive-preview`.
- **Correzione del verificatore:** Two details are off and one claim is speculative. The ForbiddenError throws are at kanban.ts:584 and 595 (586/597 are the service-call lines); the mock factory lists 36 functions, not 33, and card.service.test.ts has no getArchivedCards describe either. More importantly, the 'cross-board data-destruction hole' is hypothetical — executeBulkArchive does scope its updateMany with `column: { boardId }, archivedAt: null` (card.service.ts:486-490), and the archive is soft and reversible via unarchiveCard, so nothing is destroyed today. This is a coverage gap on a permission-bearing path, i.e. fragility.

#### `medium` · test · M — The kanban e2e suite never runs in CI — it is the only UI-level coverage and it is dead code
`.github/workflows/ci.yml:73` · anche: `frontend/vitest.config.ts`, `frontend/package.json`, `frontend/playwright.config.ts`, `frontend/e2e/kanban.spec.ts`

- **Problema:** CI's frontend job runs only `npm test` (ci.yml:73-74) = `vitest run` (package.json:14), and vitest.config.ts:8 scopes collection to `src/**/*.test.ts(x)`. There is no Playwright job and no `test:e2e` script anywhere in package.json. `frontend/e2e/kanban.spec.ts` (159 lines, 6 tests) is therefore executed by nobody on any PR.
- **Evidenza:** playwright.config.ts even branches on `process.env.CI` for `forbidOnly`, `retries: 2` and `workers: 1` (lines 6-8) — written for a CI job that does not exist. The spec is also visibly rotting already: the beforeEach carries a comment about a 'stale register-page flow replaced' and the delete test navigates by CSS-class substring (`page.locator('[class*="rounded"]')`, line 139) plus `menuBtn.click({ force: true })` (144) — assertions that will silently drift with any Tailwind restyle and that nobody will notice.
- **Impatto utente:** Every kanban UI regression that unit tests cannot see — the create-board modal, the add-card inline input, the ConfirmDialog delete flow — ships unguarded. Contributors reasonably believe the suite protects them because the file exists and is checked in.
- **Fix:** Add `"test:e2e": "playwright test"` to frontend/package.json and a third CI job that starts the backend + `npm run build && npm run preview` and runs it (Playwright's `webServer` already handles the frontend). If a full CI env is genuinely out of scope, that is a decision — but then say so in the spec header, because right now the file reads as coverage that does not exist.
- **Correzione del verificatore:** Scope is understated rather than overstated: frontend/e2e/ holds 18 specs (auth, collaboration, dexie, encryption, sharing, tasks, …), so it is the entire e2e suite that never runs on any PR, not just kanban.spec.ts. Minor line drift: the class-substring locator is at line 138 and the force click at 143 (not 139/144).

#### `medium` · test · S — duplicateCard has no test and silently diverges from the server on both position and priority
`frontend/src/features/kanban/kanbanService.ts:421` · anche: `backend/src/routes/kanban.ts`, `frontend/src/features/kanban/__tests__/kanbanService.test.ts`

- **Problema:** kanbanService.test.ts covers `splitTextForCard`, `createCard` caps and `deleteCard` — `duplicateCard` (61 lines, kanbanService.ts:421-482) is untested, and it queues a CREATE payload the backend partially discards.
- **Evidenza:** Locally it inserts the copy at `original.position + 1` and shifts every later card by +1 (lines 430, 459-463), but the queued payload is `{ id, columnId, title, description, priority }` (line 475) with NO position, and backend `createCard` assigns `(max position) + 1` (card.service.ts:23-27) — the server puts the copy at the END of the column. Separately, `createCardSchema` (kanban.ts:40-44) declares only `id`, `title`, `description`; Zod objects are non-strict by default, so `priority` is stripped without error. The local shift of sibling cards is never pushed at all.
- **Impatto utente:** User duplicates a HIGH-priority card. The copy appears directly under the original with its priority badge. After the next syncPull it jumps to the bottom of the column and its priority is blank — with no error, and no way to tell whether the priority was 'lost' or never applied. On a shared board, collaborators never see the intended position or priority at any point.
- **Fix:** Add a `duplicateCard` describe to the existing kanbanService.test.ts (the mockDb harness is already there) asserting the queued payload matches what the server will actually persist. Then make it true the lazy way: drop `priority` from the queued CREATE and follow the CREATE with an existing `updateCard(id, { priority })` enqueue — `updateCardSchema` (kanban.ts:46-53) already accepts priority — and stop pre-shifting siblings locally, letting the copy land at the end so Dexie matches the server.
- **Correzione del verificatore:** Accurate; only the visible duration of the wrong position is shorter than described — duplicateCard's mutation calls invalidateBoard(), so the board refetches from the server almost immediately and the copy jumps to the bottom right away rather than 'after the next syncPull'. The permanent part is the dropped priority. Line refs drift by ~2 (payload at 477, shift at 458-462, schema at 41-45).

#### `low` · test · M — updateDexieFromSSE untested; remote delete events bypass the dirty guard and destroy unsynced local edits
`frontend/src/features/kanban/hooks/useKanbanRealtime.ts:187` · anche: `frontend/src/features/kanban/hooks`

- **Problema:** `frontend/src/features/kanban/hooks/` contains no test files at all (verified by find). `updateDexieFromSSE` is 100 lines of Dexie mutation applied to every inbound realtime event, and its dirty-guard invariant is applied inconsistently: three cases check it, two destructive cases skip it.
- **Evidenza:** `card:updated` (line 165), `card:moved` (192), `column:updated` (215) and `columns:reordered` (234) all guard with `if (local && local.syncStatus !== 'synced') break;`. But `case 'card:deleted': await db.kanbanCards.delete(event.cardId)` (187-189) and `case 'column:deleted': ... db.kanbanCards.where('columnId').equals(...).delete()` (226-230) do the delete unconditionally, with no guard and no check of the pending DELETE/CREATE queue that syncPull's zombie-prevention (syncService.ts:355-359) is careful about.
- **Impatto utente:** Two users on a shared board. User A deletes a column while User B is offline-editing a card in it — or B has a card created offline and not yet pushed. B's client applies the SSE event on reconnect and wipes the local rows, including cards whose CREATE/UPDATE is still sitting in the syncQueue. B's unsynced work is gone with no warning, and the orphaned queue items then fail against a server that has no such column.
- **Fix:** Export `updateDexieFromSSE` and add a test file for it — it is a pure `(event, boardId) => Dexie writes` function, so it tests against the same hoisted mockDb pattern already used in kanbanService.test.ts. Cover: card:updated skips a dirty card, card:moved skips a dirty card, and (currently failing) card:deleted / column:deleted must NOT delete rows whose syncStatus !== 'synced'. Fix is the same one-line guard the other cases already use.
- **Correzione del verificatore:** The asymmetry is real but the diagnosis and the prescribed fix are wrong. A delete event is authoritative: the server row is already gone (KanbanCard.columnId has onDelete: Cascade in schema.prisma, so a deleted column takes its server cards with it), so any pending local CREATE/UPDATE for those rows would 404 against the server regardless. Applying the same dirty guard to the delete cases would leave rows that syncPull can never clean up — its prune at syncService.ts:404-411 only deletes rows with syncStatus === 'synced' — i.e. permanent zombie cards, which is worse than the behaviour complained about. The one genuinely recoverable sub-case (a card locally moved INTO a remotely-deleted column) self-heals on the next syncPull because the card still exists in its original server column. What survives is the test gap on a 100-line Dexie-mutating function, not 'unsynced work is gone'.

#### `low` · test · S — archiveCompletedCards test asserts `expect.any(Date)` for the cutoff — it cannot fail, on a path that destroys data on every board open
`backend/src/services/kanban/__tests__/card.service.test.ts:690` · anche: `backend/src/services/kanban/card.service.ts`, `backend/src/services/kanban/board.service.ts`

- **Problema:** The only assertion covering the 7-day archive window is `updatedAt: { lte: expect.any(Date) }` (line 693) plus `data: { archivedAt: expect.any(Date) }` (695). Any cutoff value passes: 7 days, 0 days, 7000 days, `new Date(0)`, `new Date(8640000000000000)`. The test verifies that a Date was constructed, not that the window is correct.
- **Evidenza:** `archiveCompletedCards` computes `new Date(Date.now() - ARCHIVE_AFTER_DAYS * 24*60*60*1000)` with `ARCHIVE_AFTER_DAYS = 7` (card.service.ts:368, 375) and is invoked unconditionally as the FIRST statement of `getBoard` (board.service.ts:144) — i.e. on every single board load, for every user, including READ-only collaborators. board.service.test.ts:14 mocks it out entirely, so card.service.test.ts is the only place it is exercised.
- **Impatto utente:** A sign flip or a unit slip in that arithmetic (`+` instead of `-`, or seconds instead of ms) makes the cutoff a future date, and the very next board open silently archives every card in every completed column of that board. The green test suite would report no problem. Users would open their board and find Done emptied.
- **Fix:** Freeze time with `vi.useFakeTimers()` / `vi.setSystemTime()` in that test and assert the exact cutoff: `updatedAt: { lte: new Date(NOW - 7*24*3600*1000) }`. One-line change to an existing test, and it turns the assertion from decorative into load-bearing.
- **Correzione del verificatore:** The weak assertion is real; the consequence is dramatised. Archiving is a soft, reversible flag (archivedAt is set, rows are excluded from getBoard by `where: { archivedAt: null }`, and unarchiveCard / getArchivedCards restore them), so even the hypothetical sign flip would not 'destroy' anything — a user would find Done emptied and could unarchive. This is a non-falsifying assertion worth one line of vi.setSystemTime, i.e. polish.

### ui-ux-components

#### `high` · dataloss · S — Escape in CardDetailModal silently discards the card title/description being typed
`frontend/src/features/kanban/components/CardDetailModal.tsx:285` · anche: `frontend/src/components/ui/Modal.tsx`

- **Problema:** Card title and description commit ONLY in onBlur handlers. Modal registers a document-level Escape listener that calls onClose(), and Modal returns null when closed, unmounting the focused input. React does not fire onBlur on unmount, so the mutation never runs and the edit is lost.
- **Evidenza:** CardDetailModal.tsx:285 onBlur={handleTitleBlur} and :308 onBlur={handleDescriptionBlur} are the only commit paths (no onChange mutation, no form, no save button; handleTitleBlur at :110, handleDescriptionBlur at :124). Modal.tsx:30-31 'const handleEscape = (e) => { if (e.key === Escape) onClose(); }' plus Modal.tsx:43 'if (!isOpen) return null;'. The X button at Modal.tsx:50 only works by accident: its mousedown blurs the input first.
- **Impatto utente:** User opens a card, rewrites a long description, presses Escape (the universal close key) and the whole edit vanishes with no warning and no toast.
- **Fix:** Wrap onClose: 'const handleClose = () => { handleTitleBlur(); handleDescriptionBlur(); onClose(); }' and pass it to <Modal onClose={handleClose}>. Reuses the two existing functions; one extra call site.
- **Correzione del verificatore:** Accurate as written; only the severity label is off. Nothing persisted is destroyed — what is lost is the unsaved edit buffer for the currently open card. That is still silent loss of user-typed text on the most common close gesture, but it is not database-level data loss.

#### `high` · i18n · S — Missing i18n key kanban.noteLink.sharingGapDescription renders the raw key to the user
`frontend/src/features/kanban/components/SharingGapModal.tsx:49` · anche: `frontend/src/locales/en.json`, `frontend/src/locales/it.json`

- **Problema:** SharingGapModal renders t('kanban.noteLink.sharingGapDescription') but that key exists in neither en.json nor it.json, so i18next falls back to printing the key string.
- **Evidenza:** SharingGapModal.tsx:49 t('kanban.noteLink.sharingGapDescription', { noteTitle: sharingCheck.noteTitle }). Programmatic lookup of every t() key used under features/kanban against both locale files: en.kanban.noteLink and it.kanban.noteLink contain pickerTitle, searchPlaceholder, untitledNote, noResults, typeToSearch, linkNote, unlink, goToBoard, boardLabel, cardLabel, sharingGapTitle, usersWithAccess, usersWithoutAccess, confirmLink -- no sharingGapDescription in either file. All other 182 kanban keys resolve in both.
- **Impatto utente:** Linking a note to a card when board members lack access shows the literal text 'kanban.noteLink.sharingGapDescription' as the modal's explanatory paragraph -- exactly where the user needs to understand what they are about to share.
- **Fix:** Add the key to both locale files under kanban.noteLink, e.g. en: 'Some board members cannot see "{{noteTitle}}". Select who should be given access.' and the Italian equivalent.

#### `high` · ux · M — Nested Modal: closing the note picker unlocks page scroll and Escape closes both modals
`frontend/src/features/kanban/components/CardDetailModal.tsx:662` · anche: `frontend/src/components/ui/Modal.tsx`, `frontend/src/features/kanban/components/NoteLinkPicker.tsx`, `frontend/src/features/kanban/components/SharingGapModal.tsx`

- **Problema:** NoteLinkPicker and SharingGapModal are rendered as children of the CardDetailModal's own Modal. Two Modal instances then run the same document-level Escape listener and the same body-overflow lock, so the inner modal's cleanup unlocks scroll while the outer is still open, and one Escape press closes both.
- **Evidenza:** CardDetailModal.tsx:662-666 renders <NoteLinkPicker> and :669-681 <SharingGapModal> inside the <Modal> opened at :265. NoteLinkPicker.tsx:44 and SharingGapModal.tsx:39 both use the same Modal. Modal.tsx:29-41: the effect adds a listener on document and sets body.style.overflow='hidden', and its cleanup runs 'document.body.style.overflow = unset' with deps [isOpen, onClose] -- so the inner modal closing unlocks scroll and the outer modal's effect never re-runs to re-lock it. Both Escape handlers fire on the same keydown. Modal does not portal (it renders inline), so useFocusTrap on the outer container (Modal.tsx:27) also matches the inner modal's focusable elements, giving two competing Tab traps.
- **Impatto utente:** Open a card, click 'Link note', press Escape to dismiss the picker -- the card modal disappears too. Or pick a note normally: the picker closes and the page behind the still-open card modal starts scrolling under the backdrop.
- **Fix:** Make Modal portal to document.body like Dialog.tsx:39 already does, and ref-count the body lock (a module-level counter incremented on open, decremented on close, only clearing overflow at zero) plus only let the top-most open modal handle Escape. Fixes every nested-modal site at once.
- **Correzione del verificatore:** One nuance on the scroll half: it self-heals. The outer Modal's onClose is the inline arrow at KanbanBoardPage.tsx:1091, so the next time KanbanBoardPage re-renders (any SSE invalidation, any query settle) the outer effect's dep identity changes and it re-locks overflow. The deterministic, always-reproducible half is Escape closing both modals at once — which, combined with finding #1, also discards the card's unsaved title/description.

#### `high` · a11y · S — Kanban cards and board cards cannot be opened with a keyboard
`frontend/src/features/kanban/components/KanbanCard.tsx:174` · anche: `frontend/src/features/kanban/components/BoardCard.tsx`

- **Problema:** The click targets that open the card detail modal and the board are plain divs with onClick, no role, no tabIndex and no key handler, so the primary action of the whole feature is mouse/touch only.
- **Evidenza:** KanbanCard.tsx:174-180 '<div className="flex-1 min-w-0" onClick={handleCardClick} onTouchStart=... >' with no tabIndex/role/onKeyDown. BoardCard.tsx:59-61 '<div onClick={() => onSelect(board.id)} className="rounded-xl ...">' likewise. Drag itself IS keyboard-capable (useBoardDnD.ts:116 registers a KeyboardSensor), which makes the gap sharper: you can move a card by keyboard but not open it.
- **Impatto utente:** A keyboard or screen-reader user can tab to the drag handle and reorder cards but can never open a card to read its description, comments or activity, and can never enter a board from the board list.
- **Fix:** On both divs add role="button" tabIndex={0} and onKeyDown={(e) => { if (e.key === 'Enter' || e.key === ' ') { e.preventDefault(); handleCardClick(); } }} -- the handlers already exist.

#### `medium` · dataloss · S — SSE card:updated wipes the description you are typing in CardDetailModal
`frontend/src/features/kanban/components/CardDetailModal.tsx:80` · anche: `frontend/src/features/kanban/hooks/useKanbanRealtime.ts`

- **Problema:** The sync-from-props effect depends on card.title and card.description, so any realtime update to that card resets local edit state. Since the typed text lives only in React state until blur, a collaborator's edit destroys it.
- **Evidenza:** CardDetailModal.tsx:80-85 'useEffect(() => { if (card) { setEditTitle(card.title); setEditDescription(card.description ?? ''); } }, [card?.id, card?.title, card?.description]);'. useKanbanRealtime.ts:161-173 handles 'card:updated' by writing the server card into Dexie; its guard 'if (local && local.syncStatus !== synced) break;' (line 165) protects the Dexie row but not the unsaved text still sitting in the React input, which is never written to Dexie until blur.
- **Impatto utente:** On a shared board, a teammate setting an assignee or due date on the same card (any field fires card:updated) makes the description textarea you are mid-sentence in snap back to the stored value.
- **Fix:** Make the effect key on identity only: change the dep array to [card?.id] so it seeds the fields when a different card is opened and never re-seeds while the same card is being edited.
- **Correzione del verificatore:** The dep array is [card?.id, card?.title, card?.description], compared with Object.is on the string values — so an assignee, due-date, priority or comment update leaves title and description identical and the effect does NOT re-run. The finding's own userImpact example (teammate sets an assignee) cannot happen. The real triggers are narrower: (a) a collaborator changes the title or description of the card you have open, or (b) your own title save (handleTitleBlur -> updateCard -> useKanbanMutations.ts:100-103 flushSync + invalidateBoard) lands while you are already typing in the description, wiping the description buffer ~300ms later. Real concurrent-edit clobber, narrow window, same one-line fix ([card?.id] only).

#### `medium` · i18n · S — t('sharing.sharedWithCount') points at the wrong namespace -- key lives under notes
`frontend/src/features/kanban/components/BoardCard.tsx:168` · anche: `frontend/src/features/notes/NoteList.tsx`, `frontend/src/components/layout/Sidebar.tsx`

- **Problema:** The share-count button's tooltip uses the namespace 'sharing', but sharedWithCount is defined under 'notes' in both locale files. i18next returns the raw key.
- **Evidenza:** BoardCard.tsx:168 title={t('sharing.sharedWithCount', { count: board.shareCount })}. en.json:565 defines "sharedWithCount": "Shared with {{count}} users" inside the block opened at en.json:514 ("notes"), and it.json:235 inside the block opened at it.json:209 ("notes"). The "sharing" block starts at en.json:409 / it.json:507 and its key list has no sharedWithCount. Same wrong key at NoteList.tsx:110 and Sidebar.tsx:620.
- **Impatto utente:** Hovering the participants badge on a shared board card shows the tooltip text 'sharing.sharedWithCount' instead of 'Shared with 3 users'.
- **Fix:** Change all three call sites to t('notes.sharedWithCount', ...). One-line sed, no locale edits needed.
- **Correzione del verificatore:** Correct, but the blast radius is one HTML title tooltip on the participants badge — the badge itself, its count and its click-through still work. Not a broken screen.

#### `medium` · mobile · S — BoardCard action menu is hover-only: share and delete are unreachable on touch devices
`frontend/src/features/kanban/components/BoardCard.tsx:81`

- **Problema:** The three-dot menu trigger is opacity-0 until group-hover. On a touch device there is no hover, and the first tap lands on the card's own onClick which navigates into the board, so the menu can never be opened.
- **Evidenza:** BoardCard.tsx:81 'p-1 rounded-md hover:bg-neutral-100 dark:hover:bg-neutral-700 opacity-0 group-hover:opacity-100 transition-opacity' on the only element (lines 78-88) that opens the Share / Delete menu (lines 90-107). The wrapping card at BoardCard.tsx:59-61 has onClick={() => onSelect(board.id)}. frontend/CLAUDE.md forbids hover-only interactions. KanbanCard.tsx:209 already solves exactly this with 'isMobile ? opacity-100 : opacity-0 group-hover:opacity-100'.
- **Impatto utente:** On the installed PWA on a phone or tablet, a user cannot share or delete any kanban board from the board list -- the only entry point is invisible and the tap navigates away instead.
- **Fix:** Copy the KanbanCard pattern: import useIsMobile and swap the class to clsx(isMobile ? 'opacity-100' : 'opacity-0 group-hover:opacity-100').
- **Correzione del verificatore:** Two overstatements. (1) 'can never be opened' is false: opacity-0 does not remove pointer events, so a tap in the card's top-right corner still hits the button and handleMenuToggle stopPropagation()s before the card's onClick — the affordance is invisible and undiscoverable, not inert. (2) 'a user cannot share or delete any kanban board' is false: both actions are explicitly mobile-enabled one level in, in the board page's three-dot menu — Share at KanbanBoardPage.tsx:676-687 (rendered under an isMobile branch) and Delete board at :717-726. So this is a discoverability defect on the board list, not a dead end.

#### `medium` · ux · S — Typing one character in the filter search silently turns the whole board read-only
`frontend/src/features/kanban/KanbanBoardPage.tsx:878` · anche: `frontend/src/locales/en.json`, `frontend/src/locales/it.json`

- **Problema:** readOnly is computed as 'readOnly || filtersActive', so any active filter strips every editing affordance from every column, with zero explanation anywhere in the UI.
- **Evidenza:** KanbanBoardPage.tsx:121 'const filtersActive = isFiltersActive(filters);' and :878 / :920 'readOnly={readOnly || filtersActive}'. grep for filtersActive across the file returns only lines 121, 173, 178, 878, 920 -- no banner, toast or tooltip. In KanbanColumn.tsx readOnly gates the drag handle (:134), the column menu (:182), and the whole Add-card section (:283); in KanbanCard.tsx it disables useSortable (:54), the drag handle (:151) and the move menu via canMove (:64).
- **Impatto utente:** User types 'bug' in the board search to find a card, then cannot add a card, drag anything, rename a column or open a card's move menu. The controls simply disappear with no message; the user assumes the board broke or their permission changed.
- **Fix:** Render one line under KanbanFilterBar when filtersActive: a neutral notice with t('kanban.filters.readOnlyWhileFiltered') and a Clear-filters link (defaultKanbanFilters is already imported). Add the key to en.json and it.json.
- **Correzione del verificatore:** The behaviour itself is defensible, not a bug: card positions in a filtered view are meaningless, so freezing DnD is the right call, and the user is not stranded — the filter state is visibly highlighted and a 'Clear all' control exists (KanbanFilterBar.tsx:577-586 desktop, :341-348 mobile sheet). What is actually missing is one line of explanation, which makes this degraded UX rather than a bug users hit as breakage.

#### `medium` · dataloss · S — New-card input discards the typed title on blur instead of saving it
`frontend/src/features/kanban/components/KanbanColumn.tsx:296`

- **Problema:** The inline add-card input commits only on Enter, but its onBlur is wired to handleCancelAdd, which clears the text and closes the input. There is no visible confirm button.
- **Evidenza:** KanbanColumn.tsx:296 'onBlur={handleCancelAdd}'; handleCancelAdd at :118-121 does setNewCardTitle('') and setIsAddingCard(false). The only commit path is :293 'if (e.key === Enter) handleAddCard()'. The rendered branch (:285-300) contains just the input -- no Add/Save button.
- **Impatto utente:** User types a card title, then clicks the board or another column expecting it to save (the Trello behaviour everyone assumes) and the text is thrown away silently.
- **Fix:** Point onBlur at handleAddCard instead; it already no-ops on empty input (line 112 'if (!trimmed) return;') and closes the editor on success, and Escape at line 294 stays the explicit cancel.
- **Correzione del verificatore:** The strongest evidence is one the finding missed: the sibling inline editor 140 lines above does the opposite — the column-title input at KanbanColumn.tsx:153 uses onBlur={handleSaveTitle} and commits. So the defect is an internal inconsistency in the same component, not just a deviation from Trello. Nothing persisted is lost, only the just-typed title, and the user sees immediately that no card appeared.

#### `medium` · ux · S — Comment delete fires immediately with no confirmation and no accessible name
`frontend/src/features/kanban/components/CardDetailModal.tsx:549`

- **Problema:** The trash icon next to your own comment mutates on the first click. Every other destructive action in the feature goes through ConfirmDialog; this one does not, and the button has neither aria-label nor title.
- **Evidenza:** CardDetailModal.tsx:549-554 '<button onClick={() => removeComment.mutate(comment.id)} className="ml-auto text-neutral-400 hover:text-red-500 ..."><Trash2 size={13} /></button>' -- no confirm, no label. Compare :651-656 + :684-697 where deleting a card correctly routes through showDeleteCardConfirm + ConfirmDialog, and BoardCard.tsx:52-56 + :180-188 for boards.
- **Impatto utente:** A mis-tap on a small unlabelled icon permanently deletes a comment on a shared board with no undo; a screen reader announces only 'button'.
- **Fix:** Reuse the pattern already in the file: a confirmingCommentId state, and one ConfirmDialog next to the existing one at line 684. Add aria-label={t('common.delete')} on the button.
- **Correzione del verificatore:** Half of this is double-counted with finding #12 (which lists CardDetailModal.tsx:549-554 among its unlabelled icon buttons) — the novel part is the missing confirmation step. Note the project rule (memory/CLAUDE.md) bans window.confirm and mandates ConfirmDialog, but does not mandate confirming every destructive action; the argument here is consistency with the two sibling deletes, which holds.

#### `medium` · mobile · S — Column rename is double-click only -- impossible on touch, unreachable by keyboard
`frontend/src/features/kanban/components/KanbanColumn.tsx:165`

- **Problema:** onDoubleClick on the column <h3> is the sole entry point to rename a column. The column overflow menu has no Rename item, and the heading is not focusable.
- **Evidenza:** KanbanColumn.tsx:164-172 '<h3 onDoubleClick={() => !readOnly && setIsEditingTitle(true)} ...>'. The overflow menu (lines 190-244) offers only mark/remove-completed, bulk archive and delete. grep for onRenameColumn across the feature returns only KanbanColumn.tsx:16/33/104 and KanbanBoardPage.tsx:874/916/987 -- no other caller. frontend/CLAUDE.md forbids hover/desktop-only interactions and requires 44px touch targets.
- **Impatto utente:** On a phone, double-tapping the column heading zooms the page instead of editing; there is no other way to rename a column, so the feature is desktop-mouse-exclusive. Keyboard users have no path either.
- **Fix:** Add a Rename item to the existing overflow menu that calls setIsEditingTitle(true) -- roughly six lines matching the sibling menu buttons, and it makes the affordance discoverable on desktop too.
- **Correzione del verificatore:** The 'double-tapping zooms the page instead' claim is wrong: frontend/index.html:7 sets maximum-scale=1.0, user-scalable=no, so double-tap-to-zoom is disabled and a double tap generally does dispatch dblclick on touch — rename may well work on a phone, just undiscoverably and with no 44px target (frontend/CLAUDE.md:12). The solid parts are: no menu item, no keyboard path, no visible affordance.

#### `medium` · a11y · S — Eleven icon-only buttons in the Kanban UI have no accessible name
`frontend/src/features/kanban/components/KanbanColumn.tsx:184` · anche: `frontend/src/features/kanban/KanbanPage.tsx`, `frontend/src/features/kanban/KanbanBoardPage.tsx`, `frontend/src/features/kanban/components/BoardCard.tsx`, `frontend/src/features/kanban/components/BoardChatSidebar.tsx`, `frontend/src/features/kanban/components/KanbanFilterBar.tsx`, `frontend/src/features/kanban/components/NoteLinkPicker.tsx`, `frontend/src/features/kanban/components/CardDetailModal.tsx`

- **Problema:** The feature has only three aria-labels in total. Most icon-only controls carry neither aria-label nor title, so assistive tech announces a bare 'button'. frontend/CLAUDE.md makes aria-label mandatory on icon-only buttons.
- **Evidenza:** grep -rn aria-label over features/kanban returns exactly 3 hits (BoardChatSidebar.tsx:193, BulkMoveMenu.tsx:97, KanbanCard.tsx:211). Unlabelled icon-only buttons verified by reading: KanbanColumn.tsx:184-189 (column overflow menu) and :135-141 (GripVertical drag handle); KanbanPage.tsx:76-81 (mobile hamburger) and :88-94 (New board -- its label is 'hidden sm:inline', so under 640px it is a bare Plus); BoardCard.tsx:78-88 (board overflow menu); BoardChatSidebar.tsx:173-179 (Send) and :214-219 (desktop close X); KanbanFilterBar.tsx:180-184 and :369-376 (clear search X) and :188-203 (mobile Filter button); NoteLinkPicker.tsx:60-67 (clear search X); CardDetailModal.tsx:549-554 (delete comment); KanbanBoardPage.tsx:613 (board overflow menu).
- **Impatto utente:** A screen-reader user hears an undifferentiated list of 'button, button, button' across the board header, every column header and the chat composer, and cannot tell which one deletes a column versus sends a message.
- **Fix:** Add aria-label={t(...)} to each, reusing keys that already exist (common.delete, common.close, kanban.column.deleteColumn, kanban.filters.title, kanban.chat.send if present). Mechanical, one line each.
- **Correzione del verificatore:** Two small refinements. The count is 13 sites, not eleven. And the two drag handles are not literally nameless: KanbanColumn.tsx:135-141 and KanbanCard.tsx:152-157 spread dnd-kit {...attributes}, which add role="button", tabIndex=0, aria-roledescription="sortable" and aria-describedby, so AT announces them as sortable buttons with instructions — they need a name, but they are not silent. The other eleven are.

#### `low` · i18n · S — ArchivedCardsModal prints raw column titles (TODO, IN_PROGRESS) instead of translating them
`frontend/src/features/kanban/components/ArchivedCardsModal.tsx:55`

- **Problema:** Every other surface maps default column titles through DEFAULT_COLUMN_KEYS before rendering; the archive list renders card.columnTitle verbatim.
- **Evidenza:** ArchivedCardsModal.tsx:55 '<span ...>{card.columnTitle}</span>' -- the file never imports DEFAULT_COLUMN_KEYS. Compare KanbanColumn.tsx:75-76, KanbanCard.tsx:88-91 and CardDetailModal.tsx:214-217, which all do the lookup. The raw values really occur: backend/src/services/kanban/board.service.ts:118 'const cols = columnTitles || { todo: TODO, inProgress: IN_PROGRESS, done: DONE }' and :325-326 use the same fallbacks for boards created from a task list, and routes/kanban.ts:101 returns 'columnTitle: r.card.column.title'.
- **Impatto utente:** An Italian user whose board columns render as 'Da fare / In corso / Fatto' opens the archive and sees the same cards labelled 'TODO' and 'IN_PROGRESS'.
- **Fix:** Import DEFAULT_COLUMN_KEYS and render '{DEFAULT_COLUMN_KEYS[card.columnTitle] ? t(DEFAULT_COLUMN_KEYS[card.columnTitle]) : card.columnTitle}' -- the same two-line helper the three other components already use.
- **Correzione del verificatore:** The user story is self-contradictory and inflates the impact. New boards never store the raw literals: CreateBoardModal.tsx:35-39 always sends columnTitles: { t('kanban.defaultColumns.todo'), ... } and ConvertTaskListToKanbanModal.tsx:146 does the same, so a board whose columns read 'Da fare' stores the string 'Da fare' and the archive shows 'Da fare' correctly. The mismatch only appears on boards created without columnTitles (legacy rows or direct API calls) — exactly the population DEFAULT_COLUMN_KEYS exists for. A real consistency gap on a shrinking set of boards.

#### `low` · ux · S — Board filters reset every time you leave and re-enter a board
`frontend/src/features/kanban/KanbanBoardPage.tsx:118`

- **Problema:** Filter state is plain component state seeded from defaultKanbanFilters, so it is discarded on every unmount -- including the navigate() back to the board list and back in.
- **Evidenza:** KanbanBoardPage.tsx:118 'const [filters, setFilters] = useState<KanbanFilters>(defaultKanbanFilters);'. KanbanPage.tsx:35-37 unmounts/remounts KanbanBoardPage purely on the boardId search param, and KanbanPage.tsx:62 navigate('/kanban?boardId=...') is the only way in. No localStorage, no uiStore persistence, no URL params for filters anywhere in the feature.
- **Impatto utente:** A user filters a 200-card board down to 'assigned to me, overdue', clicks into a linked note, comes back, and has to rebuild the whole filter set. Every time.
- **Fix:** Key the state off the board id in localStorage, or lazily initialise from and write to the URL search params (useSearchParams is already imported in KanbanPage). Persisting search+assignee+dueDate is enough.
- **Correzione del verificatore:** Factually right, but this is an enhancement rather than a defect: transient view state resetting on unmount is the React default and no invariant, error path or data is at stake. Worth noting it interacts badly with finding #7 — since any active filter forces the board read-only (KanbanBoardPage.tsx:878/920), persisting filters across visits would silently return the user to a board with every editing affordance missing, so the fix has to ship with the missing read-only notice or it makes things worse.

#### `low` · i18n · S — Read-only due date renders as a raw ISO string, not a localised date
`frontend/src/features/kanban/components/CardDetailModal.tsx:372`

- **Problema:** formatDueDate returns the ISO yyyy-MM-dd slice, which is correct as an <input type="date"> value but is shown directly as display text in the read-only branch.
- **Evidenza:** CardDetailModal.tsx:39-41 'function formatDueDate(dueDate) { return new Date(dueDate).toISOString().split(T)[0]; }' used both at :389 (input value, correct) and :372 '{card.dueDate ? formatDueDate(card.dueDate) : -}' as visible text. The file already imports date-fns format and resolves dateLocale at :53, and uses it correctly at :235 for activity dates.
- **Impatto utente:** A read-only collaborator on an Italian board sees '2026-09-14' where the rest of the app shows '14/09/2026'.
- **Fix:** In the read-only branch use format(new Date(card.dueDate), 'P', { locale: dateLocale }) -- both format and dateLocale are already in scope.

#### `low` · ux · S — Unarchive has no pending state and swallows failures
`frontend/src/features/kanban/components/ArchivedCardsModal.tsx:28`

- **Problema:** handleUnarchive is a bare async function with no try/catch and no disabled/loading state on the button, unlike the rest of the feature which goes through react-query mutations.
- **Evidenza:** ArchivedCardsModal.tsx:28-34 'async function handleUnarchive(cardId) { await kanbanService.unarchiveCard(cardId); queryClient.invalidateQueries(...); ... onUnarchive(); }' -- no error handling. The button at :64-71 has no disabled prop and no isPending, so it stays fully enabled during the round trip.
- **Impatto utente:** Offline or on a 403, the row just stays put with no toast and an unhandled promise rejection in the console; the user clicks again and fires duplicate requests.
- **Fix:** Convert to a useMutation like the neighbouring hooks (useKanbanComments does this) so isPending drives disabled and onError can toast, or minimally wrap in try/catch with toast.error and a local pending id.

#### `low` · darkmode · S — Delete-column menu item has no dark-mode text colour
`frontend/src/features/kanban/components/KanbanColumn.tsx:230`

- **Problema:** The destructive menu item uses text-red-600 with a dark hover background but no dark: text variant, so in dark mode red-600 sits on neutral-800.
- **Evidenza:** KanbanColumn.tsx:230 "'text-red-600 hover:bg-red-50 dark:hover:bg-red-900/20'" inside a menu panel painted 'bg-white dark:bg-neutral-800' (line 193). The author clearly intended dark support (the hover background has a dark: variant) and only missed the text token. Every sibling destructive control does it correctly: BoardCard.tsx:101 'text-red-600 dark:text-red-400', CardDetailModal.tsx:653 'text-red-500 ... dark:text-red-400', KanbanFilterBar.tsx:344 and :580 the same.
- **Impatto utente:** In dark mode the 'Delete column' item renders #dc2626 on #262626 (~3.4:1), below the 4.5:1 minimum, and looks muddy next to the correctly-tinted archive item right above it.
- **Fix:** Append dark:text-red-400 to that class string.
- **Correzione del verificatore:** Contrast recomputed: #dc2626 on #262626 is about 3.1:1, not 3.4:1 — slightly worse than stated, still under the 4.5:1 minimum. Also note the item is disabled (and grey) whenever the column has cards (:225-231), so the red only shows on empty columns.

#### `low` · ux · S — Card titles are clamped to two lines with no tooltip or full-text access
`frontend/src/features/kanban/components/KanbanCard.tsx:183`

- **Problema:** Card titles accept up to 500 characters but the board renders them line-clamp-2 with no title attribute, so long titles are truncated with no way to read them short of opening the modal.
- **Evidenza:** KanbanCard.tsx:183-185 '<p className="text-sm font-medium text-neutral-900 dark:text-white line-clamp-2 flex-1 min-w-0">{card.title || t(kanban.card.untitled)}</p>' -- no title attr. The 500-char cap is enforced at KanbanColumn.tsx:289 and CardDetailModal.tsx:282. The same file already uses the tooltip pattern for shorter values: :226 title={card.assignee.name || card.assignee.email} and :273 title={card.note?.title}.
- **Impatto utente:** Two cards whose titles differ only after the clamp point look identical on the board; the user must open each one to tell them apart.
- **Fix:** Add title={card.title} to the <p>, matching the assignee and linked-note tooltips two dozen lines below.
